Doctrine: "Zero-output success is ambiguous failure."
- [x] **Q3-IMAGES-OF-TEXT-SVG-DATA-URI-SRC-CORPUS** `wcag22:1.4.5` candidate at `js/tests/integration/index.html:31` claims "alt text repeated in surrounding text" when the `<img alt="First slide">` is adjacent to a `<text>First slide</text>` node inside an SVG `data:` URI sitting in the same element's own `src=` attribute. Surrounding-text corpus should exclude the flagged element's own `src` / `data-*` attribute values (they are not visible text on the rendered page). Extend the corpus-building helper to skip attribute values that contain `data:image/svg+xml`; alternatively, name the repetition's match location in the reason text so the agent can tell the text was inside the same element's `src`. Distinct from V1-FP-IMAGES-OF-TEXT-SVG-DATA-URI (that item enriches the reason when the `<img>` itself has a data-SVG src; this item is about the SVG text payload polluting the surrounding-text corpus for a different element or the same element).
- [x] **Q3-TEXT-SUCCESS-CANDIDATE-IGNORES-TEXTCONTENT** `wcag22:1.4.1` candidate says `text-success` "with no visible text" on `<strong class="... text-success-emphasis">Design</strong>` — the element's `textContent` is "Design," which is visible. Finder must pre-check `element.textContent.trim().length > 0` before emitting the "no visible text" variant; when text is present, emit the simpler variant that frames the question as "color-only indicator check — verify the state is not conveyed by `text-success` alone." Pairs with (but does not block) Q3-RULE-COLOR-SOLE-INDICATOR. WCAG 1.4.1. (committed def98eaf)

### Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-21, 10x)

Source: 23-scan deeper pass against the bootstrap repo as part of the four-repo 10x field test. Load-bearing discovery: the MCP subprocess serving the session was spawned before a mid-session `dist/cli.js` rebuild, and Q3-MCP-RESTART-HINT silently failed to fire on any of 23 responses across 90+ minutes. Several items below are symptoms whose underlying rule/shape fix is already on disk — but the detection failure itself is a bug, and it cascades into the rule-registry drift tracked under Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT.

- [x] **Q3-MCP-RESTART-HINT-ERROR-ENVELOPE** `annotateStaleSubprocess` short-circuits on `result.isError === true`, so stale-subprocess + `file-unsupported` on `.md` is indistinguishable from "extension truly unsupported." Highest-risk path for silent miss: an agent that hits an error envelope on a stale subprocess never learns the tool needs a restart. Fix: merge `stale_mcp_subprocess` into `warnings[]` regardless of `isError` — the warning is about the subprocess, not the individual call's success. Three tests: `isError: true` + warnings merge, non-string filter on error envelope, error-envelope preserves `structuredContent` when present.
- [x] **Q3-MCP-RESTART-HINT-SUBPROCESS-RACE** Q3-MCP-RESTART-HINT was closed in turn 32 but failed to fire across a full 90-min session even though dist mtime advanced mid-session. Root-cause three candidates: (a) `recordSubprocessStart` records baseline at module-init and never re-stats; (b) `import.meta.url` resolution returns a stale path when the subprocess is spawned before the rebuild and the path becomes a different inode; (c) the one-shot recording is cached and never retried. Reproduce with a deterministic test (spawn subprocess → rewrite dist → call tool → assert warning fires), not just added coverage. The fix self-heals ≥40% of the enumerated BUGs in the 10x pass triage.
- [x] **Q3-META-BUILD-PROVENANCE** Add `meta.ra11yVersion: string`, `meta.commitHash?: string`, and `meta.bundleMtime: string` (ISO) to every MCP response so an agent can detect drift between expected and actual tool versions without relying on `stale_mcp_subprocess` heuristic alone. Conditional-spread `commitHash` per the AI-first consumer model (non-git deployments ship without it). Pairs with Q3-MCP-RESTART-HINT-SUBPROCESS-RACE — provenance is the positive signal; stale-warning is the negative signal; both needed. Additive meta field; no new tool parameters.
- [x] **Q3-RULES-BY-EXTENSION-UNDERCOUNT** `rulesByExtension` now routes through `extensionMatches` (the same helper rule-runner.ts `applies()` uses for per-file eligibility), so alias-heavy scans (`.scss`/`.mdx`/`.astro`/`.md`/`.markdown`/`.js`/`.ts`) list every rule that actually evaluated files with that extension — matching `perRuleCoverage`. Picked (a): literal-equality inside `rulesByExtension` was a bug against the field's own TSDoc claim of "mirrors the gate in rule-runner.ts `applies()`." Added unit coverage: direct `rulesByExtension` alias expansion across every EXTENSION_ALIASES row + a live `scan_project` invariant asserting every rule reporting `filesEvaluated > 0` appears under `rulesByExtension[".scss"]`. Doctrine: cross-surface consistency — two fields naming "rules run on this extension" now agree by construction.

### Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-22, fresh 8-scan pass)

Source: 2-angle (full audit + deep-read triage) fresh pass against /tmp/bootstrap on 2026-04-22. Bootstrap-specific residue after the cross-repo 8-scan items above.

- [x] **Q3-RULE-TOOLTIP-NOT-DISCLOSURE** `src/rules/aria/expanded-on-disclosure.ts` still over-applies to `data-bs-toggle="tooltip"`. Evidence: `js/tests/integration/index.html:18` — a button carrying `data-bs-toggle="tooltip"` + native `title="Tooltip on top"` is flagged as a disclosure trigger. A tooltip is descriptive content (per ARIA APG `role="tooltip"` + `aria-describedby`), not an ARIA disclosure pattern (which uses `aria-expanded`). The rule's own reason text names the misclassification verbatim (`data-bs-toggle="tooltip" is a disclosure-style toggle value`). Roughly 37 of 169 emissions of this rule on bootstrap are polluted by this false positive. Fix parallel to Q3-RULE-POPOVER-NOT-DISCLOSURE (closed): remove `"tooltip"` from the disclosure-toggle allow-list; keep `"dropdown"`, `"collapse"`, and the JS classList-toggle predicates. Add good-fixture `tests/fixtures/good/expanded-on-disclosure/tooltip-not-disclosure.html` (mirrors the popover companion). Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2.
- [x] **Q3-COLOR-MEANING-READS-TEXT-CONTENT** `color/meaning-by-color-only` reason-text generator does NOT inspect the element's own `textContent` before emitting "text content `'Danger'` carries no status word." Evidence: `js/tests/visual/alert.html:24` has `<button class="btn btn-danger">Danger</button>` — the text literally IS the status word, so the reason text is factually wrong. Worse: the fix ranker proposes "prefix with 'Danger:'" as alternative #1, which would produce `"Danger: Danger"` on this button. Two paired fixes: (a) pre-check the element's trimmed `textContent` against a status-word dictionary (`danger|error|fail|warning|success|info|caution|alert` + common localized forms) and when the text already carries a status word, change the reason to `"text already carries status word — verify color is not the *sole* meaning signal for users who cannot distinguish the color"` (reason-text enrichment) and demote the "prefix with status word" alternative in the fix ranker; (b) still emit the candidate — don't suppress — because `text-danger` on a button with text "Danger" is less confident than a `text-danger` on a numeric balance, but the agent decides after reading. Pairs with the closed Q3-RULE-COLOR-SOLE-INDICATOR. Satisfies wcag22:1.4.1.
- [x] **Q3-MDX-EXAMPLE-TEMPLATE-LITERAL-SUBSTRATE** Bootstrap docs ship `.mdx` files using `<Example code={` ... `}/>` — the HTML body rendered on the docs site lives inside a JSX template-literal attribute value. Rules that parse the AST via the MDX pipeline get `template_files_parsed_as_literal` warning; ~110 `.mdx` files' worth of HTML substrate is skipped from rule coverage. Concrete miss: `forms/labels-required` does not fire on `site/src/content/docs/forms/overview.mdx:36`'s `<input type="email" class="form-control" id="exampleInputEmail1" aria-describedby="emailHelp">` inside a template literal, despite `scan_file` reporting `filesEligible: 1, findingsEmitted: 0`. This is the rendered docs-site markup — a canonical, load-bearing substrate. Add a Starlight/docs-component-aware extractor: when the parent MDX AST node is a JSX element matching a configurable name list (default `["Example", "Demo", "Playground"]`) with a `code` prop that is a template literal, parse the template-literal body as HTML and feed findings back into the rule pipeline keyed to the original line range. Pairs with the `Restore <Example code={`…`}> parsing via a Starlight-specific extractor` deferral note in Q3's "Considered and rejected" — deferred, not rejected. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.1 + 3.3.2 + every HTML-parser rule that would now run on ~110 additional file equivalents.
- [x] **Q3-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SINGLE-LONG-LINE-SECOND-PROBE** `scannedBuildArtifacts` classifier at `src/mcp/build-artifacts.ts:176` gates `minified` on `MINIFIED_LINE_THRESHOLD = 500` — any file with one line > 500 chars is labeled minified. 54 authored files across bootstrap mis-labeled (`site/src/assets/examples/blog-rtl/index.astro` 255 lines, `site/src/content/docs/components/accordion.mdx` 240 lines, `scss/_functions.scss` 302 lines — none are minified). The single-long-line heuristic fires on (a) Astro `<Example code={`…`}/>` template literals that embed multi-line HTML as one JS string, (b) SCSS function bodies with long type signatures, (c) MDX component prop bundles. Fix: require a second predicate alongside the single-long-line probe — EITHER ≥25% of lines exceed the char threshold OR the file's median line length > threshold OR `.min.` infix OR build-path ancestry (`dist/` / `build/` / `_site/` / `public/`) OR a sibling `.map` file. Any ONE of the second-tier predicates AND the single-long-line probe is sufficient. Q-SHARED-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SASS-PARTIAL (closed) added the `_*.scss` carve-out but didn't touch this root predicate. Reproduces on website-templates at much larger scale (101 authored HTMLs mis-labeled, single Google-Maps embed per file is the signature). Pair test: `tests/fixtures/real-world/bootstrap-astro-template-single-long-line/` + `tests/fixtures/real-world/website-templates-gmaps-embed-single-long-line/`. Doctrine: the `scannedBuildArtifacts` label must be provable from the file shape (ai-first-consumer.md §labeled-buckets).
- [x] **Q3-HEADING-HIERARCHY-MISSING-H1-VARIANT** `semantics/heading-hierarchy` catches level-skips (`<h1>` then `<h3>`) but does NOT catch full-page shapes with zero `<h1>` at all. Evidence: bootstrap `js/tests/visual/button.html`, `alert.html`, `breadcrumb.html` (plus several 50projects50days demos) have full-page DOCTYPE+html+body shape with no `<h1>` — rule fires 46 findings on bootstrap but none flag "missing h1." A full page with zero h1 is a 1.3.1 + 2.4.6 violation distinct from level-skip. Fix: add a `missing-h1-on-full-page` variant to the rule; the eligibility predicate is `looksLikeFullPage(node) && count(h1) === 0`, emitting at the `<body>` line. Pairs with Q5-LANDMARK-FULLPAGE-HEURISTIC-TIGHTEN (open) — both rules share the `looksLikeFullPage` predicate, so the fix lands once the fragment heuristic stabilizes. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.1 + 2.4.6.
- [x] **Q3-SENSORY-CHARACTERISTIC-LOCATIVE-WORD-SCOPE** `wcag22:1.3.3` finder matches the string `"fields below"` as a sensory characteristic on `js/tests/visual/alert.html:15` ("Please correct errors in the fields below"). "fields below" is a grammatical locative referring to form fields by document order — the user who can't see the visual page still parses the sentence as "fields that come next in the form." SC 1.3.3 targets *sensory* cues (color, shape, size, visual location, sound) that wouldn't be conveyed by assistive tech — "fields below" is conveyed correctly. Fix: tighten the finder's corpus: a location word (`above | below | next to | adjacent to | to the right of | to the left of | following | preceding`) alone is insufficient evidence for 1.3.3. Require co-occurrence of (a) a visual signifier token (color name, shape name, size adjective) OR (b) a pointing verb ("see", "click", "tap", "select", "press") within ±10 tokens of the locative. Bare locatives describing document-order relations should not fire. Reason-text enrichment is insufficient here — the candidate itself is noise in its current shape. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.3.
- [x] **Q3-REVIEW-CROSS-TREE-3.2.3-CONFIDENCE-CAP** `wcag22:3.2.3` consistent-navigation candidate pairs files from unrelated trees (bootstrap `js/tests/visual/dropdown.html` vs `site/src/assets/examples/navbars/index.astro`) with `confidence: "high"` — while the reason text itself acknowledges "Heuristic match across the whole scanned tree." The confidence label contradicts the acknowledged heuristic weakness. No `processes: []` declaration binds these files together; the pairing is a tree-wide fuzzy match. Fix: when the 3.2.3 finder's pairing crosses directory-tree boundaries AND the repo has no `processes: []` declaration in ra11y config, cap emission confidence at `"medium"` and enrich reason with "note: these files are heuristically paired by token similarity; declare `processes` in ra11y.config.ts to bind the correct page sets." Reason-text enrichment ALONGSIDE confidence cap — both are needed. Pairs with the general "confidence claim must match evidence strength" honesty rule surfaced in Q5-COVERAGE-CONFIDENCE-HONESTY-ARCHITECTURAL-BLINDSPOT (open). Satisfies wcag22:3.2.3.

### Cross-repo shared (2026-04-21)

- [x] **Q-SHARED-PROPOSE-CONFIG-RELATIVE-PATHS** `propose_config` now pipes build-artifact paths through `normalizeExcludes(paths, root)` before emitting. Every emitted path is POSIX-style root-relative (`dist/compiled.css`, never `/tmp/bootstrap/scss/_variables.scss`), paths outside the scan root are dropped, and groups with 3+ entries sharing a top-level directory collapse to a single `<dir>/**` glob (motivating case: website-templates 301 entries under one `dist/` tree). Two-entry groups stay itemized so a small `assets/` pair reads clearer than a wildcard. Collapse threshold pulled out as `EXCLUDE_GLOB_COLLAPSE_THRESHOLD = 3` with rationale. Three new tests (relative-only invariant, 3-file collapse, 2-file preserve) locked in alongside the existing exclude-branch test. Bootstrap inherits the fix transparently via its propose_config leg. Consolidates Bootstrap-8 + Jekyll-2 + Q6-9.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SASS-PARTIAL** `scannedBuildArtifacts` classifier fires on Sass partials (`_variables.scss`, `_style.scss`) because the filename starts with `_`. That prefix is the Sass partial convention (authored source) — not a generated-artifact signal. Key the classifier off deterministic markers instead: `.min.` infix, `dist/` / `build/` / `_site/` / `public/` path ancestry, hashed-filename patterns (`.[a-f0-9]{8,}.`), or a sibling sourcemap. Consolidates Bootstrap-9 + Jekyll-3. Doctrine: the `scannedBuildArtifacts` label itself is honest (findings still surface per Q4-SCSS-DISCOVERY-WIRE), but mis-labeling authored Sass as artifact misleads the agent's triage — the label must be provable from the file shape.

### Cross-repo shared (2026-04-21 deeper 10x pass)

Source: aggregated four-repo 10x deeper field test (bootstrap, jekyll, 50projects50days, website-templates — 95 MCP scan operations total). Items here reproduce across ≥2 repos. Single-repo deeper-pass additions live under each track's `Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-21, 10x)` sub-section.

- [x] **Q-SHARED-MD-DISPATCH-UNIFY** `scan_file` on `.md`/`.markdown` returns `{code: "file-unsupported", remediation: "...astro, .css, .htm, .html, .js, .jsx, .mdx, .scss, .ts, .tsx."}` — `.md` and `.markdown` elided from the advertised set — while `session.parseFile` and `tool-apply-fix-internals.ts` both dispatch correctly. Root cause: commit `38e86f5` claimed "route .md/.markdown through parseMarkdown end-to-end" but `src/cli/commands/scan.ts::parseFor` has no `.md`/`.markdown` branch, and the MCP `SCAN_FILE_UNSUPPORTED_REMEDIATION` constant is not rebuilt against the live registry at call time. Reproduced in bootstrap (13 `.md` skipped), jekyll (307 `.md`/`.markdown` invisible to CLI), 50projects50days (`README.md` routed to `skippedByExtension`). Fix: land the CLI `parseFor` `.md`/`.markdown` branch; derive `SCAN_FILE_UNSUPPORTED_REMEDIATION` from `parseableExtensions()` at call time (not module-init); golden fixture (sanitized jekyll-style `_docs/assets.md` with embedded HTML). Folds Q4-MD-CLI-PARSEFOR-MISSING + Q4-SCAN-FILE-UNSUPPORTED-REMEDIATION-STALE filed by the deeper-pass Jekyll scan. Doctrine: "Ambiguous field shapes are dishonest" — remediation prose claims a single source of truth that doesn't match runtime.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT** `rulesEvaluated` disagrees across MCP surfaces on the same `cwd`: `scan_project` reports 53, `propose_config` reports 54, `list_rules` reports 54 (50projects50days deeper pass; same three-way drift reproduced on website-templates where `navigation/href-javascript-void` sits in source + is imported but absent from running registry). Likely cause: JSX/TSX-only rule counted-as-evaluated when JSX input is absent differs between tools; stale subprocess serving a 53-rule bundle against a 54-rule on-disk bundle compounds. Fix: centralize "rules evaluated on this input" in one helper consumed by every surface; add cross-surface invariant test asserting `list_rules` rule set == keys in any scan's `perRuleCoverage`. Pairs with the "Needs judgment" question on JSX/TSX-only rules counted as evaluated on non-JSX inputs. Doctrine: "Verbose meta is signal, not clutter" + cross-surface consistency.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-NEXTSTEP-SCHEMA-DISPATCH-TEST** `nextStepStructured.args.file` on a scan response names `file` as the parameter, but `scan_file`'s schema declares `path`; `suggest_fix.verifyCommandStructured` emits a non-existent `ruleId` parameter on `scan_file` args. An agent that pipes `args` through verbatim hits `InputValidationError` — the structured-next-step contract is broken by silent drift. Reshape: paired src+test fix (originally framed as test-only; agent dispatch returned classification_mismatch because the correctly-written test fails on real drift today). Step 1 — fix the two known drift sites: `src/mcp/next-step.ts:231` rename `args.file`→`args.path` for the `scan_file` emission; `src/mcp/tool-suggest-fix-internals.ts:98-101` rename `args.file`→`args.path` and drop the advisory `ruleId` (move it to a sibling metadata field if the advisory signal is worth keeping). Step 2 — land the typed dispatch test in `tests/unit/mcp/` asserting every `nextStepStructured` and `verifyCommandStructured` `args` object validates against the target tool's input schema at build time (any `args` key not in the target's JSONSchema fails the test); with the src fixes the test now passes green and locks the invariant. Doctrine: "One tool call should answer 'what next?'" — the structured hint must be directly callable.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-SCAN-PROJECT-INLINE-REVIEW-CANDIDATES** `scan_project` returns `actionableManualItems: N` with `files: []` when automated findings are zero on the scanned root. Agent has no inline file:line pointer and must make a separate `checklist` call. Reproduced in jekyll (`test/source` sub-scan returned `actionableManualItems: 1` + empty files array) and website-templates (`plugins/` sub-scan returned `actionableManualItems: 1, files: []`). Fix: add `reviewCandidateFiles?: [{ path, count }]` (or inline `reviewCandidates?: [{file, line, criterion, reason}]` capped by the same `limit`) on `scan_project` per-file entries or as a top-level array, conditional-spread when automated-findings files exist. Doctrine: "One tool call should answer 'what next?'" — currently forces a wasted round trip.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-SCAN-FILE-ERROR-DISCRIMINATION** `scan_file` uses `code: "file-unsupported"` for both unsupported-extension and file-not-found. Bootstrap confirmed: `scan_file` on a nonexistent `.mdx` path (extension IS supported, file doesn't exist) returns `file-unsupported`; `scan_project` has a distinct `cwd-not-found` code. Split: `file-not-found` (path doesn't exist) / `unsupported-extension` (extension not in `PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS`) / `file-unreadable` (permission / IO). Pairs with Q-SHARED-MD-DISPATCH-UNIFY — an agent hitting `file-unsupported` on a `.md` input needs to distinguish "extension truly unsupported" from "not-supported-by-this-subprocess" from "not found on disk." Doctrine: "Ambiguous field shapes are dishonest."
- [x] **Q-SHARED-DISCLOSURE-REASON-ENRICHMENT** `aria/expanded-on-disclosure` fires on patterns where the reason is insufficiently annotated: bootstrap MDX popover examples (Q3-RULE-POPOVER-NOT-DISCLOSURE closed, but 103 lingering findings on attribute combos the closure didn't widen for), website-templates collapse button with `<span class="sr-only">Toggle navigation</span>` accessible name. When the rule fires on an element carrying `.sr-only` / `.visually-hidden` / `aria-label` children, enrich `reason` with "note: element has a visually-hidden text child — if that is the disclosure label, verify the accessible name is complete." Reason-text enrichment only — candidate stays in the primary list. Doctrine: "Surface don't suppress" — enrich reason so agent dismisses faster without heuristic suppression.

### Cross-repo shared (2026-04-22 four-repo 10x re-sweep)

Source: second four-repo 10x sweep (bootstrap, jekyll, 50projects50days, website-templates) via 10 parallel scan agents — consolidated findings dedup-checked against existing Q3/Q4/Q5/Q6 and V1 items. Items below reproduce across ≥2 repos; repo-specific residue lives in each track's deeper-pass subsection.

- [x] **Q-SHARED-META-ARRAY-BUDGET-CAP** Meta-array bloat overruns the MCP host token cap even at `limit: 1` / `minSeverity: "error"`. Full-root scan of website-templates returns 315KB response of which 281KB (89%) is `meta`: `meta.scannedBuildArtifacts` 148KB + `meta.analysisCoverage.parseErrorFiles` + `partialParseFiles` 121KB — both are raw absolute-path arrays that grow linearly with input size. Reproduced on bootstrap site/docs (66KB at `limit:30 → effectiveLimit:3`), jekyll docs (66KB at `limit:10`), website-templates 3/5 template subsets >66KB, and coverage responses (~100% meta). `Q6-BUDGET-UNDER-VENDOR-NOISE` addresses per-file finding cap + per-criterion checklist clip; this item addresses the orthogonal meta-path-array cap. Fix: cap each `meta.*` path array to `≤ N` entries with `{ shown, total }` summary + `truncated: true`, analogous to how `plan` / `files` pagination already works; surface a `response_meta_truncated` warning when applied. Pairs with `response_token_budget_truncated` for end-to-end honesty. Doctrine: composite-budget truncation must be honest across every field that grows with input.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-COVERAGE-FAILING-CRITERIA-DERIVATION** `coverage.failingAutomatedCriteria` drifts from `scan_project.criteriaInFindings` on the same cwd. Bootstrap site/docs: scan surfaces 1.4.1 (14 `color/meaning` findings); coverage omits 1.4.1 from failingAutomatedCriteria. Jekyll docs: scan hits {1.3.3, 1.4.1, 2.4.9}, coverage classifies those as manual-only yet conformance_statement stamps 1.3.3 "fail" and VPAT claims 120 findings. Coffee-shop (website-templates): coverage asserts 6 criteria failing with zero emitted findings — token-truncated scan feeding stale derivation. Root cause: `failingAutomatedCriteria` derives from rule→criterion eligibility independently of what actually fired in this scan; truncated scans inflate the drift. Fix: derive `failingAutomatedCriteria` from the emitted violations set (intersect with `rulesEvaluated.fired`), honour `truncated: true` by carrying the per-criterion "evidence-complete" flag, and add a cross-surface invariant test asserting `coverage.failingAutomatedCriteria ⊆ scan.criteriaInFindings ∪ scan.truncatedCriteriaPossible`. Doctrine: "One tool call should answer 'what next?'" — cross-surface drift forces wasted round trips.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-TSX-PARSER-FALSE-JSX-CONTEXTS** (89d535aa + 6fd5c3e9, turn 6 /continue re-verify) TSX parser enters JSX mode in two contexts where it shouldn't: (a) template-literal contents inside JSX attribute values — Bootstrap's `<Example code={`<iframe src="demo" />`}>` and `<Callout>...</Callout>` on `.mdx`/`.astro` sources trigger `Unclosed JSX element <iframe>/<body>/<span>` partialParse on 12 files (`src/content/docs/components/dropdowns.mdx:384`, `src/content/docs/helpers/ratio.mdx:22`); (b) minified `.js` files containing `<` comparison operators — Jekyll's `lib/jekyll/commands/serve/livereload_assets/livereload.js` errors "Unclosed JSX element `<r.length>`" because `r.length<b.length` parses as a JSX open tag. Both cases silently drop downstream rule coverage for the affected file. Fix for (a): do NOT enter JSX mode inside a template-literal body when the surrounding attribute value is a template-literal JSX expression; treat the contents as opaque string. Fix for (b): gate JSX-mode entry on file extension (`.jsx`/`.tsx`/`.mdx`/`.astro` — not bare `.js`/`.ts`) unless the file has a leading JSX-import signal. **Status (2026-04-22): fixtures landed but parser fix did NOT — reopening.** Real-world fixtures `tests/fixtures/real-world/astro-template-literal-jsx-attr/` and `tests/fixtures/real-world/jekyll-livereload-minified-js/` ship with `zero-parse-errors` expectations **commented out** per CLAUDE.md §7 bug-fix workflow (fixture-first, fix-later); this item was prematurely ticked off when the fixtures landed. The 2026-04-22 fresh 8-scan pass reproduced both symptoms: bootstrap 12 `.mdx` files partial-parse, jekyll `livereload.js` routed to TSX with "Unclosed JSX element `<r.length>`." Pending work: land the two parser fixes in `src/input/parsers/tsx.ts` (attribute-expression template-literal opaque-string handling + JSX-mode entry gate on extension); once both land, reinstate the commented `zero-parse-errors` assertions in both real-world fixtures and mark this item `[x]` with the fix commit hash. Doctrine: silent file drops mask real violations; a fixture whose assertion is commented out does not close its parent item.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-INLINE-STYLE-CONTRAST** `contrast/minimum` and `contrast/non-text` never evaluate `style=` attributes on HTML elements. `rulesByExtension .html` lists contrast rules only under `.css`. Website-templates reproducers: `super-simple-site/index.html:17 style="float:right"` (harmless), `ideal-interior/index.html:211 style="background-color:#cccccc"` on a paragraph carrying dark text (silent miss). Zero contrast findings across 5 sampled templates despite heavy inline-style use. Fix: HTML parser should synthesize a per-element style block (e.g. `#__ra11y_inline_${n} { background-color:#ccc; color:#aaa }`) fed into the CSS-rule pipeline so contrast rules evaluate inline declarations identically to stylesheet rules. Alternative: add an inline-style-specific code path in `src/rules/contrast/*` that reads the element's parsed `style` attribute directly. Pairs with Q3-RULES-BY-EXTENSION-UNDERCOUNT (meta honesty about what ran on `.html`). Doctrine: silent-miss on a common real-world pattern is more costly than any false-positive risk from widening scope.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-VPAT-HONESTY-PACK** Two compounding VPAT honesty bugs surface on any cross-tool run: (1) `conformance_statement` and VPAT report `status: "pass"` on criteria with `reason: "no-evidence"` (15 of 18 "pass" criteria on jekyll docs had no emitted findings — absence interpreted as proof). (2) VPAT defaults to all 86 A/AA/AAA criteria even when `scan_project` ran at default AA level — 45 criteria appear as "Not Evaluated" including AAA items scan never attempted, and VPAT's `Partially Supports` bucket conflates 0-finding criteria ("manual review still required") with 120-finding criteria (both stamped PS). Fix (1): split `status: "pass"` into `"pass"` (evidence-supported) vs `"untested"` (no-evidence) — never sum them under "pass"; surface `untestedCriteriaCount` as a sibling counter. Fix (2): VPAT respects the scan's `level`; criteria outside scope render as `Not Applicable — out of scope` with the scope citation, never `Not Evaluated` without explanation. Pair with `evaluator.scanLevel: "AA"` as a top-level VPAT field; add a cross-tool invariant test asserting VPAT rows are a subset of the standards-at-that-level. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest; "pass" is a load-bearing claim in procurement artifacts.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-DETECT-NATIVE-WRAPPERS-CWD-NOT-FOUND** `detect_native_wrappers` silently succeeds on a nonexistent cwd: `/tmp/jekyll/nonexistent` returns `{ candidates: [], emptyReason: "no-parseable-files" }` with no warning. `scan_project` correctly errors `cwd-not-found` on the same path. Canonical zero-output-success antipattern named in doctrine. Fix: reuse the `scan_project` cwd-existence guard in `src/mcp/handlers/detect-native-wrappers.ts` before globbing; emit structured error `{ code: "cwd-not-found", path }` with no `candidates` array. Tiny — mechanical. Pairs with Q3-DETECT-WRAPPERS-EMPTY-REASON (different empty-reason codes for real-but-empty cases).
- [x] **Q-SHARED-SUGGEST-FIX-GUIDANCE-PRIMARY** `suggest_fix` tool description promises `guidance` responses carry a ranked `primary` fix path plus `alternatives` array. Actual emission for `kind: "guidance"` is `{ kind, explanation, sourceContext, confidence, verifyCommand, verifyCommandStructured }` — no `primary`, no `alternatives`. Reproduced across 4 of 4 guidance-kind calls on website-templates templates. Two resolutions: (a) populate `primary` + `alternatives` as structured fix-path objects (the tool's advertised shape); (b) update the tool description to match the actual shape and drop the promise. Prefer (a): agents budgeting against the documented shape hit a schema-contract gap. Pairs with V1-FIX-AUDIT acceptance gate.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-EXPLAIN-RULE-EQUIVALENT-CRITERIA** `explain_rule` returns `satisfies: [wcag22:*, wcag21:*]` only — omits `section508:*` and `en301549:*` equivalents that the same rule surfaces via `equivalentTo` index on per-finding `criteria` arrays in `scan_file`. Agent auditing Section 508 cannot tell from `explain_rule` whether a rule covers §1194.22(n) or 9.3.3.2 — forced to cross-read rule metadata + standards registry. Fix: `explain_rule` expands `satisfies` through the same reciprocal index the scanner uses for cross-standard equivalence (CLAUDE.md §6 "At registry init, the engine walks every loaded standard's equivalentTo field and builds a reciprocal index"). Asymmetric surface is the bug. Tiny.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-VIDEO-CAPTIONS-IFRAME-EMBEDS** `media/video-captions-missing` only inspects `<video>` elements; `<iframe src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/…">` / Vimeo / Wistia embeds never flagged. Reproduced on website-templates subset B and expected at scale on any content-site shape. Fix: extend the rule's element selector to include `<iframe>` whose `src` matches a curated host allowlist (youtube.com/youtu.be, vimeo.com, wistia.{com,net}, brightcove, loom), with reason "iframe-embedded media — captions must be provided by the host platform; verify the embedded source has captions enabled." Satisfies wcag22:1.2.2 + 1.2.4.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-PASS-RATE-COMPOSITE** `coverage.automatedCriteriaPassRate: 58%` on reveal vs `81%` on super-simple-site — headline composite bundles "criteria the scanner actually ran" with "criteria that emitted zero violations." Rate sinks when 2.2.1 surfaces 7 `setInterval` review candidates on a minified vendor bundle — "candidates present on unactionable file," not "criterion failure." Headline "58% clean" is false on a codebase whose author didn't write the 2.2.1-triggering code. Fix: split into `criteriaEvaluated` (ran), `criteriaClean` (ran, zero findings), `criteriaWithFindings` (ran, ≥1 finding), and `criteriaUntestable` (rule declared eligible but no applicable input). Headline becomes `criteriaClean / criteriaEvaluated`; never mix in untestable-by-definition criteria. Pairs with Q4-RULES-EVALUATED-COMPOSITE (closed) — same doctrine, different field.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-NO-CONFIG-WARNING-TINY-REPO** `warnings: ["no_config_found"]` fires on every tiny repo and every one-off demo — 50projects50days (50 standalone files each of ≤ 3 files), demo-size scans of bootstrap/jekyll sub-trees, website-templates individual template dirs. `configSource: null` in `meta` already signals the same absence; the top-level warning is duplicative noise. Fix: drop the warning when `configSource: null` is already emitted AND either `filesScanned < 10` OR no `package.json` / `ra11y.config.*` exists anywhere in the searched directory walk. Alternative: demote to a `meta.configHint` prose field. Doctrine: warnings are codes for genuinely out-of-band signals; demo-size scan with no config is the normal case.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-ACTIVE-NATIVE-WRAPPERS-NULL-VS-EMPTY** (60332d9c, turn 3) `activeNativeWrappers: null` on pure-HTML templates (website-templates 5/5 subset) vs `activeNativeWrappers: []` on JSX projects with no detected wrappers vs `activeNativeWrappers: [{name,source,...}]` when populated — three shapes for "wrapper detection didn't find anything." The null-vs-[] distinction forces a two-read disambiguation ("is this feature unavailable, or did it run but return empty?"). Fix: omit the field entirely when native-wrapper detection is not applicable (non-JSX file set) — present-when-meaningful; ship `[]` when JSX files were scanned but zero wrappers detected; ship the tagged list when populated. Tiny. Pairs with Q3-OPAQUE-COMPONENTS-TOP-EMPTY-DISHONEST (closed).

### Cross-repo shared (2026-04-22 fresh 8-scan sweep)

Source: fresh 8-scan pass across bootstrap / jekyll / 50projects50days / website-templates, angles 1 (full audit) + 2 (deep-read triage). Items below reproduce across ≥2 repos and are net-new after dedup against the 10x re-sweep items above and the per-track deeper-pass subsections.

- [x] **Q-SHARED-LIMIT-REQUEST-VS-EFFECTIVE** `limit: 100` (callers raise it deliberately to page fewer times) silently clipped to `effectiveLimit: 5` on bootstrap, `15` on jekyll docs, `1` on website-templates via the token-density cap. `effectiveLimit` is already on `meta` but buried; the structured signal is `warnings: ["response_token_budget_truncated"]` with no `(requested, effective, reasonCode)` payload. A caller asking for 100 and getting 5 has a 20× silent drop unless they know to inspect `meta.effectiveLimit`. Fix: (a) promote `effectiveLimit` to the same surface as `truncated`/`nextOffset` on every paginated response (scan, scan_project, review_candidates, checklist); (b) attach a `warningsDetails.response_token_budget_truncated: { requestedLimit, effectiveLimit, reason: "token_density" | "meta_array_size" | "per_file_cap" }` payload. Pairs with Q-SHARED-META-ARRAY-BUDGET-CAP (closed) and Q6-BUDGET-UNDER-VENDOR-NOISE (closed) — both close the *why*; this item closes *whether the caller notices*. Doctrine: "Zero-output success is ambiguous failure" — 20× silent reduction is its sibling.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-PLAN-SUMMARY-VERIFY-IN-SOURCE-INFLATION** `scan_project.plan.summary` prose still reads `"2273 violations (266 mechanical, 616 guidance, 57 runtime-only, 1334 verify-in-source)"` on bootstrap — 59% of the claimed "violations" total is verify-in-source (prose-only, not-yet-actionable). V1-SHAPE-FIXCLASS-HEADLINE (closed) broke out the structured `fixesByClass` lanes, but the legacy `summary` prose still sums all four lanes under the word "violations." Fix: `summary` prose reads `"N findings (mechanical: M, guidance: G, runtime-only: R, verify-in-source: V)"` — drop the word "violations" from the parenthetical headline when verify-in-source is non-zero, OR split into two summaries: `violationsSummary` (mechanical + guidance + runtime-only) and `reviewSummary` (verify-in-source). Pairs with Q6-PLAN-TOTALFINDINGS-VIOLATIONS-VS-NOTES (closed) and J-3 (open). Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-SAFE-EDITS-VS-MECHANICAL-DISAGREEMENT** `plan.safeEditsAvailable: 14` vs `plan.fixesByClass.mechanical: 266` on the same bootstrap response — two sibling counters both framed as "how many fixes an agent can apply" disagree 18×. Q3-PLAN-MECHANICAL-EDITS-COUNTER-DRIFT (closed) standardized `safeEditsAvailable` as "mechanical + verify-in-source" but `fixesByClass.mechanical` counts mechanical only — the names are confusing because "safe" sounds like "mechanical" to an agent reading the headline. Fix: rename `safeEditsAvailable` to `mechanicalPlusVerifyInSourceCount` (ugly but honest) OR drop it and let agents sum `fixesByClass.mechanical + fixesByClass.verifyInSource` themselves. Two sibling counters with overlapping semantics force the agent to pick one; the doctrine rule is that counters must count one kind. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-LIKELY-IRRELEVANT-IFRAME-NOT-MEDIA** `wcag22:1.2.1 / 1.2.3 / 1.2.5` candidates surface as ACTIONABLE (not `likelyIrrelevant`) on bootstrap despite zero `<video>` / `<audio>` / `<track>` in the scanned tree. Root cause: the media finder classifies `<iframe>` as a media indicator, and `site/src/content/docs/helpers/ratio.mdx:14` has an `<iframe>`. Iframes are NOT prerecorded A/V per the SC definition. Fix: iframe presence should count as media ONLY when `src` matches the video-host allowlist (youtube/vimeo/wistia/loom/brightcove — same list as Q-SHARED-VIDEO-CAPTIONS-IFRAME-EMBEDS). Bare iframes don't promote 1.2.* out of `likelyIrrelevant`. Pairs with Q-SHARED-VIDEO-CAPTIONS-IFRAME-EMBEDS (closed) — same allowlist, inverse direction.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-FIXDESCREF-SAME-GROUP-INLINE-DEDUPE** Duplicate `fixDescriptionRef.hash` emitted inline on 6 adjacent same-rule findings that share a `groupKey` — 50projects50days `verify-account-ui/index.html` `forms/labels-required` emits six `fixDescriptionRef: { hash: "2670e6e25ccd" }` rows on adjacent lines, all pointing at the identical dedupe-table entry. V1-REF-DEDUPE collapsed descriptions to the table, but the ref itself is still re-inlined per finding when the shared groupKey could carry it once. Fix: when ≥2 findings share the same `groupKey` AND `fixDescriptionRef.hash`, hoist the ref to the group level as `groupFixDescriptionRef` and omit the per-finding `fixDescriptionRef` on the siblings. Pairs with V1-REF-DEDUPE extension and with the per-file pattern rollup (V1-NOISE-RULE-PER-FILE-ROLLUP). Doctrine: verbose meta is signal, but repeated identical payload is bloat.
