## Track Q4 — Static-site-generator field test (Jekyll, 2026-04-20)

Owner: main session + general-purpose. Source: 10-agent parallel eval against the Jekyll repo (jekyll/jekyll, /tmp/jekyll). Slices: `_layouts/`, `_includes/`, `pages/`, `_docs/` (alphabetical split), `_posts/` + `_tutorials/`, CSS/SCSS, raw `test/source/*.html`, `lib/` scaffold templates, Ruby source tree, and an MCP surface critique from a non-React-project angle.

Jekyll is the canonical static-site-generator shape: HTML templates with Liquid (`{% ... %}`, `{{ ... }}`), YAML frontmatter, `.md`/`.markdown` content, SCSS, ERB fixtures, Ruby source. Where Q3 (Bootstrap) probed the docs-site MDX/Astro axis, Q4 probes the Ruby/SSG/Liquid axis — the failure modes are different. Cross-referenced against Q3 to avoid duplication; items that overlap are tagged as such.

**MCP freshness:** one MCP subprocess spawned post-rebuild (pid 16243, started 23:07 vs dist rebuilt at 22:30), so findings reflect current bundle behavior — the Q3 stale-process confound does not apply here.

### v0.2.0 — accepted (P0 — parser correctness, load-bearing)

- [x] **Q4-LIQUID-ATTR-QUOTE-BUG** HTML parser's attribute-value tokenizer breaks on nested-quote Liquid. `lang="{{ site.lang | default: "en-US" }}"` tokenizes as `lang="{{ site.lang | default: "` — the inner `"` terminates the attribute value, leaving `en-US" }}"` as stray content. Triggers FALSE `parsing/html-has-lang` findings on every Jekyll scaffold template (`lib/blank_template/_layouts/default.html:2`, `lib/theme_template/...`) — every `jekyll new` site inherits the false positive. Fix: teach the attribute-value tokenizer to recognize `{{ ... }}` / `{% ... %}` spans and skip their contents when searching for the closing quote. Load-bearing: until this is fixed, every Liquid-templated HTML project gets confident wrong findings.
- [x] **Q4-LIQUID-TEXT-LITERAL** Rules that consume visible text (`semantics/label-in-name`, `semantics/list-structure`, `document/page-titled`, `navigation/link-descriptive-text`) treat Liquid template expressions as literal visible text, producing FALSE positives when the rendered output would be fine. Examples: `docs_contents_mobile.html:2` (Liquid `{% for %}` loop inside `<select>` tagged as the accessible-name string); `docs_contents.html:9` (`{% capture %}` block reinserts `<a>` inside `<li>` at a later line — `list-structure` sees naked `<a>`-in-`<ul>`). Fix: strip or placeholder-ize Liquid/ERB/Jinja directives in the parser's text-node output so rules operate on the rendered-text shape, not the source shape. Pair with a `template_directive_stripped` signal in `reason` text so an agent knows the check ran against the stripped text. Doctrine: not heuristic suppression — the rule's premise (visible text) is what's being computed, and Liquid directives are not visible text.
- [x] **Q4-SCSS-DISCOVERY-WIRE** Audited the full path — the wiring was already complete at the time the item was filed: `feat(input): route .scss through the scss parser end-to-end` (96620ec) landed all four plumbing pieces (`PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS`, `parseForExtension` dispatch, `extensionMatches` `.scss → .css` alias, apply-fix `Ext` discriminator). Reproduced against current `main` via a direct `scan_file` handler call and an MCP-subprocess JSON-RPC round-trip: both parse `.scss` cleanly and fire `contrast/minimum` on authored Sass (ratio 4.48:1 flagged). `discoverFilesWithDiagnostics` accepts `.scss` and emits no `skippedByExtension` entry. The Jekyll field report was observing a stale bundle despite the turn-10 freshness check. Added two regression tests in `tests/unit/mcp/tools.test.ts` — one asserting `scan_file` on a `.scss` file parses end-to-end and fires `contrast/minimum` with `wcag22:1.4.3`, one asserting `scan_project` on a mixed `.scss`+`.css` directory counts every `.scss` under `filesByExtension` and does NOT report them as `skippedByExtension`. Future refactors that drop any link in the chain (discovery allow-list → parser dispatcher → rule eligibility) will trip the tripwires.
- [x] **Q4-MARKDOWN-SUPPORT** ADR-only phase done in ADR 0025 (4e0bc95); leaving item open for Q4-MARKDOWN-IMPL. `.md` (157) and `.markdown` (150) return `file-unsupported` in Jekyll — every `_docs/*.md`, `_posts/*.markdown`, `_tutorials/*.md`, `pages/*.md`, and `README.markdown` is invisible. Raw HTML embedded in markdown (tables, iframes, admonition divs, inline `<a>`/`<img>`) never reaches any rule. Options: (a) full CommonMark parser (large); (b) lightweight HTML-in-markdown extractor that strips `#`-headings + fenced-code blocks, feeds HTML-block residue to `parseHtml`, scans `![alt](url)` for alt-text, and maps kramdown IAL (`{: .note .warning}`) to `class=` on the preceding block. (b) is ~300 LOC, unlocks static-site-generator ecosystem (Jekyll, Hugo, MkDocs, Docusaurus, Astro content collections). Decision: explore (b); ADR before implementation.
- [x] **Q4-MARKDOWN-IMPL** Implement Option B per ADR 0025 — ~300 LOC HTML-residue extractor at src/input/parsers/markdown.ts, discovery wiring, analysisCoverage hint. Blocks on nothing. Medium. **Cross-ref (2026-04-21 Bootstrap second-pass):** Bootstrap repo skipped 13 `.md` files as "extensions_skipped_no_parser" despite having no MDX/Liquid syntax — those are plain-HTML-in-markdown just like Jekyll `.md`. When implementing, consider a fast-path: route `.md` through the `.mdx` parser when no MDX syntax is detected (no `import`/`export`, no JSX element opens), emit `md_files_skipped_no_parser` warning if the parser declines. Same implementation; two caller sites validate it. (a1a0e3b+a7b169e, turn 31)

### v0.2.0 — accepted (P1 — template-awareness and fragment detection)

- [x] **Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION** Jekyll `_includes/` partials (top.html, footer.html, header.html, error.html) are fragments — they have no `<html>`, no `<head>`, start mid-document with `{%- include ... -%}`. ra11y currently fires `document/page-titled`, `semantics/landmark-main`, `navigation/skip-link`, `parsing/html-has-lang` on these files as if they were full pages. Detect the fragment shape (file under `_includes/`, `_layouts/`, `components/`, or file without matching `<html>`/`<body>`/`<head>` structure) and suppress full-page-required rules with a structured `fragment_detected` signal (review-candidate, not suppression — the agent can still manually check that the composed layout has `<main>`). Alternative: emit an `analysisCoverage.fragmentFiles: [paths]` counter so the agent knows partials were scanned but page-level rules didn't apply. **Confirmation (2026-04-21 second pass):** 2.4.5 finder still flags page-titled sibling cases (Q4-PARTIAL-PAGE-TITLED already closed handles page-titled; the broader fragment-detection gap persists for landmark-main + skip-link). Still open.
- [x] **Q4-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-CLASSIFIER-DRIFT** (dfd9ed5, turn 25, 2026-04-21) Fixed in `src/mcp/analysis-coverage.ts` (agent confirmed real location; `src/input/analysis-coverage.ts` does not exist). Classifier now accumulates per-file family sets and performs per-file majority-vote rather than a global accumulation, eliminating cross-file tag bleed. All existing template tests pass; added targeted round-trip test for Liquid + Jinja coexistence. 107 lines changed.
- [x] **Q4-XMLLANG-LANG-MISMATCH** `test/source/_layouts/default.html:7` has `<html xmlns="..." xml:lang="en" lang="en-us">` — both present, differ (`en` vs `en-us`). No rule validates the consistency. Extend `document/lang-attribute` or add `document/lang-xmllang-mismatch`. WCAG 3.1.1.
- [x] **Q4-CROSS-INCLUDE-LANDMARK-COMPOSITION** Jekyll layouts compose as `top.html` (head + opening body) + `{{ content }}` + `footer.html`. Each file parses in isolation; `semantics/landmark-main` fires on `error.html` (no `<main>`) but stays silent on `default.html` even though the composed page also has no `<main>`. Two options: (a) teach the scanner to follow `{% include %}` directives for in-tree composition (complex, cross-file, may produce false positives); (b) document the cross-file gap in a `couldBeWrongBecause: ["partial_or_layout_file_requires_composed_check"]` on the relevant rules. (b) is the honest minimum; (a) is a larger design.

### v0.2.0 — accepted (P1 — MCP shape for non-React projects)

- [x] **Q4-LANGUAGE-CLASS-WARNING** No headline-level warning when `skippedByExtension` shows a content-language dominance that ra11y can't parse. Jekyll scan drops 307 `.md`/`.markdown` files, 160 `.rb`, 18 `.scss` silently under a generic `extensions_skipped_no_parser` code — an agent skimming the top-level fields concludes "20 findings, scan had teeth" when the reality is "the actual content layer was never seen." Emit structured codes: `content_files_skipped: { count, exts }` when markdown/rst count > 50; `scss_files_skipped` until Q4-SCSS-DISCOVERY-WIRE lands; `source_language_unsupported: { language }` on detection of Ruby/Python/Go/PHP dominance. Doctrine: "Zero-output success is ambiguous failure" — sibling case for "near-zero output on the wrong-shape project." Note: `scss_files_skipped` code intentionally NOT added — `.scss` is in PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS and never reaches `skippedByExtension`. (6431452, turn 24)
- [x] **Q4-PROPOSE-CONFIG-ECOSYSTEM** (d8fdce5, turn 26, 2026-04-21) `propose_config` emits a `@ra11y/core` TypeScript config into any project — including one whose root has `Gemfile` / `Pipfile` / `go.mod` and no `package.json`. No warning that the consumer may not want to add a Node toolchain. Fix: probe the repo root for ecosystem markers (`package.json`, `Gemfile`, `pyproject.toml`, `go.mod`, `Cargo.toml`) and either (a) emit `warnings: ["foreign_ecosystem_detected: ruby"]` alongside the config, (b) propose an alternative invocation (`npx @ra11y/core scan` rather than a committed `ra11y.config.ts`), or (c) ask the agent to confirm via `nextStep` before writing config. Do NOT suppress the config — the user may genuinely want to add it — but surface the context.
- [x] **Q4-BOOTSTRAP-CI-SNIPPET-ECOSYSTEM** `bootstrap` CI snippet hardcodes `actions/setup-node@v4` in the suggested workflow. For Ruby/Python/Go projects, the consumer's existing CI almost certainly uses `ruby/setup-ruby` / `actions/setup-python` / `actions/setup-go`. Two options: (a) detect the ecosystem and pick the matching setup action; (b) emit the snippet as additive — "add this job alongside your existing CI" — with a prose preface acknowledging the existing toolchain. (b) is less fragile. **Confirmation (2026-04-21 second pass):** still open; Jekyll re-scan confirmed the `setup-node@v4` hardcode is what surfaces on a Ruby project.
- [x] **Q4-SSG-BUILD-HINT** (95c87a5, turn 28) Detect static-site-generator signals at the repo root (`_config.yml` → Jekyll; `hugo.toml`/`config.toml` → Hugo; `astro.config.*` → Astro; `.eleventy.js` → Eleventy; `gatsby-config.*` → Gatsby; `mkdocs.yml` → MkDocs) and add a `nextStep` suggestion: "for full coverage, build the site and point `additionalPaths` at the rendered output (`_site/`, `public/`, `dist/`)." Structured hint: `meta.detectedFramework: { name, buildOutput, buildCommand }`. This is the discoverable answer to "what does ra11y CAN'T do on my static site." Pairs with existing `analysisCoverage.hints` prose.
- [x] **Q4-ADDITIONALPATHS-REDUNDANT** `scan_project` with `additionalPaths: ["docs/_includes"]` returns `additionalPathsScanned.filesAdded: 0` when those files were already discovered by default enumeration. No signal to the agent that the flag was redundant (not ignored — just redundant). Emit `warnings: ["redundant_additional_paths"]` when the requested paths' files are already in the default-discovered set. Companion to Q3-ADDITIONAL-PATHS-SKIP-REASON (caller asked for N, got M_new, (N - M_new) were already scanned or dropped — distinguish).

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P1 — rule gaps + tightenings)

Deduped against Q3 where applicable. Items marked `[dup-Q3]` overlap and should share implementation.

- [x] **Q4-RULE-IFRAME-TITLE** [dup-Q3-RULE-IFRAME-TITLE] `<iframe>` without `title` or with generic title (`"YouTube video"`, `"iframe"`). Jekyll `_tutorials/video-walkthroughs.md:10` + `_docs/assets.md`. WCAG 4.1.2 + 2.4.1. Roll into Q3 ticket.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-TABLE-CAPTION-SCOPE** [dup-Q3-RULE-TABLE-CAPTION + Q3-RULE-TABLE-TH-SCOPE] Table caption + th-scope. Jekyll's `_docs/front-matter.md:51-200`, `_docs/permalinks.md:50-170`, `_docs/variables.md`, `_tutorials/csv-to-table.md:179-204` — repeated pattern across 20+ Jekyll tables. Roll into Q3 tickets. Covered by Q3-RULE-TABLE-CAPTION + Q3-RULE-TABLE-TH-SCOPE (77db3b5); `src/rules/semantics/table-caption-missing.ts` + `src/rules/semantics/table-th-scope-missing.ts` evaluate HTML and fire on Jekyll's tables through the shared HTML parser path. No Jekyll-specific work required.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-NOTE-ROLE-FROM-CLASS** New `aria/role-from-class-only`. Admonition patterns (`.note`, `.warning`, `.alert`, `.tip`, `.info`) — HTML `<div class="note warning">` or kramdown IAL `{: .note .warning}` — without `role="note"` / `role="alert"` / textual severity prefix. WCAG 1.3.3 + 1.4.1 + 4.1.2. Jekyll docs use this pattern ~30 times across `_docs/`, all invisible today (even without markdown support, the pattern will apply to every SSG). Distinct from Q3-RULE-COLOR-SOLE-INDICATOR which is about status-colored text anywhere — this is admonition-widget-specific.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-EXTERNAL-LINK-NEW-WINDOW** New `navigation/link-target-blank-announcement`. `<a target="_blank">` without `rel="noopener noreferrer"` + no new-window announcement (visible text, sr-only span, or `aria-label` containing "opens in new window"). WCAG 3.2.5. Jekyll's `showcase.html:14` + `_tutorials/navigation.md` have the pattern. (713c7fe+a362448, turn 29)
- [x] **Q4-RULE-DUPLICATE-LANDMARK-UNLABELED** New `semantics/duplicate-landmark-unlabeled`. Two or more `<nav>` / `<main>` / `<aside>` / `<form>` landmarks on a rendered page, none or fewer-than-N with `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby`. WCAG 1.3.1 + 2.4.1. Jekyll's `header.html` ships two `<nav>` elements (mobile + desktop), neither labeled. Requires cross-file composition awareness (Q4-CROSS-INCLUDE-LANDMARK-COMPOSITION) to fully catch. **Confirmation (2026-04-21 second pass):** in-file single-nav variant also worth covering — `header.html` on the second pass surfaced a single unlabeled `<nav>` in a fragment file; widen the rule's fragment-file single-nav eligibility alongside the multi-nav case.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-SECTION-ACCESSIBLE-NAME** New `semantics/section-accessible-name-missing`. `<section>` used as a content wrapper without `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby` / enclosing heading. WCAG 1.3.1 / 4.1.2. `<section>` without name is semantically a generic `<div>` for AT. Low priority (many `<section>` usages are fine as-is per spec — only flag when section is a direct child of `<body>` or sibling to other landmarks).
- [x] **Q4-RULE-PLACEHOLDER-ALT** New `media/alt-text-placeholder`. `<img alt="...">` / `alt="image"` / `alt="screenshot"` / `alt="TODO"` / `alt="placeholder"` — generic alt that teaches bad patterns when copy-pasted. WCAG 1.1.1. Jekyll's `_tutorials/` docs + `pages/showcase.html` + `_docs/posts.md` teach `![My helpful screenshot](...)` as canonical — docs propagate weak patterns to every user. (0177360, turn 30)
- [x] **Q4-RULE-PAGINATION-GLYPH-NAME** New review finder `navigation/pagination-glyph-accessible-name`. Pagination controls using `«` / `»` / `‹` / `›` as sole link text without `aria-label` or sr-only expansion. WCAG 2.4.4 + 4.1.2. Jekyll's `_docs/pagination.md:112-169` teaches the glyph pattern; similar patterns across design-system docs.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-DISABLED-LINK-SURROGATE** New `aria/disabled-link-surrogate`. `<span class="disabled">` / `<span class="previous">` used as a disabled control where the live state is a `<a>`. Without `aria-disabled="true"`, AT announces the span as static text, losing the disabled-state signal. WCAG 4.1.2.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-XMLLANG-LANG-MATCH** [cross-ref Q4-XMLLANG-LANG-MISMATCH] Same rule — the tightening doubles as a new rule if `document/lang-attribute` doesn't already cover `xml:lang`. (turn 32.5 — redundant: `document/lang-attribute.ts` already reads `xml:lang`, validates BCP 47 agreement at line 69-85, emits a tailored violation when the two disagree; Q4-XMLLANG-LANG-MISMATCH already closed this gap)

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P2)

- [x] **Q4-WARNING-DOWNGRADE-NOISE** (623c4c42, turn 3) `warnings: ["template_files_parsed_as_literal"]` fires on every Liquid-templated scan — it's a constant on any Jekyll/Hugo/Eleventy repo. Consider demoting to a `meta.templateDirectivesFound` hint when no findings hit lines containing directives (i.e. the literal-parse didn't actually pollute results). Keep the warning when it matters; drop it when it's constant noise.
- [ ] **Q4-LABELS-REQUIRED-VS-LABEL-ADJACENT-OVERLAP** (follow-up from Q5-LABEL-ADJACENT-UNASSOCIATED, turn 2 this run) The new `forms/label-adjacent-unassociated` rule can fire alongside `forms/labels-required` on the same input when there is an adjacent `<label>` without `for=`. Both emissions are honest per AI-first surface-don't-suppress doctrine, but field use may reveal whether the overlap is readable or noisy. Observation path: if field reports cite "same input flagged twice," consider de-overlapping by making `labels-required` silent when a label sibling exists (and let `label-adjacent-unassociated` be the canonical voice for that shape). Do NOT preemptively suppress — wait for evidence that the dual-emission confuses consumers. WCAG 1.3.1 / 3.3.2 / 4.1.2. Cross-ref commits 5fdc228+a3f04657 (turn 2 this invocation).
- [x] **Q4-RULES-EVALUATED-COMPOSITE** (bc29957, turn 27) `rulesEvaluated: 52` reports total loaded rules, not rules-with-eligible-inputs. On a pure-HTML/SSG project, many React-specific rules show `filesEligible: 0` in `perRuleCoverage` — they didn't fire because they couldn't. Replace the single count with a split: `rulesEvaluated: { loaded, withEligibleInputs, fired }` so an agent sees "52 loaded, 18 had eligible inputs on this file set, 9 fired." Doctrine: "Composite headline counts are dishonest."
- [x] **Q4-PARTIAL-PAGE-TITLED** `document/page-titled` fires on `top.html` (a `<head>` fragment) because the rule sees `{% seo %}` as literal text, not a rendered `<title>`. Pairs with Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION — the rule should skip fragment files, or at minimum emit `couldBeWrongBecause: ["title_may_be_template_injected"]` when the file lacks `<html>`/`<body>`.

### Considered and rejected (per CLAUDE.md §1)

- **Auto-suppress React-specific rules on non-React projects** → rejected per §1 "Labeled buckets are suppression too." The rules correctly no-op when `filesEligible: 0`; the agent sees that in `perRuleCoverage`. Don't filter — surface. Q4-RULES-EVALUATED-COMPOSITE is the right fix (make the empty-eligible-set state legible at the headline level, keep the per-rule data honest).
- **Auto-exclude `_site/`, `vendor/bundle/`, `node_modules/` in `propose_config`** → **accepted in a narrower form**: these are definitionally build output / vendor dumps where findings are not the user's to fix. Fold into `DEFAULT_EXCLUDED_PATTERNS` (see `src/input/discover.ts`) rather than `propose_config` — the exclude belongs at discovery, not config. CLAUDE.md §1 "Default-exclude globs are suppression too" permits this because findings in those paths are "definitionally wrong for any consumer." Add `_site/`, `.jekyll-cache/`, `vendor/bundle/`, `_build/` (Hugo), `public/` (Gatsby — though `public/` is ambiguous). Tracked as **Q4-DEFAULT-EXCLUDE-BUILD-DIRS** in P2 above if landed.
- **Heuristic "this looks like a Jekyll include, suppress skip-link finding"** → rejected as heuristic suppression. Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION is the principled alternative: detect partial/fragment structurally, not by path-pattern guessing.
- **Full markdown CommonMark parser as a P0** → deferred. Q4-MARKDOWN-SUPPORT option (b) — a lightweight HTML-in-markdown extractor — covers 80% of real findings at ~15% of the implementation cost. Revisit full parser after the extractor ships.
- **Auto-detect and scan `_site/` output when Jekyll is detected** → rejected as "tool should not run build toolchains." The agent reads `_config.yml` + `Gemfile` + knows `bundle exec jekyll build`; ra11y should point at the output, not produce it. Q4-SSG-BUILD-HINT is the right mechanism.

### Second-pass additions (2026-04-21)

Source: second Jekyll-repo pass after turn-28 items landed. Confirms three previously-open Q4 items (fragment detection, Bootstrap-CI-snippet ecosystem, duplicate-landmark) still fire on the current build — notes appended inline above rather than filed as new items. The four items below are net-new honest-signal gaps. Two Jekyll findings (`BOOTSTRAP-EXCLUDE-ABSOLUTE-PATH`, `BOOTSTRAP-EXCLUDES-SCSS-BECAUSE-BUILD-ARTIFACT`) moved into `Q-SHARED-PROPOSE-CONFIG-RELATIVE-PATHS` / `Q-SHARED-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SASS-PARTIAL` above.

- [x] **Q4-PARSE-ERROR-DETAIL** `parse_errors_present` / `parseErrorFileCount: 1` surfaced on `docs/_includes/top.html` with no detail — which parser failed, where, why. Pair the top-level boolean with a structured `analysisCoverage.parseErrors: [{ path, parser, reason }]` so an agent has a fix-pivot beyond "one file didn't parse." Doctrine: verbose meta is scan-confidence signal; the bare boolean is the silent-failure shape. Distinct from Q3-PARSE-ERROR-FILE-CONTRADICTION (that item distinguishes total vs partial failure; this item populates the detail payload for both classes). Landed as `{ path, parser, reason }` entries on both existing buckets (`parseErrorFiles` invisible + `partialParseFiles` partial), always shipped when non-empty (no verboseMeta gate) — the split is the honest shape the prior Q3 work established; enriching both with the per-entry detail is the minimal additive fix.
- [x] **Q4-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-REASON** `warnings: ["scanned_build_artifacts_present"]` fires with paths but no per-path classification reason. Under the doctrine's "provable from code" bar (`ai-first-consumer.md` §labeled-buckets), the label has to survive inspection — `scannedBuildArtifacts` should be `[{ path, reason: "contains-data-url-gradient" | "minified" | "dist-path" | "hashed-filename" | "sourcemap-sibling" }]` so the agent can triage without reading each file. Pairs with Q-SHARED-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SASS-PARTIAL (that item fixes the classifier's predicate; this item makes the classifier's verdict inspectable). Structured over `warnings: string[]` — reason is a per-item payload, not a response-level code.
- [x] **Q4-REVIEW-1.4.5-SR-ONLY-SIBLING** `header.html:7` logo image flagged for `wcag22:1.4.5` (images of text) but the reason doesn't mention the `<span class="sr-only">Jekyll</span>` sibling inside the same `<a>`. The sibling is a strong dismissal signal: the image is the visual rendering of the text, and an SR-only text equivalent is already carried in the DOM. Extend the 1.4.5 finder to detect `sibling_sr_only_text_present` when the flagged image shares a parent with an element carrying `.sr-only` / `.visually-hidden` / `aria-label` on the parent anchor; enrich `reason` with "note: a visually-hidden text sibling (`.sr-only` span) is present — if the image is the textual logotype and the sibling is the SR-accessible equivalent, this is the documented logotype pattern." Reason-text enrichment only — candidate stays visible.
- [x] **Q4-REVIEW-SANITIZE-LIQUID-FINDERS** `showcase.html:16` 1.4.5 reason literally quotes the Liquid template expression `{{ entry.name }}` as the repeated surrounding text. Q4-LIQUID-TEXT-LITERAL stripped Liquid in the rule-side text-node paths, but the fix didn't propagate to `src/review/finders/**` — the same `template_directive_stripped` sanitizer must run on text the finders harvest for reason-building. Apply the existing stripper helper at the finder-side surrounding-text extraction sites; add a fixture under `tests/fixtures/real-world/jekyll-liquid-review-finder/` with a `{{ entry.name }}` repetition asserting the reason shows the rendered placeholder form (or omits the directive entirely) rather than the source token.
- [x] **Q4-RELEVANCE-REASON-PARSE-COVERAGE-CAVEAT** `likelyIrrelevant` on 1.2.x criteria reports "no `<video>` or `<audio>` elements" as if the file set were fully inspected, when 307 `.md`/`.markdown` files were silently skipped. When `skippedByExtension` includes content extensions above a high-confidence threshold (`.md`, `.markdown`, `.rst`, `.adoc` — authored-content extensions that commonly embed media), enrich `relevanceReason` with a parse-coverage caveat: "note: X files in content extensions ({exts}) were skipped — if the site embeds `<video>` / `<audio>` in markdown content, the media check is under-covered." Doctrine: `likelyIrrelevant` is one of the few labels that must be provable from the code (`ai-first-consumer.md` §labeled-buckets); the bucket claim is only honest when the corpus was actually inspected. Pairs with Q4-MARKDOWN-IMPL — once markdown parsing lands, the caveat vanishes automatically for those repos.

### Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-21, 10x)

Source: 27-scan deeper pass against the jekyll repo as part of the four-repo 10x field test. Load-bearing discovery: commit `38e86f5` ("route .md/.markdown through parseMarkdown end-to-end") overstated scope — the MCP side works, the CLI side does not (see Q-SHARED-MD-DISPATCH-UNIFY). Items below are Jekyll-specific residue after the cross-repo Q-SHARED items are filed above.

- [x] **Q4-TEMPLATE-CLASSIFIER-LIQUID-AS-MUSTACHE-SINGLE-FILE** Q4-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-CLASSIFIER-DRIFT fixed the cross-file majority-vote drift, but a single-file variant remains: jekyll `_layouts/default.html` (pure Liquid with `{%- include -%}`, `{%- comment -%}`, `{{- content -}}`) is classified as `handlebars-or-mustache` because the per-file majority-vote drops `{%- ... -%}` Liquid-only tokens and counts `{{ ... }}` tokens — which the two families share. Fix: treat `{%- ... -%}` / `{% capture %}` / `{% if %}` as high-confidence Liquid-only evidence that decisively wins the per-file vote even when `{{ ... }}` tokens outnumber them. Pairs with Q3-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-STARLIKE-MISDETECT (Bootstrap half on Astro/YAML). Doctrine: verbose meta integrity — wrong template-directive classification leads the agent to reach for the wrong strip rules downstream.
- [x] **Q4-WARNING-DETAILS-CROSS-SURFACE-UNIFY** `warningsDetails.extensions_skipped_no_parser` is populated on `coverage` responses but absent from `scan_project` responses — same warning code, two shapes. Website-templates reproduces the same pattern for `scanned_build_artifacts_present`, `parse_errors_present` (populated on some surfaces, bare code elsewhere). Fix: ensure every response carrying `warnings: [code]` also carries `warningsDetails: { [code]: { ... } }` when details exist for that code; when no details exist, omit the key rather than shipping empty. Typed-test the cross-surface matrix: for each warning code, it must appear or not appear consistently across `scan_project` / `scan_file` / `coverage` / `checklist`. Doctrine: cross-surface consistency + present-when-meaningful.
- [x] **Q4-LABEL-IN-NAME-LIQUID-STRIP-MISSING** Q4-LIQUID-TEXT-LITERAL was marked closed but `semantics/label-in-name` specifically still quotes raw Liquid `{% for ... %}` as "visible text" on `docs_contents_mobile.html:2` (and two sibling files). Reach: the strip helper was applied to some rules but `label-in-name` never picked it up. Fix: audit every rule under `src/rules/semantics/**` + `src/rules/navigation/**` that harvests visible-text strings, ensure each calls the `stripTemplateDirectives` helper before comparing, and add a rule-inventory regression test that mounts a fixture with `{% for %}` / `{{ var }}` tokens and asserts NO rule emits a finding quoting the raw directive. Pair with Q4-REVIEW-SANITIZE-LIQUID-FINDERS (finder-side already tracked).
- [x] **Q4-SUGGEST-FIX-PROPAGATES-POISONED-VISIBLE-TEXT** `suggest_fix` primary proposal for `semantics/label-in-name` on the Jekyll `docs_contents_mobile` findings builds an `aria-label="{% for section in site.data.docs_nav %}..."` — pasting the raw Liquid template expression into a static ARIA attribute. If an agent applied this mechanically, the page would ship a broken accessible name on every render. Root cause: `suggest_fix` reads the rule's `visibleText` field, not a sanitized version. Fix: run `stripTemplateDirectives` on any text that `suggest_fix` interpolates into `newText`; when the result is empty or nonsense, fall through to a guidance-only suggestion with `fixClass: "guidance"`. Doctrine: verbose meta integrity — `suggest_fix.safety: "safe"` on a proposal that would break the page is a false safety claim.

### Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-22, 10x re-sweep)

Source: four-repo 10x re-sweep. Jekyll-specific residue after cross-repo items above. Liquid-strip gap now reaches `document/` rules that Q4-LABEL-IN-NAME-LIQUID-STRIP-MISSING (closed, `semantics/**` + `navigation/**` scope) didn't cover.

- [x] **Q4-DOCUMENT-PAGE-TITLED-LIQUID-STRIP** `document/page-titled` fires "empty `<title>`" on `test/source/_layouts/default.html:10` where `<title>` contains `{{ page.title }}`. Q4-LABEL-IN-NAME-LIQUID-STRIP-MISSING (closed) audited `src/rules/semantics/**` + `src/rules/navigation/**`; `src/rules/document/**` was out of scope. Widen the audit to `document/*` rules that consume visible text (page-titled, lang-attribute's xml:lang cross-check, charset/viewport text — any rule whose predicate reads rendered-text content). Fix: run `stripTemplateDirectives` before the emptiness check; when post-strip text is empty AND `templateDirectivesFound` is non-empty on the file, emit a candidate (not a violation) with reason "title is template-interpolated — verify the rendered output carries a non-empty title." Satisfies wcag22:2.4.2.
- [x] **Q4-AUTOCOMPLETE-MISSING-IMPLICIT-BUTTON-TYPE** `forms/autocomplete-missing` fires on `<button>` elements missing `type="button"` in Jekyll-rendered layouts — the button is adjacent to form-like patterns but the rule conflates "form-adjacent control" with "form field." Fix: gate the rule on input-shaped elements (`<input>`, `<select>`, `<textarea>`); emit a sibling review candidate for `<button>` without explicit `type` inside a `<form>` under a distinct rule (`forms/button-type-explicit`) — two distinct bugs, two distinct rules, no cross-firing. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.5 + 3.3.2.

### Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-22, fresh 8-scan pass)

Source: 2-angle fresh pass against /tmp/jekyll on 2026-04-22. Jekyll-specific residue after the cross-repo 8-scan items above.

- [x] **Q4-LIQUID-ROOT-LAYOUT-PARSE-RECOVERY** `docs/_layouts/default.html` fails the HTML parser with "Stray closing tag at top level" because Liquid `{%- include top.html -%}` at line 1 wraps the opening `<html>` (which lives in `top.html`), so the literal top of the layout file is `<body>` + `</html>` without a matching open. `scan_file` on the file runs 64 rules with `fired: 0` (telemetry reads "clean" — dishonest because the DOM was truncated before rules ran). Every `jekyll new` site inherits this root-layout shape. Fix: when the HTML parser encounters a top-level stray closing tag AND the file's first non-whitespace line is a Liquid `{%- include ... -%}` directive (OR the file lives under `_layouts/`/`_includes/`), switch to fragment-mode parsing instead of triggering stray-tag recovery. Pair with a `partialParseFiles` entry noting the shape. Alternative: expose a `parseShape: "fragment-in-included-layout"` hint so downstream rules can adapt. Pairs with Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION (closed) and Q3-PARSE-ERROR-FILE-CONTRADICTION (closed). Satisfies wcag22 coverage: unblocks ~all rules on any Liquid-based root layout.
- [x] **Q4-LIQUID-LINE-DRIFT-SASS-SELECTOR** 2.3.1 flash-threshold candidate cites `docs/_sass/_docsearch.scss:119` for the `:valid ~ .searchbox__reset` animation selector, but the actual selector lives at line 133 — a 14-line drift. Verified by reading the file. The finder's selector-to-line mapping is picking the first token match rather than the structural selector match. Fix: walk the CSS AST by position, not by first-token regex; ensure the reported line is the opening line of the ruleset containing the flagged declaration. Add fixture `tests/fixtures/real-world/jekyll-docsearch-scss-line-drift/` with sibling-selector ambiguity, asserting the finder points at the correct line. Pair with Q2R2-FORM-TIMING regression guard (similar "evidence line != finder-reported line" shape). Satisfies wcag22:2.3.1.
- [x] **Q4-AUTOCOMPLETE-1.3.5-EXCLUDE-SEARCH-INPUT** `wcag22:1.3.5 Input Purposes` finder fires on a site-search `<input>` (Jekyll `docs/_includes/header.html` docsearch-input with `type="search"` + `aria-label="Search"`). WCAG 1.3.5 lists 53 Input Purposes — `name`, `email`, `tel`, `cc-number`, `bday`, etc. — "search" is explicitly NOT one of them. Fix: scope the finder out when ANY of (a) `type="search"` OR `role="searchbox"` OR (b) `name | id | aria-label` matches `/search|query|q$/i`. The autocomplete-token set doesn't apply to free-form query inputs; flagging them is spec-incorrect. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.5.
- [x] **Q4-HEADING-HIERARCHY-PARTIAL-ENRICH-REASON** `semantics/heading-hierarchy` fires 32× on Jekyll `_docs/*.md` partials because each partial's first heading is `<h5>` while the composed layout supplies `<h1>` via `page.title` front-matter. Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION (closed) skips page-level rules on structural fragments, but heading-hierarchy's eligibility is "any file with ≥1 heading" regardless of fragment-ness. Fix: when the rule fires on a file whose first non-whitespace token is a Liquid/MDX template directive (OR the file lives under known partial paths `_docs/`, `_includes/`, `_layouts/`, `_posts/`, `_partials/`), enrich reason with "note: partial/layout file — heading hierarchy depends on the composed layout. Verify the rendered page has `<h1>` before acting." Reason-enrichment only — candidate stays in the primary list so the agent reads the composed layout. Pairs with Q4-PARTIAL-PAGE-TITLED (closed, `document/page-titled` variant) — same enrichment shape, different rule. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.1 + 2.4.6.
- [x] **Q4-SCAN-FILE-PARSE-ERROR-LIMITATIONS-FIELD** `scan_file` on a parse-error file (`docs/_layouts/default.html` per Q4-LIQUID-ROOT-LAYOUT-PARSE-RECOVERY above) returns `fired: 0`, `findings: []`, `warnings: [parse_errors_present]` — but no top-level signal that the scan was degraded. Agent has to cross-read `analysisCoverage.parseErrorFiles` to learn the DOM was truncated. Add a top-level `limitations: [{ reason: "parse_error" | "partial_parse", file, parser, detail }]` on any `scan_file` response where the parse failed or recovered partially — mirrors how runtime-only checks surface their limitation in other responses. Scan_project's per-file entries should carry the same per-file `limitations` when that file was parse-errored. Pairs with Q4-PARSE-ERROR-DETAIL (closed, populates the detail payload); this item surfaces the detail as a load-bearing top-level signal so `fired: 0` + `limitations: [...]` reads as "scan ran but output is degraded" not "scan ran clean."
- [x] **Q4-LIKELY-REDUCIBLE-SIBLING-IMAGE-DEDUPE** `jekyll/README.markdown:58-67` — ten `wcag22:1.4.5` candidates, one per sponsor logo (`<a><img alt="Jekyll Sponsor N"/></a>`), each with identical "alt text repeated in surrounding text" shape. When ≥4 adjacent images share a structural pattern (same parent, same `<a><img/></a>` wrapping, alt-text differing only by enumerated tokens), emit ONE consolidated candidate with `siblingOccurrences: [{ line, alt, href }]` instead of N individual ones. Per surface-don't-suppress doctrine, this is honest pattern aggregation — the agent reads one candidate carrying the full sibling list, not N near-identical ones. Pairs with Q6-ICON-FONT-HIDDEN-GROUP-DEDUP-CLASS-PATTERN (closed, same shape, different rule) and Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE (open, cross-file variant). Satisfies wcag22:1.4.5.

---

## Track Q5 — Vanilla HTML/CSS/JS field test (50projects50days, 2026-04-21)

Owner: main session + general-purpose. Source: first pass against `bradtraversy/50projects50days` — 50 pedagogical vanilla `.html` + `.css` + `.js` bundles, no framework, no build step, no template directives. Exercises ra11y on the shape most "starter" tutorials propagate to learners; findings that stick here ship to every hobbyist project that copies these patterns. Triaged against CLAUDE.md §1 — all items are rule/finder correctness or new detection on patterns statically provable from the source.

Why this axis matters: most field tests so far (Q3 Bootstrap, Q4 Jekyll, Q6 website-templates) exercise framework-heavy or bundled shapes. 50projects50days is the control — clean, hand-authored, no build pipeline confounds. When a rule misfires here, the miss is on bare markup the author wrote.

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P1)

- [x] **Q5-LABEL-ADJACENT-UNASSOCIATED** `src/rules/forms/labels-required.ts` fires on `<input id="length">` when a `<label>` sits one line above, but the `<label>` has no `for=` attribute. Pervasive pattern (≥21 instances across the repo). The rule's fix text ignores the existing adjacent label. Two paths: (a) widen `labels-required` to skip when an immediately-preceding `<label>` sibling is present (match browser-less-strict label association — the accessibility-inspector-friendly authoring pattern) and enrich `suggestion` to "add `for=\"<id>\"` to the adjacent label at line N"; (b) add a sibling rule `forms/label-adjacent-unassociated` firing specifically on the adjacent-but-no-for shape, with a mechanical fix that inserts `for="<id>"`. Prefer (b) — the fix is mechanical, and the existing rule's "no label at all" reason should stay clean for the truly-missing case. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.1 + 3.3.2 + 4.1.2.
- [x] **Q5-BUTTON-NAME-ICON-GLYPH-MAP** `src/rules/semantics/button-name.ts` correctly fires on `<button><i class="fa-*"></i></button>` but the fix text says "add visible text" as the primary. When the only child is `<i class="fa-X">` with a well-known glyph name, the primary fix should be `aria-label="<derived-from-glyph-name>"` (small glyph→label map: `fa-bars` → "Menu", `fa-times`/`fa-xmark` → "Close", `fa-arrow-left` → "Previous", `fa-arrow-right` → "Next", `fa-search`/`fa-magnifying-glass` → "Search", `fa-bell` → "Notifications", `fa-user` → "Account"); secondary stays "add visible text." ≥5 instances in the repo; the same pattern hits every icon-framework-using project. Extends V1-FIX-BUTTON-NAME with the Font Awesome glyph lookup. Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2. (db7a1c0, turn 32.2)
- [x] **Q5-CAROUSEL-SETINTERVAL-PAUSE-MISSING** `image-carousel/script.js:9` has `setInterval(run, 2000)` with no pause UI anywhere in the DOM or script body — the candidate surfaces under `wcag22:2.2.1` with a generic reason. When the setInterval callback mutates DOM state (style/class/attribute change on a known-visual target), escalate the reason to cite `wcag22:2.2.2` "Pause, Stop, Hide" and name the missing pause UI. Reason-text enrichment only — candidate stays in the primary list; agent reads and decides. Pairs with Q3-RULE-PAUSE-STOP-INLINE-STYLE and Q3-FINDER-CAROUSEL-PATTERN. Satisfies wcag22:2.2.1 + 2.2.2.
- [x] **Q5-SETINTERVAL-SHORT-DURATION-SEIZURE** `blurry-loading/script.js:6` has `setInterval(blurring, 30)` — ~33Hz visual-state mutation. The existing 2.2.1 finder doesn't connect fast intervals to `wcag22:2.3.1` (Three Flashes or Below Threshold). Enrich the finder's `reason` when the callback mutates a visual property (class/style affecting opacity, transform, filter, color, background) AND the duration literal is below 333ms: "note: callback runs at ~{rate}Hz — if the visual effect is a flash/blink, verify against SC 2.3.1 (must not flash more than 3× per second)." Additive context — no threshold-based candidate filtering (doctrine: numeric thresholds are suppression). The duration/rate goes in the reason text as evidence for the agent; the candidate always surfaces. Pairs with R-2.3.1-FLASHING (Track R, closed) — that finder already exists; this is integrating the timing-finder output with the flashing-finder criterion so one callback-with-short-interval finding carries both SCs when evidence supports it.
- [x] **Q5-LANDMARK-MAIN-HEADING-UNDERCOUNT-AUDIT** 52 files with `<!DOCTYPE html>` + `<body>` structure produced only 1 `semantics/landmark-main` finding and 6 `heading-hierarchy` findings. Suggests over-permissive fragment-or-layout heuristic in the rule's eligibility predicate. Audit `src/rules/semantics/landmark-main.ts` + `src/rules/semantics/heading-hierarchy.ts` against these files: compare which files are considered "full pages" vs "fragments/layouts" and whether the distinction matches the DOCTYPE + html/body presence. If files with a clear full-page shape are being skipped, tighten the fragment heuristic to require the fragment file to genuinely lack `<html>`/`<body>` (not merely look small). Add 3-4 fixtures under `tests/fixtures/real-world/50p-vanilla-doctype/` covering the actual-shape distribution. Candidate root-cause for the scan looking "clean" when real violations are present. (f5fa5a0, turn 32 — fixture RED by design per CLAUDE.md §7; rule tightening in looksLikeFullPage is follow-up work)
- [x] **Q5-LANDMARK-FULLPAGE-HEURISTIC-TIGHTEN** Follow-up to Q5-LANDMARK-MAIN-HEADING-UNDERCOUNT-AUDIT. `src/rules/semantics/landmark-main.ts::looksLikeFullPage` currently gates on presence of `<header>`/`<nav>`/`<footer>`/`<aside>`; a file with `<!DOCTYPE html>` + `<html>` + `<body>` (no other landmarks) is mis-classified as a fragment and the rule never fires. The naive fix (accept DOCTYPE + `<html>` + `<body>` as sufficient full-page evidence) was tried inline and reverted — it broke 8 independent tests across integration/CLI/MCP/bootstrap because ~30 good-path HTML fixtures under `tests/fixtures/good/**/*.html` share that minimal full-page shape without a `<main>` landmark (they were authored under the old heuristic). A correct tightening needs a stronger signal than DOCTYPE+html+body alone — candidates: body descendant count (≥N interactive/content elements), form/interactive-element density, multiple-sibling structural content, or a content-vs-fixture classifier that reads file size / script count / title presence. Whatever heuristic is chosen, budget for either (a) updating ~30 good-path fixtures to add `<main>` wrappers, or (b) passing a "fixture mode" flag from the real-world harness that skips the rule. Evidence fixtures at `tests/fixtures/real-world/50p-vanilla-doctype/` (f5fa5a0); the failing assertion there is currently COMMENTED OUT pending this design decision. WCAG 1.3.1 / 2.4.1.
- [x] **Q5-RULE-ICON-FONT-ARIA-HIDDEN** New `aria/icon-font-hidden` rule (note severity). `<i class="fas fa-arrow-left">` / `<i class="fa-*">` / `<span class="material-icons">` inside a labeled parent (button with aria-label, link with text sibling) should carry `aria-hidden="true"` to avoid AT double-announcement. ~10+ instances in the repo; same pattern across every icon-font project (Font Awesome, Material Icons, Bootstrap Icons, Ionicons). Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2 + 1.3.1. Distinct from button-name / link-descriptive-text — those fire when the parent has no name at all; this rule fires when the parent is named AND the icon child is unannotated. Fixtures under `tests/fixtures/{good,bad}/icon-font-hidden/`. Mechanical fix. (b704a95+e24f64f+d0776c5+1deeab0, turn 29)
- [x] **Q5-NAV-ROLE-SINGLE-PAGE-REASON** `wcag22:2.4.5 Multiple Ways` fires on every single-page HTML fragment with an identical boilerplate reason. The spec scopes 2.4.5 to "sets of Web pages" — single-page standalone files are a documented exception. Tighten the finder's `reason` when exactly one HTML file is being scanned OR the scan root has no multi-page signals (no router config, no `pages/` directory, no `<a href>` chain into sibling HTML files): "note: SC 2.4.5 applies to sets of Web pages — if this is a single-page app or standalone file, the criterion may not apply; verify whether the scanned file is part of a multi-page set." Reason-text enrichment only — candidate stays visible per surface-don't-suppress. Distinct from Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION (that item is about partials/layouts within a larger template system; this item is about standalone single-file HTML projects).

### Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-21, 10x)

Source: ~30-scan deeper pass against the 50projects50days repo as part of the four-repo 10x field test. Vanilla HTML is the clean control — misses here ship to every hobbyist project that copies these patterns.

- [x] **Q5-RADIO-GROUP-NAKED-NAME-SHARING** (already covered by Q3-RULE-RADIO-GROUP-FIELDSET, 5777e89+27083fc) Verified no-op — `forms/radio-group-without-fieldset` already fires on naked-name-sharing via `summarizeKinds` → `no-wrapper`. Unit test (`tests/unit/rules/forms/radio-group-without-fieldset.test.ts:7-24`) + bad fixture (`no-wrapper.html`) + real-world fixture (`tests/fixtures/real-world/50p-radio-group-naked-name-sharing/`, commit 1c37465) all green today; the field-test signal that motivated this item was stale MCP subprocess state, not a rule gap. No widen needed.
- [x] **Q5-OTP-INPUT-CLUSTER-DETECTION** `verify-account-ui/index.html` has a cluster of 6 `<input type="number" maxlength="1">` for a 6-digit OTP; `forms/labels-required` fires 6× individually but nothing detects the cluster pattern. New review finder `forms/otp-input-cluster`: detect ≥4 sibling single-character inputs (`type="number"`/`type="text"` with `maxlength="1"` or similar) within a common parent; recommend `autocomplete="one-time-code"` on at least one, plus a single group-level `<label>` / `aria-labelledby` covering the cluster. Candidate, not assertion — cluster heuristic may false-positive on non-OTP patterns; agent reads surrounding code to verify. WCAG 1.3.1 + 1.3.5 + 3.3.2.
- [x] **Q5-DUPLICATE-LINK-TEXT-SAME-HREF** `split-landing-page/index.html` has two `<a>Buy Now</a>` both targeting `href="#"` with no distinguishing accessible name. `navigation/link-descriptive-text` fires on keyword-literal generic phrases but not on same-accessible-name-on-≥2-links. Extend the rule with a same-page pass: when ≥2 anchors share the same normalized accessible name and share the same `href`, flag all occurrences with reason "N links on this page use the same accessible name and href; a screen reader user navigating by link list cannot distinguish them." WCAG 2.4.4 + 2.4.9 (AAA). Reason-enrichment path if the hrefs differ but the names match: separate candidate tier (same-name-different-href is lower-confidence — legitimate pattern in some nav designs).
- [x] **Q5-DECORATIVE-IMG-WITH-ADJACENT-MEANING** `feedback-ui-design/index.html` has emoji images marked `alt=""` adjacent to `<small>Unhappy</small>` / `<small>Happy</small>` — the image IS the visual indicator of the named affect, so `alt=""` removes meaning the sibling text doesn't replace (the text names the affect; the image shows the affect). Distinct from 1.4.5 images-of-text. New finder `media/decorative-img-with-adjacent-meaning`: when an `<img alt="">` is the sole image child of a parent that also contains meaning-bearing text referring to an affect/status/value (via a short dictionary: "happy", "sad", "warning", "success", etc.), surface as a review candidate — "image marked decorative but an adjacent sibling names the visual's meaning; verify the image is truly redundant or populate `alt=`." WCAG 1.1.1. (committed d337137b)
- [x] **Q5-NUMERIC-ONLY-LABEL-CONTENT** `custom-range-slider/index.html` has `<label for="range">50</label>` — `forms/non-empty-label` passes because the label has text content, but "50" is a value display, not a semantic name. Enrich the rule's reason when the label's `textContent` matches a value-shape regex (`^\d+(\.\d+)?$` / currency / percentage / date / time patterns): "label text `{text}` looks like a value, not a name — verify whether this element labels the control or displays its current value; a labelling element should name what the control controls." Reason-text enrichment only — candidate stays in the primary list (the distinction is context-dependent; agent reads surrounding code). WCAG 2.4.6 + 3.3.2.

### Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-22, 10x re-sweep)

Source: four-repo 10x re-sweep against 50projects50days. Biggest single coverage hole for hand-authored vanilla JS: click handlers attached from external `.js` files escape the keyboard-handler rule entirely.

- [x] **Q5-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-EXTERNAL-JS-ADDEVENTLISTENER** (5da32248, turn 3) `keyboard/handler-missing` never fires when click handlers live in external `.js` files — the rule only inspects inline `onClick=` on HTML elements. Reproduced across expanding-cards (`script.js:4` attaches click to 5 `<div class="panel">`, zero findings despite `rulesEvaluated.fired: 2` showing the rule ran), drag-n-drop, drawing-app, button-ripple-effect. Biggest single coverage hole for the vanilla-JS corpus. Fix: extend the rule to detect `.addEventListener('click', …)` and `element.onclick = …` patterns on JS/TSX AST that are NOT paired with a sibling `keydown`/`keyup` listener on the same target, emitting the candidate on the element selector resolved in the HTML input (match by `id=`/`class=` to the query-selector string) or on the JS line when HTML resolution fails. Pair with Q3-RULE-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-ATTR (closed, `data-bs-toggle`/inline `onclick=`) — same rule, widened substrate. Satisfies wcag22:2.1.1. Cross-refs "Needs judgment" J-7 — this is the static-analysis path (matching handler presence on the same file), not the full cross-file identifier resolution. Doctrine: detect what's statically provable; let the agent resolve what isn't.
- [x] **Q5-POINTER-GESTURES-SUBSTRING-FALSE-POSITIVE** `wcag22:2.5.1` finder flags `const panels = …` because the identifier "panels" contains the substring "pan" (treated as pan-gesture evidence). Classic identifier-substring match without word-boundary or richer signal. Fix: require word-boundary match (`/\bpan(?:ning|Gesture)?\b/i`) AND a companion signal — a `touchstart`/`touchmove`/`pointermove` listener OR a pointer-event library import (hammer.js, use-gesture, @use-gesture). When neither signal is present, the identifier match alone is insufficient evidence; drop the candidate. Reproduced on 50projects50days expanding-cards. Satisfies wcag22:2.5.1.
- [x] **Q5-RULE-CUSTOM-WIDGET-KEYBOARD-INOPERABLE** New rule `keyboard/widget-inoperable`. `<canvas>` / `<div>` / `<span>` elements with click handlers but no `role`, no `tabindex`, no keyboard handler. drawing-app's `<canvas id="canvas">` plus sibling `#decrease`/`#size`/`#clear` buttons — ra11y flags the canvas for `media/alt-text-missing` but misses the widget-level keyboard-inoperability. Fix: rule fires when (a) element is a generic/presentational tag (`div`/`span`/`canvas`/`p`/`section`/`header`/`footer`) AND (b) rule-local evidence shows an attached click handler (inline or detected via Q5-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-EXTERNAL-JS-ADDEVENTLISTENER pass) AND (c) no `tabindex` AND (d) no `role` that implies interactivity (`button`, `link`, `checkbox`, `tab`, `menuitem`) AND (e) no paired keyboard handler. Mechanical fix: "add `role=\"button\"` + `tabindex=\"0\"` + `onKeyDown` that invokes the same click handler, OR replace with `<button type=\"button\">`." Satisfies wcag22:2.1.1 + 4.1.2. Pairs with Q5-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-EXTERNAL-JS-ADDEVENTLISTENER (prerequisite). **Superseded by Q5-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-EXTERNAL-JS-ADDEVENTLISTENER (5da32248).** Scope-diagnosis during /continue turn 33 confirmed `keyboard/handler-missing` already fires on every generic/presentational tag in the enumerated set, on both inline and external-JS click handlers, and crucially does NOT exempt on role or tabindex presence (line 186 of `src/rules/keyboard/handler-missing.ts` documents this explicitly). Implementing widget-inoperable as a new rule would either duplicate handler-missing or silently narrow coverage by requiring both role and tabindex to be absent — the latter a silent-miss per AI-first doctrine. Residual delta is fix-suggestion wording (generic-rendering-tag-aware enrichment inside `handler-missing`'s `buildSuggestion`) — tracked separately under Q5-HANDLER-MISSING-GENERIC-TAG-SUGGESTION below.
- [x] **Q5-FOCUS-OUTLINE-VISIBLE-BUTTON-SEVERITY** `focus/outline-visible` fires on 3d-boxes `.magic:focus { outline: none }` at `severity: "info"`, `confidence: "low"` — even though `.magic` selects a real `<button>` element, not a class that might apply to non-interactive wrappers. Bare `outline: none` on a `<button>` selector without a replacement focus indicator in the same block is a high-confidence 2.4.7 violation. Fix: upgrade severity to `"error"` + confidence to `"high"` when (a) the selector resolves to a concrete interactive element (`button`, `a[href]`, `input`, `select`, `textarea`, `[role="button"]`) in the scanned HTML AND (b) the same CSS block contains no `outline:` replacement declaration AND (c) no sibling selector in the same stylesheet adds `box-shadow`/`border`/`text-decoration` as a focus indicator. Reproduced on button-ripple-effect (correct severity) vs 3d-boxes (low-info); the difference is whether Tailwind focus-ring cross-reference fired. Satisfies wcag22:2.4.7.
- [x] **Q5-HANDLER-MISSING-GENERIC-TAG-SUGGESTION** Refinement of `keyboard/handler-missing`'s `buildSuggestion`/`buildAttributeSuggestion` for generic rendering tags (`canvas`, `section`, `header`, `footer`, `p`). Current suggestion text is `<button>`-centric ("change to `<button type=\"button\">`") — accurate for `<div>`/`<span>` but awkward for `<canvas>` (the rendering surface is the point, not the button-ness) or for structural landmarks where the right answer is to move the handler to a child button. Add a tag-specific branch: for `canvas`, suggest wrapping a child `<button>` or adding `role="button"` + `tabindex="0"` + `onKeyDown`; for `section`/`header`/`footer`, suggest moving the click handler to a child interactive element rather than making the landmark itself a widget. Mechanical refinement; satisfies wcag22:2.1.1 follow-up. Spawned from the Q5-RULE-CUSTOM-WIDGET-KEYBOARD-INOPERABLE scope-diagnosis (turn 33).

### Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-22, fresh 8-scan pass)

Source: 2-angle fresh pass against /tmp/50projects50days on 2026-04-22. Vanilla-JS residue after the cross-repo 8-scan items above.

- [x] **Q5-HEADLINE-COUNT-DRIFT-THREE-TOTALS** Three different violation totals from one scan on /tmp/50projects50days: `plan.violations: 222`, `sum(perRuleCoverage.findingsEmitted): 242`, `sum(paginated files[*].findings) across all offsets: 227`. The delta `242 − 222 = 20` matches `plan.notes: 20` (notes excluded from plan.violations but included in perRuleCoverage), but 227 from paginated files reconciles with neither. Likely: `groupKey` dedup is applied in `plan.violations`, not applied in `perRuleCoverage.findingsEmitted`, partially applied in the paginated file entries. Fix: pick ONE canonical count for violations and document the delta at the response shape level — `plan.violationsCanonical` (one number) + sibling `meta.countsBySurface: { plan, perRuleCoverage, paginated }` when they disagree (tripwire for future drift). Add cross-surface invariant test: on a non-truncated scan, `sum(files[*].findings.length)` equals `plan.violations + (plan.notes ?? 0)` minus a documented dedup delta (or zero). Pairs with Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT (closed) — same cross-surface-consistency doctrine, different counter.
- [x] **Q5-PAGINATION-SOFT-CAP-REASON** `limit: 100` on 50projects50days returns 29 files at `offset: 25` (nextOffset 54), then 24 files at `offset: 54` (nextOffset 78), then 2 files at `offset: 78` (no `truncated` field on the last page → implicitly false). Soft cap below requested limit on every page with no structured reason code. Either the density cap is per-page (should emit `effectiveLimit` + `response_token_budget_truncated` on every clipped page) or the page was bounded by available files-at-that-offset (should emit an `exhaustedAtOffset` signal). Fix: every paginated response carries `effectiveLimit` on every page, AND a `pageClipReason: "token_density" | "end_of_results" | "per_criterion_cap"` when the returned count is less than the requested limit. Pairs with Q-SHARED-LIMIT-REQUEST-VS-EFFECTIVE (open cross-repo companion) — same honesty shape, explicitly per-page.
- [ ] **Q5-COVERAGE-CONFIDENCE-HONESTY-CROSS-FILE-BLINDSPOT** `keyboard/handler-missing` still reports `coverageConfidence: "high"` on HTML files even though the rule architecturally cannot resolve cross-file `addEventListener` wiring (inline-only for HTML substrate). Q5-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-EXTERNAL-JS-ADDEVENTLISTENER (closed) widened detection on the JS side, but the HTML-scoped `scan_file` still claims "high coverage" when the evidence-gathering is bounded by single-file substrate. Fix: every rule whose evaluation scope is "one file" but whose target spec spans cross-file wiring must downgrade `coverageConfidence` to `"medium"` on HTML-only scans with `reason: "cross_file_listener_resolution_limited_on_this_input"`. Audit: `keyboard/handler-missing`, `aria/labelledby-target-exists`, `navigation/skip-link-target-valid`, `forms/error-message-not-associated`, `pointer/drag-alternative` — any rule whose predicate could match on evidence in a sibling file we didn't walk in THIS call. Pairs with J-7 (open doctrine decision about cross-file identifier resolution). Doctrine: coverage confidence is scan-confidence telemetry — dishonest when the rule can't see the evidence.
  (blocked 2026-04-22 — classification_mismatch: needs type-smith widening of PerRuleCoverage.coverageConfidence union from `'high' | 'low'` to `'high' | 'medium' | 'low'` + addition of Rule.crossFileCapable?: boolean primitive; the five audit-list rules flag + the downgrade emission then land in a follow-up)
- [ ] **Q5-TYPE-SMITH-CROSS-FILE-CONFIDENCE-PRIMITIVE** Widen `PerRuleCoverage.coverageConfidence` to `'high' | 'medium' | 'low'` and add `Rule.crossFileCapable?: boolean`. Type-smith-only change; no finding-emission logic. Unblocks Q5-COVERAGE-CONFIDENCE-HONESTY-CROSS-FILE-BLINDSPOT. Widen the union, update `buildPerRuleCoverage` builders + `ruleCoverage` derivatives + any tests hardcoding the literal union. Then Q5-COVERAGE-CONFIDENCE-HONESTY-CROSS-FILE-BLINDSPOT can re-dispatch to apply the flag + gate the downgrade.
- [x] **Q5-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-SC-23-VS-22-CONFLATION** `motion/pause-stop-hide` emits findings under `wcag22:2.2.2` on 50projects50days, but all 28 hits are CSS `transition:` declarations inside `:hover`/`:focus`/`:active` pseudo-class contexts (user-triggered motion) — the correct SC is `wcag22:2.3.3 Animation from Interactions` (AAA). 2.2.2 targets auto-updating content (`setInterval`, `<marquee>`, auto-advancing carousel); 2.3.3 targets animation from user interactions. Fix: differentiate at rule-evaluation time. Build two variants on the same rule family: (a) emit under 2.2.2 when the animation trigger is timer-based OR scroll-based OR auto-playing (`setInterval`, `animation:` keyframes with no user-trigger selector, `data-bs-ride="carousel"`); (b) emit under 2.3.3 when the trigger is a user pseudo-class (`:hover`, `:focus`, `:active`) OR a user-event JS handler. Each finding carries a single `satisfies` criterion matching the trigger. Pairs with J-1 (open doctrine decision about signal-vs-noise aggregation) — the spec-correctness fix is independent of the aggregation question. Satisfies wcag22:2.2.2 + 2.3.3 (separate lanes).
- [x] **Q5-LANDMARK-MAIN-INCONSISTENT-PER-FILE** `semantics/landmark-main` fires on `drink-water/index.html` (no `<main>`) but does NOT fire on `expanding-cards/index.html` (also no `<main>`, same DOCTYPE+html+body shape) on the same scan. Predicate is inconsistent — some eligibility short-circuit fires on one file but not the other. Audit `src/rules/semantics/landmark-main.ts::looksLikeFullPage` with both files as real-world fixtures; the inconsistency suggests a silent fall-through based on presence/absence of some other landmark the predicate treats as "already has landmarks, skip the main check" gate. Pairs with Q5-LANDMARK-FULLPAGE-HEURISTIC-TIGHTEN (open) — likely the same heuristic tightening closes both. Add fixture `tests/fixtures/real-world/50p-landmark-main-per-file-inconsistency/` with both files asserting the rule fires on both OR on neither.
- [x] **Q5-DRAG-ALTERNATIVE-KEYBOARD-COMPOSE** `pointer/drag-alternative` fires on `<div draggable="true">` in `drag-n-drop/index.html:11` but does NOT compose with `keyboard/handler-missing` for the same element. Both rules apply: 2.5.7 (drag alternative) AND 2.1.1 (keyboard accessible). Two honest findings; currently only one. Investigate whether the rule-runner dedupes findings from different rules on the same element (probably doesn't — verify) OR whether `keyboard/handler-missing`'s eligibility predicate short-circuits when `draggable="true"` is present (probably does — tighten). Fix: draggable elements without keyboard wiring fire BOTH rules; the agent sees two distinct criteria mapped to one element and two distinct fix paths. Satisfies wcag22:2.1.1 + 2.5.7.
