## Track Q6 — Bulk-template catalog field test (website-templates, 2026-04-21)

Owner: main session + general-purpose. Source: first pass against a 40+ bulk-template catalog (`website-templates/` repo). Each template is a pre-built marketing/landing-page bundle shipping jQuery-era vendor CSS/JS (bootstrap.css, font-awesome.css, jquery.fancybox.pack.js) alongside authored HTML. Exercises ra11y at scale — the scan hit the MCP token ceiling, surfaced several rule misfires against vendor bundles, and exposed catalog-scale quality gaps in the proposed config + build-artifact warning path.

Why this axis matters: website-templates is the "buy a template kit" shape that a huge fraction of small-business and freelance sites ship. The vendor-CSS noise ratio and jQuery-era link antipatterns (`href="javascript:void(0)"`, icon-only social links) are not framework-authoring problems — they are the default ra11y-on-real-purchased-templates experience. When the token budget overruns, legitimate findings never reach the agent.

Three cross-repo items moved out: `PROPOSE_CONFIG_EMITS_ABSOLUTE_PATHS_AND_TRAILING_COMMA` folds into `Q-SHARED-PROPOSE-CONFIG-RELATIVE-PATHS` (Track Q3 cross-repo section). Two Q6 items consolidate under one roof each: Q6-1 + Q6-13 → `Q6-BUDGET-UNDER-VENDOR-NOISE`; Q6-10 + Q6-11 + Jekyll-4 share scope with Q4-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-REASON (cross-refs inline).

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P0 — response budget + signal-to-noise)

- [x] **Q6-BUDGET-UNDER-VENDOR-NOISE** Default `limit: 25` collapsed to `effectiveLimit: 1` via the density cap when vendor files dominated — `bootstrap.css` emitted 5,458 findings and `font-awesome.css` emitted 8,940 on a single scan, exhausting the 88K-char budget on one file. Two compounding gaps: (a) the token-density cap (V1-SIZE-RESPONSE-BUDGET-DENSITY-2) held the envelope but starved the response to one file's worth of findings; (b) a single criterion can carry 200+ review candidates on one file (see Q6-MEANINGFUL-SEQUENCE-UTILITY-CLASSES — `.order-0..13` utilities flagged 200+ times). Two additive fixes: per-file finding cap (hard limit ≈100 findings per file per response, with `perFileTruncated: true` + `findingsTotalInFile` on the file's entry) to prevent one file hoarding the budget; per-criterion byte cap on checklist responses (clip to first 3 candidates per criterion when response bytes exceed threshold, with `perCriterionClipped: true`). Both caps are additive labels — findings are not suppressed, they are paginated per scope. Consolidates Q6-1 + Q6-13. Pairs with `scanned_build_artifacts_present` — vendor bundles drive the budget overrun. Doctrine: pagination is honest; suppression is not.
- [x] **Q6-PLAN-TOTALFINDINGS-VIOLATIONS-VS-NOTES** `plan.totalFindings: 24772` buries `notes: 1079` under the same counter (violations: 23693). Composite headline count per `ai-first-consumer.md` §composite-headline-counts. Either rename to `plan.totalFindingsComposite` with a clearer breakdown elsewhere, or split into `plan.violationsCount` + `plan.notesCount` at the same level (matches the shape `ScanSubset` already carries post-V1-COMP-BOOTSTRAP-SCAN-TOTAL). The `--format agent` CLI summary already breaks out "N violations + M notes"; align the MCP plan counter with the same split.

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P1 — rule/finder correctness)

- [x] **Q6-CHECKLIST-IFRAME-FINDER-JS-STRING-LITERALS** Review candidates for 1.2.x / 4.1.2 iframe criteria include `jquery.fancybox.pack.js:4` with snippet `"<iframe id=\"fancybox-frame{rnd}\"..."`. The iframe-review finder line-scans `.js` files as if the literal string contents were HTML elements on the page. Restrict the finder to HTML-family inputs: `.html` / `.htm` / `.jsx` / `.tsx` / `.vue` / `.svelte` (the files ra11y actually parses as DOM). JS string literals containing `<iframe>` are not rendered iframes — they are library code building DOM at runtime; the agent reads the surrounding file to verify. Mirrors the TSX-text-corpus fix in V1-FP-IMAGES-OF-TEXT-SVG-DATA-URI's shape: the text-matching pass must run on DOM-origin text only, not on string-literal payloads inside source code.
- [x] **Q6-MEANINGFUL-SEQUENCE-UTILITY-CLASSES** 1.3.2 review candidates fire on every `.order-0` .. `.order-13` utility-class selector in `bootstrap.css` / authored compiled CSS — 14 selectors × ~40 templates = ~560 candidates per scan, all pointing at the same Bootstrap utility definitions rather than at any consumer. Finder must operate on HTML/JSX *consumers* that apply `class="order-N"`, not on the CSS selector definition site. Tighten: lift the 1.3.2 finder from the CSS-selector scope to the HTML/JSX usage scope (match `class="...order-N..."` / `className="...order-N..."` on elements); silence on the CSS definition when no consumer in the scan applies the class. When the finder *does* hit a CSS-only definition, enrich the reason with "note: utility-class definition; verify consumers apply this class intentionally." Doctrine: the finder's premise (sequence-dependent visual order) is real; the current location (CSS definition) is wrong.
- [x] **Q6-RULE-HREF-JAVASCRIPT-VOID** New `navigation/href-javascript-void` rule. 120 occurrences of `<a href="javascript:void(0)">` across 10 sampled files; zero findings today. Covers `href="javascript:void(0)"`, `href="javascript:;"`, and `href="#"` when no click-handler delegation is detectable (e.g. no matching jQuery `.on("click")` selector, no event delegation parent). All three forms are `<button>` roles masquerading as links; AT announces "link," keyboard Tab lands with no navigation. Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2 + 2.1.1. Mechanical fix: "change `<a href=\"javascript:void(0)\">` to `<button type=\"button\">` — this control mutates state, not navigates." Fixtures under `tests/fixtures/{good,bad}/href-javascript-void/`. (0b942fc, turn 30)
- [x] **Q6-LINK-ICON-ONLY-ACCNAME** `src/rules/navigation/link-descriptive-text.ts` does not flag `<a><i class="fa fa-twitter"></i></a>` — the anchor has no text, no `aria-label`, no labeling child. Extend the rule to compute the full accessible name: ignore presentational descendants (`<i class="fa-*">`, `<span class="material-icons">`, decorative `<img role="presentation">`), then flag when the computed name is empty. Pairs with Q5-RULE-ICON-FONT-ARIA-HIDDEN — that rule enriches labeled parents with icon children; this rule flags unlabeled parents with only icon children. Satisfies wcag22:2.4.4 + 4.1.2. (2ce309f+8932f04+481c86f, turn 31)
- [x] **Q6-AUTODETECT-WRAPPERS-LEAKS-JS-IDENTIFIER-NOISE** `autoDetectWrappers: true` on the website-templates scan returned `opaqueCustomComponentNames: ["AG.y","B","H.length","J","J.length","Math.abs"]`. The extractor walks `.js` files grepping for identifier expressions; single-letter variables, member-access expressions (`J.length`), and built-ins (`Math.abs`) are not component names. Restrict extraction to JSX/TSX element tag positions only — `<ComponentName ... />` / `<ComponentName>...</ComponentName>` where the tag name starts with an uppercase letter and is a bare identifier (no `.`, no single-char unless explicitly allowlisted). Skip the `.js` extension entirely for JSX-tag harvesting (plain `.js` has no JSX syntax). Paired with Q3-OPAQUE-COMPONENTS-TOP-EMPTY-DISHONEST under opaque-component quality, but the extraction-source filter is the root-cause fix.
- [x] **Q6-PLACEHOLDER-IN-FIX-SUGGESTION** Minor refinement on `forms/labels-required`: when the rule fires AND the offending `<input>` has a `placeholder="..."` attribute, include the placeholder verbatim in the fix suggestion ("`<label for=\"email\">Email address</label>` — placeholder text `\"Enter email\"` may carry the intent; verify it's accurate before using as label copy"). Pairs with Q3-RULE-PLACEHOLDER-AS-LABEL (new rule for placeholder-as-only-label antipattern) — this item is the fix-suggestion enrichment for the already-firing `labels-required` rule when the placeholder is additive evidence for the correct label text. Extends V1-FIX-NON-EMPTY-LABEL's placeholder path.

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P2 — build-artifact rollup + signal routing)

- [x] **Q6-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-GROUP-BY-BASENAME** Companion to Q4-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-REASON. When `scannedBuildArtifacts` surfaces 301 entries (bootstrap.css: 45, font-awesome.css: 80, etc.), the flat list is unactionable — the agent sees 301 individual paths and cannot tell that one `exclude` glob covers most of them. Group by basename with a suggested glob: `scannedBuildArtifacts: { grouped: [{ basename, count, pathHint, suggestedGlob }], ungrouped: [paths] }`. `suggestedGlob` is inline-ready for `propose_config`. Additive shape over the existing flat `scannedBuildArtifacts: [paths]` array (consumers that iterate the flat list still work; the grouped form is a sibling field). Pairs with Q-SHARED-PROPOSE-CONFIG-RELATIVE-PATHS — collapsing 301 paths to ~5 glob patterns.
- [x] **Q6-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-PER-FILE-AGGREGATION** `motion/pause-stop-hide` produced 8,940 findings (36% of the scan's total) against vendor `bootstrap.css` alone. Every CSS animation keyframe / transition declaration is technically a 2.2.2 concern; vendor bundles carry hundreds. When a single rule fires >N× in one file AND that file is in `scannedBuildArtifacts`, add `perRuleCoverage[ruleId].vendorConcentration?: { file, count }` pointing at the densest vendor file with its total count. Findings still emit individually under `files[]` per surface-don't-suppress (pairs with V1-NOISE-RULE-PER-FILE-ROLLUP, which rolls up the same signal for non-vendor files above a concentration threshold). Additive label — zero information loss, one more field for the agent's triage. The V1 item handled the non-vendor case; this item extends the same shape to vendor bundles explicitly so the agent sees "most of this rule's fire is one vendor file" without having to sort findings themselves.
- [x] **Q6-NEXTSTEP-AVOIDS-VENDOR-CSS** `nextStepStructured.file` pointed at `bootstrap.css:365` — vendor CSS is rarely the file the agent should act on first. When the top-ranked violation or next-step target sits in `scannedBuildArtifacts`, rank the suggestion toward a non-vendor file with a finding of the same rule family (if one exists in the same response); fall back to the vendor file only when every violation sits in vendor code. Doctrine-adjacent to "surface-don't-suppress": the vendor finding stays in `files[]`, but the agent's first action should land where they can actually edit. Reason-text when this rerouting applies: "note: highest-severity finding in this scan is in `vendor/bootstrap.css`; next-step points at `authored/site.css` where a same-family fix is applicable." Additive — when no rerouting applies, `nextStepStructured` behaves exactly as today.

### Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-21, 10x)

Source: 15-scan deeper pass against the website-templates repo as part of the four-repo 10x field test. 3,973 files / 24,772 findings / 20.5 s internal scan time (see "Needs judgment" section below for the performance-budget call). Items here are residue after the cross-repo Q-SHARED items and the existing Q6 caps (Q6-BUDGET-UNDER-VENDOR-NOISE et al.) are addressed.

- [x] **Q6-RULE-ARIA-REF-MISMATCH-WIDEN-ATTRS** Website-templates `coffee-shop/index.html:162` has `aria-labelledby="myModalLabel"` pointing at an id that does not exist anywhere in the document. Q3-RULE-LABELLEDBY-TARGET (closed turn 24) covers `aria-labelledby` / `aria-describedby` / `aria-controls` but did NOT fire here — first investigate whether this is rule-registry drift (see Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT); once the registry is fixed and the rule fires, widen coverage to all eight ARIA ID-ref attributes: `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-controls`, `aria-owns`, `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-errormessage`, `aria-flowto`, `aria-details`. Deterministic, zero-false-positive check. Fixtures under `tests/fixtures/{good,bad}/aria-ref-widen/` covering each of the eight attributes. WCAG 4.1.2.
- [x] **Q6-RULE-INVALID-ID-SHAPE** Website-templates `agile-agency/index.html:60` has `id="#top"` — the `#` is an invalid id-start character per HTML-living-standard (ids may not contain whitespace but are otherwise any non-empty string; however, `id="#top"` is the author confusing id syntax with href-fragment syntax). `parsing/duplicate-id` only fires on duplicates, not on malformed shapes. Add a companion `parsing/invalid-id-shape` rule (or extend duplicate-id): flag ids that start with `#`, contain whitespace, are empty-string, or collide with well-known CSS-escape-required characters when used unescaped. Fixtures under `tests/fixtures/{good,bad}/invalid-id-shape/`. WCAG 4.1.1 (parsing) — note: WCAG 2.2 removed 4.1.1 as a testable SC; keep the rule under `parsing/` as best-practice with `satisfies: [wcag21:4.1.1]` (parsing-failure in WCAG 2.1) only, not 2.2.
- [x] **Q6-ICON-FONT-LABELED-PARENT-REGRESSION** Q5-RULE-ICON-FONT-ARIA-HIDDEN (closed turn 29) should fire on `<button><i class="fa-bars"></i></button>` patterns in coffee-shop (lines 59, 82, 86), but the rule emitted zero findings. Investigate: (a) registry drift (covered by Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT); (b) the rule's "labeled parent" predicate may not cover `<button>` wrapping `<i>` without any other child text when the parent's accessible name comes solely from an `aria-label` rather than a text node — re-read the rule and add `tests/fixtures/real-world/bulk-template-icon-button/` asserting the rule fires. If (b), widen the labeled-parent signal to include `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby` / `aria-describedby` on the parent. WCAG 4.1.2 + 1.3.1.
- [x] **Q6-ANCHOR-ICON-ONLY-NAME-REGRESSION** Q6-LINK-ICON-ONLY-ACCNAME (closed turn 31) should fire on `<a href="#home"><img src="logo.png" alt="..."/></a>` patterns in coffee-shop (`index.html:35`), but the deeper scan emitted `media/alt-text-missing` at the `<img>` level only — not a secondary finding at the anchor level for missing accessible name. When the only child of an `<a>` is a decorative/presentational element (icon `<i>`, `<img role="presentation">`, `<svg aria-hidden>`) AND the anchor has no `aria-label`, the anchor has no accessible name regardless of the child's alt. Investigate: registry drift (see Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT) vs rule predicate gap (img with non-empty alt counted as giving the anchor a name, masking the distinction between "anchor name = alt" vs "anchor unnamed"). WCAG 2.4.4 + 4.1.2.
- [x] **Q6-MINIFIED-FILE-SNIPPET-COLUMN-ENRICHMENT** Website-templates cited `bootstrap.min.js:6` and `col 1` eight times for setTimeout review candidates on a 6-line minified file — every match points at the same line/col because the entire file is one line. The agent cannot locate the specific call site. When the cited file matches minified heuristics (single line > 1000 chars, `.min.` infix, sibling sourcemap present), enrich `reason` with (a) byte-column offset within the single line (so `col 47201` disambiguates multiple matches), (b) a pointer to the unminified sibling if one exists (`authored: "src/bootstrap.js"` when `src/bootstrap.js` exists alongside `dist/bootstrap.min.js`), and (c) the ~80-char context window around the match. Additive reason enrichment — findings remain in `files[]`; the agent gets a usable pointer. Doctrine: "The tool's job is to point" — a line:col pointer to a minified file that resolves to "the whole file" is a dishonest pointer. Shipped (a) byte col + (c) ~80-char context window via `src/review/finders/timing-minified.ts`; sibling resolution (b) dropped per backlog "if sibling-resolution gets expensive, skip" clause — node-scoped finders can't see other files without a scope widening that's out of proportion with the pointer-disambiguation value.
- [x] **Q6-SCSS-PARSING-DISCLOSURE** Website-templates `filesByExtension` reports 584 `.scss` files, but `perRuleCoverage.filesEvaluated` numbers don't cleanly account for them (`1637 ≠ 1053 + 584`). Response does not disclose whether `.scss` is parsed as CSS (via `extensionMatches` alias) or skipped. Add `analysisCoverage.sassParsingMode: "css-alias" | "skipped" | "native"` (or equivalent) so the agent knows whether a `.scss`-heavy repo was fully inspected by CSS-family rules. Alternative: enrich `filesByExtension` entries with a `parsedAs` field (`"css"` / `"skipped"` / `"native"`). Doctrine: "Ambiguous field shapes are dishonest" — a filesByExtension count that doesn't match the per-rule file count forces the agent to guess coverage.

### Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-22, 10x re-sweep)

Source: four-repo 10x re-sweep against a website-templates subset-B sample (delite-music, sb-admin, sb-admin-2, frames-corporate, vone). Items here are residue after the cross-repo Q-SHARED additions above.

- [x] **Q6-RULE-LABEL-ADJACENT-UNASSOCIATED-NO-IDS** `forms/label-adjacent-unassociated` never fires on the canonical orphan-label shape `<label>Text Input</label><input class="form-control">` — 12 instances at `sb-admin/forms.html:142,148,158,163,230-ish` (and similar counts across other Bootstrap-templated forms). The rule's predicate requires the `<input>` to already carry an `id` so it can match against the absent `for=`; real templates routinely omit the id entirely. Without it, `forms/labels-required` takes over with generic guidance and the richer "add `for=\"<id>\"` + add `id=\"<id>\"`" mechanical pair never surfaces. Fix: adjacency predicate fires on `<label>` immediately preceding an input-shaped element regardless of whether either carries an `id`; mechanical fix proposes a synthesized id and both attributes in one edit. Most common form-a11y bug in the wild; rule misses it. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.1 + 3.3.2 + 4.1.2. Cross-refs Q5-LABEL-ADJACENT-UNASSOCIATED (closed, different shape) and Q4-LABELS-REQUIRED-VS-LABEL-ADJACENT-OVERLAP (open dedup policy).
- [x] **Q6-FINDINGID-COLLISION-SAMEFILE-SAMELINE** `navigation/link-descriptive-text` emits both "not descriptive" AND "N duplicates" findings on the same anchor elements at the same file:line — 8 findings for 4 links on `super-simple-site/index.html:14-17`, with same `findingId` hash (`87dd7b97d2eb`) appearing twice at L14. Both findings are legitimate (the rule satisfies 2.4.4 for descriptiveness AND 2.4.9 for duplicate names), but the `findingId` collision breaks the agent's dedup and the suppress-by-findingId flow. Root cause: the hash input excludes the rule's sub-variant discriminator ("not descriptive" vs "duplicates"). Fix: include the variant key (e.g. `reasonKind`/`messageKey`) in the `findingId` hash computation in `src/engine/rule-runner.ts` so two findings with distinct sub-variants get distinct ids. Add a cross-invariant test: `new Set(violations.map(v => v.findingId)).size === violations.length` per file-level response. Doctrine: the `findingId` is a load-bearing primary key for the suppress + dedup flows.
- [x] **Q6-ICON-FONT-HIDDEN-GROUP-DEDUP-CLASS-PATTERN** `aria/icon-font-hidden` emits 180 findings on sb-admin — the bulk are identical `<i class="fa fa-*">` patterns inside sibling buttons. `groupKey` dedup operates at the per-element level but doesn't collapse structurally-identical occurrences within one file. Per surface-don't-suppress, the candidates stay visible — but a per-site concentration summary (`perRuleCoverage[ruleId].concentration: { file, count, classPattern }`) would let the agent triage 180 into "one fix pattern, applied site-wide." Fix: compute per-file-per-class-pattern concentration on rules that fire >N× on one file with identical `class` evidence; emit `concentration` as a meta field, not a filter. Pairs with V1-NOISE-RULE-PER-FILE-ROLLUP (same shape, different rule family). Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2 (unchanged scope).
- [x] **Q6-CONFIG-CONTEXT-TRIPLE-READOUT** Three fields re-emit the same "no config found" signal: `configSource: null`, `configSearchedFrom: "<path>"`, `configNote: "No ra11y.config found at … — using built-in defaults"` (200-char boilerplate). All three appear together on every response when no config exists; `configNote` duplicates what the warning + `configSource: null` already say, and the 200-char repetition inflates response size on tiny-repo scans. Fix: collapse to `configSource: null` (present on every response) + `configSearchedFrom: <path>` (present-when-meaningful — omit when identical to cwd); drop `configNote` as a user-facing field, or demote to `_meta.configNote` for debugging only. Pairs with Q-SHARED-NO-CONFIG-WARNING-TINY-REPO (whether the warning itself should fire). Doctrine: present-when-meaningful.

### Deeper-pass additions (2026-04-22, fresh 8-scan pass)

Source: 2-angle fresh pass against /tmp/website-templates on 2026-04-22. Catalog-scale residue after the cross-repo 8-scan items above. Several items are regression-audits on previously-closed closures whose symptoms still reproduce — file them as investigate-then-extend rather than as clean new bugs.

- [x] **Q6-CONTRAST-INLINE-STYLE-REGRESSION-AUDIT** Evidence: `vteam.../index.html:89 <strong style="color:#FFF317">` (1.17:1 yellow-on-white), `street-life/index.html:13 <h1>Street <span style="color:#FFEA00">Life</span></h1>` (1.25:1). `perRuleCoverage.remediation` still says `"add CSS source files or additionalPaths"` — wrong guidance when the color lives in `style=""`. `Q-SHARED-INLINE-STYLE-CONTRAST` (marked [x]) prescribed an inline-style evaluation path, but symptoms reproduce on this scan. Three possibilities: (1) closure shipped but this subprocess is serving a pre-closure bundle (check Q3-MCP-RESTART-HINT-SUBPROCESS-RACE), (2) closure was narrower than the spec (only `<style>` blocks, not `style=""` attrs), (3) closure covered HTML scans but per-rule coverage text wasn't updated to match. Investigation path: `git log src/rules/contrast/minimum.ts` + `git log src/input/parsers/html.ts`; confirm against a fresh-subprocess `scan_file` on `vteam.../index.html`. Fix the smaller delta revealed. Add a real-world fixture `tests/fixtures/real-world/inline-style-contrast-yellow-on-white/` asserting the rule fires on `<strong style="color:#FFF317">Text</strong>`. Satisfies wcag22:1.4.3.
- [x] **Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE** No cross-template pattern dedupe on website-templates. `<button class="navbar-toggle" data-toggle="collapse">` appears in ≥8 sibling template dirs and emits 8 distinct `findingId`/`groupKey` values per rule per template. At 174 templates × 3 rules firing on this Bootstrap vendor-snippet = ~500 findings from one canonical pattern copied across the catalog. Agents have no affordance for "dismiss this pattern across all siblings" — each finding gets a distinct id. Fix: add `patternId` as a sibling field on each finding, computed as `hash(ruleId + canonicalize(snippet))` where canonicalize strips attribute order, trivial whitespace, unique id/class tokens, and template/SSG interpolation placeholders. Agents can bulk-dismiss or bulk-fix by `patternId` across sibling template copies. Surface-don't-suppress: findings still appear individually; `patternId` is additive affordance, not a filter. Pairs with Q6-ICON-FONT-HIDDEN-GROUP-DEDUP-CLASS-PATTERN (closed, single-file pattern rollup) — this item is the cross-file sibling. Satisfies wcag22 all.
- [x] **Q6-CATALOG-REPO-SIBLING-HINT** `scan_project` on /tmp/website-templates (174 top-level sibling dirs, each carrying its own `index.html` + `css/` + `js/`) treats the whole tree as one flat repo — `detectedFramework: "jekyll"`, `parseErrorFileCount: 501`, `opaqueCustomComponentNames: ["AG.y","B",...]` are all catalog-scale fallout. Angle-2 confirmed per-subdir scans DO recover signal (per-template `rulesFired`, `parseErrorFileCount` 2-6 instead of 501, `opaqueCustomComponentNames` collapses to 0-1). Add a meta-signal: when the root has ≥N (say 5) top-level siblings each carrying a recognizable site-entry-point shape (`index.html` + sibling `css/` or `images/` or `assets/`), emit `meta.catalogHint: { topLevelSiblings: N, exampleSiblings: [names] }` + a `nextStep` suggestion: "this looks like a catalog of N sibling sites; consider `scan_project({ cwd: \"<subdir>\" })` for per-site signal." Pairs with Q6-NEXTSTEP-AVOIDS-VENDOR-CSS (closed) — same routing shape, different trigger. Doctrine: "One tool call should answer 'what next?'" — catalog-shape detection gives the agent the right second call.
- [x] **Q6-FRAMEWORK-DETECT-JEKYLL-FALSE-POSITIVE** `meta.detectedFramework: "jekyll"` fires on /tmp/website-templates because a stray `_config.yml` at the root triggers Jekyll detection. No `_layouts/`, no `_includes/`, no Gemfile, no `Gemfile.lock`. Single-filename evidence is insufficient. Fix (tighten heuristic in `src/mcp/framework-detection.ts` or equivalent): Jekyll detection requires `_config.yml` AND at least one of (a) `_layouts/` dir, (b) `_includes/` dir, (c) a Gemfile mentioning `jekyll`, (d) a top-level `_posts/` or `_drafts/` dir. When only `_config.yml` is present, emit `frameworkCandidate: "jekyll" (weak)` as a candidate rather than a confident detection, OR omit the detection entirely and let the agent inspect. Per-subdir scans (angle-2 confirmed) correctly emit `detectedFramework: null` because no `_config.yml` is present in the subdirs. Pairs with Q3-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-STARLIKE-MISDETECT (closed) — same class of over-confident detection from weak evidence.
- [x] **Q6-OPAQUE-COMPONENTS-MINIFIED-JS-REGRESSION** (e8e1a7d7) `opaqueCustomComponentNames: ["AG.y","B","H.length","J","J.length","Math.abs"]` on /tmp/website-templates full scan. `Q6-AUTODETECT-WRAPPERS-LEAKS-JS-IDENTIFIER-NOISE` (marked [x]) restricted wrapper extraction to JSX/TSX tag positions with active unit-test coverage (`tests/unit/mcp/analysis-coverage.test.ts`), but symptoms reproduce. Root cause is most likely the **reopened** Q-SHARED-TSX-PARSER-FALSE-JSX-CONTEXTS (now `[~]` above): 501 `.js` files routed through the TSX fallback error-parse, contributing fake tag positions to the wrapper extractor that the JSX-tag-position filter then accepts as legitimate. Two-layer fix: (a) the upstream TSX-parser fix stops `.js` from entering JSX mode at all — closes most of the bleed; (b) belt-and-braces, wrapper extraction must skip files in `parseErrorFiles` AND `scannedBuildArtifacts` regardless of how they got there. Add an invariant test: `for every name in opaqueCustomComponentNames, the source file must not be in parseErrorFiles`. Pairs with Q3-OPAQUE-COMPONENTS-TOP-EMPTY-DISHONEST (closed, single-file shape) and Q3-BOOTSTRAP-WRAPPER-DETECT-EMPTY-REASON (closed, MDX/Astro case). Track this item against (b) specifically; (a) lands via the parent TSX item.
- [x] **Q6-CONTRAST-VENDOR-CSS-CROSS-FILE-DEDUPE** `contrast/minimum` fires 5458× across the website-templates scan, with the vast majority on vendor `bootstrap.css` / `animate.css` duplicated across 100+ template subdirs. One fix to `bootstrap.css` in one subdir does not propagate; the agent has no affordance to say "this is the same finding as N siblings." Add per-basename cross-file dedupe: when the same `(selector, declaration)` pair in files sharing the same basename (`bootstrap.css`) fires the same rule across sibling dirs, collapse to one canonical finding with `vendorOccurrences: [{ path, line }]` sibling list. Pairs with Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE (open, element-level) and Q6-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-GROUP-BY-BASENAME (closed, meta-level). Surface-don't-suppress: findings still enumerable via the occurrences list; the headline is deduped. Satisfies wcag22:1.4.3.
- [x] **Q6-SUGGEST-FIX-MECHANICAL-VS-GUIDANCE-DRIFT** `suggest_fix` returns `kind: "guidance"` on `navigation/href-javascript-void` findings on website-templates despite the scan's `plan.fixesByClass.mechanical` counting them as mechanical (and the rule's docs advertise a trivial `<button type="button">` replacement). Cross-surface contradiction: the plan says "mechanical," the fix call says "guidance." Root cause candidates: (1) `suggest_fix` degrades mechanical → guidance when the specific finding's replacement text depends on surrounding context it can't statically resolve; (2) the fix handler reads a different class lookup than the plan. Fix: when `suggest_fix(ruleId, file, line)` can produce a well-formed `newText` for a rule the scan-plan counts as mechanical, return `kind: "edit"` with populated `newText`; when the context makes the replacement ambiguous, emit `kind: "guidance"` BUT also emit `meta.mechanicalInPrinciple: true` so the agent knows the rule family supports a mechanical path. Pairs with Q-SHARED-SUGGEST-FIX-GUIDANCE-PRIMARY (closed) — different fix call shape. Doctrine: cross-surface consistency.

### Considered and rejected (per CLAUDE.md §1)

- **Auto-exclude `bootstrap.css` / `font-awesome.css` / `jquery*.js` via `DEFAULT_EXCLUDED_PATTERNS`** → rejected per §1 "Default-exclude globs are suppression too." Vendor-bundle findings ARE real findings — contrast in bootstrap.css is a real contrast issue on the rendered page, just not one the consumer edits. The existing `scannedBuildArtifacts` label + proposed rollups (Q6-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-GROUP-BY-BASENAME + Q6-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-PER-FILE-AGGREGATION) give the agent the signal without discarding the findings. Pairing with the build-artifact-aware next-step rerouting (Q6-NEXTSTEP-AVOIDS-VENDOR-CSS) closes the "agent lands on unactionable file" failure mode.
- **Default-downgrade vendor findings to `severity: "note"`** → rejected per §1 "Don't downgrade priority to hide things." Severity is for sorting; a downgrade hides real contrast violations from agents that filter by severity. The `scannedBuildArtifacts` membership is the honest label — agents decide triage; the tool does not pre-decide severity.

---

## Needs judgment — 2026-04-21 field test

Source: aggregated four-repo 10x deeper field test (bootstrap, jekyll, 50projects50days, website-templates). Items below are open strategy/doctrine questions surfaced during triage that need a user-level decision before the corresponding backlog work can land. They are not tracked as dispatchable work — they are blockers on specific items above. Once answered, each resolves into an existing or new backlog item.

- **J-1 (pause-stop-hide signal-vs-noise strategy).** `motion/pause-stop-hide` produced 8,940 findings on website-templates and 55 on 50projects50days. Most are user-triggered hover/focus transitions, not the auto-playing motion WCAG 2.2.2 targets. Current reason text doesn't distinguish. Is this (a) over-surface that needs reason-text enrichment only ("auto-playing?" / "user-triggered?" signal, let the agent decide), (b) spec-correctness matter where the rule should narrow to auto-playing-only, (c) both? Surface-don't-suppress argues (a); narrowing risks silent-miss on genuine 2.2.2 auto-playing violations. Blocks Q6-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-PER-FILE-AGGREGATION scoping decision.

- **J-2 (vendor CSS noise, primary mitigation choice).** Website-templates: 69% of 24,772 findings = 3 rules firing on vendor CSS. Doctrine forbids default-excluding vendor paths. Primary mitigation options: (a) fix the `scannedBuildArtifacts` classifier so agents can filter by it with confidence, (b) add a per-file finding cap (Q6-BUDGET-UNDER-VENDOR-NOISE), (c) both, (d) something else (e.g., surface rule-per-file concentration aggressively and trust the agent to route). Currently Q6-BUDGET and Q6-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-GROUP-BY-BASENAME are both open; the question is whether they land independently or one supersedes the other. Blocks prioritization of both items.

- **J-3 (plan.totalFindings: rename vs remove).** Every `scan_project` ships a top-level `plan.totalFindings: N` that sums violations + notes. Doctrine forbids composite headline counts. Agents may already depend on the field for aggregation. Remove entirely (breaking — v1.0 gate) or rename to `plan.totalFindingsComposite` with explicit "sum of violations+notes, prefer the split" prose (additive, non-breaking)? Blocks Q6-PLAN-TOTALFINDINGS-VIOLATIONS-VS-NOTES implementation shape.

- **J-4 (rulesEvaluated definition: loaded-vs-fired).** `rulesEvaluated` disagrees cross-surface on the same cwd: `scan_project: 53` vs `propose_config: 54` vs `list_rules: 54` (50projects50days). Likely cause: JSX/TSX-only rule counted as "evaluated on 0 applicable files" differs by tool. Which is correct: "evaluated" = "the rule ran on 0 applicable files" (54) or "the rule didn't run because no applicable files" (53)? Either is honest; they must agree cross-surface. Blocks Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT shape.

- **J-5 (likelyIrrelevant bucket: parse-coverage caveat).** `likelyIrrelevant` for 1.2.x is honest on website-templates ("no video/audio in scanned files"). On Jekyll it's technically honest but 307 `.md`/`.markdown` files were never parsed — under the doctrine rule that bucket labels must be provable, is "no video in scanned files" sufficient when the scan missed the content layer? Options: (a) enrich `likelyIrrelevant` reason with the skipped-content caveat automatically (covered by Q4-RELEVANCE-REASON-PARSE-COVERAGE-CAVEAT), (b) consider it the agent's job to reconcile `likelyIrrelevant` with `extensions_skipped_no_parser` separately, (c) suppress `likelyIrrelevant` entirely when content extensions are skipped. Blocks Q4-RELEVANCE-REASON-PARSE-COVERAGE-CAVEAT scoping.

- **J-6 (performance budget at 4k-file vendor-heavy scale).** Website-templates full-scan ran 20.5 s internal (~32 s wall-clock) against 3,973 files — 1.7× over linear extrapolation of the 1000-file 3 s budget. Options: (a) add a new bench scenario at 3-4k vendor-heavy files with an explicit budget, (b) document a "precommit use not supported beyond N files" scope limit, (c) default to `--changed`-only discovery on repos > 1000 files with a `largeRepoHint` warning, (d) accept the slowdown and document it. Blocks performance-track decisions + CLAUDE.md §11 table update.

- **J-7 (static-analysis limit vs cross-file handler resolution).** `<a href="#">` / `<li>`-acting-as-tab / `<div>`-acting-as-button patterns with click handlers in separate `.js` files (50projects50days + website-templates common). `navigation/href-javascript-void` (registry-drift notwithstanding) catches raw `href="#"`; the broader "custom interactive widget with JS-only handler" class requires cross-file resolution. Read sibling `.js` files to resolve handlers (expensive, cross-file complexity, risks wrong-identifier matches) OR accept as an inherent static-analysis limitation and document it in `limitations`? Doctrine: "don't duplicate capability the agent already has" argues for the latter — the agent can Read+Grep across files. Blocks any rule/finder that would widen predicates to cross-file handler presence.

- **J-8 (scan_file error discrimination split).** Bootstrap confirmed `scan_file` conflates `file-unsupported` (extension not in set) with file-not-found. Split options: (a) `file-not-found` / `unsupported-extension` / `file-unreadable` (three distinct codes), (b) fold not-found into a single `file-unreadable` (two codes: "can't read this file" + "can't parse this extension"), (c) keep `file-unsupported` as umbrella with a `details.reason` sub-discriminator. Doctrine prefers explicit codes (a); (b) is more compact; (c) keeps the existing discriminator stable. Blocks Q-SHARED-SCAN-FILE-ERROR-DISCRIMINATION implementation shape.

---

## Track C — Conformance-claim gaps

Owner: main session + `spec-researcher` + `doc-writer`. Staged; do not dispatch alongside active tracks. ra11y today produces *signals* (findings, review candidates, coverage hints). It does not produce a *claim*. Track C closes the four gaps that separate the two — attestation ledger, runtime-evidence ingest, process-level scope, conformance statement. **No new rules**; the track widens what an agent can defensibly say after using the tool.

Sequencing (cross-gap, soft): attestations first (unlocks evidence semantics everywhere else), then axe ingest (wide-reach runtime coverage), then process scope, then conformance capstone. Within each gap items are ordered by the ADR-then-foundation-then-surface pattern.

### v0.3.0 — attestation ledger (foundation)

- [x] **C-ATTEST-ADR** ADR 0011 (evidence as first-class primitive) + ADR 0012 (rule-scoped attestations) cover this slot.
- [x] **C-ATTEST-STORE** `.ra11y/attestations.jsonl` append-only store with validated envelope landed in `src/config/attestation-store.ts`.
- [x] **C-ATTEST-TOOL** `attest` MCP tool landed; accepts `criterionId`, `ruleIds?`, `verdict`, `scope`, `location?`, `reason`, `by`; stamps commit hash; rejects bare verdicts.
- [x] **C-ATTEST-LIST** `list_attestations` MCP tool landed with git-backed staleness probe + `staleProbeUnavailable` signal for non-repo environments.
- [x] **C-ATTEST-PRUNE** `ra11y attestations prune [--dry-run]` subcommand landed; pure function + injectable `fileExists` predicate.
- [x] **C-ATTEST-CHECKLIST** Checklist items spread per-criterion `attestation?: { verdict, stale?, evidence, by? }` with conditional-spread discipline.
- [x] **C-SUPPRESSION-ATTEST** Bare pragmas emit `verdict: "pending"` attestations; reasoned pragmas emit `"pass"`; `suppression/no-reason` finder retained as complementary signal.

### Rejected — runtime evidence bridge (2026-04-19)

A prior plan proposed ingesting vendor runtime results (axe-core JSON → normalized shape → ledger) as the path to close runtime-only WCAG criteria. Rejected: vendor-specific ingest adapters duplicate capability agents already have via their own test harnesses, and the tool's job is to point at the source — not at another tool's output. The ingest shape is also fragile to vendor schema drift, and the normalized layer re-buckets findings in ways the reading agent can't re-audit. Agents that run runtime checks bridge their results through the existing `attest` tool — the reason text is the evidence, and the attestation ledger is the durable, vendor-neutral channel.

Items struck: C-RUNTIME-ADR, C-AXE-SCHEMA, C-AXE-INGEST, C-RUNTIME-MAP. C-COVERAGE-MERGE is re-scoped below.

- [x] **C-COVERAGE-MERGE** Coverage report integrates attestations; per-criterion row carries optional `attested: { verdict, stale? }`. Manual criteria with fresh pass/n-a attestations count toward covered. Stale/pending/fail don't flip static verdicts.

### v0.3.0 — process-level scope

- [x] **C-PROCESS-ADR** ADR 0016 (process-level scope) landed.
- [x] **C-PROCESS-CONFIG** `processes: [{ name, pages }]` config primitive + schema validation + loader wiring.
- [x] **C-PROCESS-SCAN** `scan_process` MCP tool landed; orchestrates per-page scan with process-level frame.
- [x] **C-PROCESS-CONSISTENCY** `consistent-navigation` finder honors declared processes; heuristic path retained as fallback with a reason-text nudge to declare the primitive.
- [x] **C-PROCESS-IDENT** `consistent-identification` finder for wcag22:3.2.4; emits zero candidates when no processes config (honest needs-config gap).

### v1.0.0 — conformance capstone

- [x] **C-PROFILE-WCAG21AA** `ConformanceProfile` primitive + 8 built-ins (wcag21-a/aa, wcag22-a/aa/aaa, section508, en301549, ada); `--profile` CLI flag with explicit-override warning. Profile→reports scope filtering wired in turn 8.
- [x] **C-CONFORM-ADR** ADR 0017 (conformance-statement-output) landed.
- [x] **C-CONFORM-STATEMENT** `conformance_statement` MCP tool + `src/reports/conformance.ts` landed; refuses when coverage is incomplete; blocker list cites missing criteria.
- [x] **C-CONFORM-SIGN** SHA-256 digest over (commit hash + attestations + in-scope criteria + config fingerprint); canonical JSON; `verifyConformanceBundle` for drift detection.
- [x] **C-CONFORM-DOC** `docs/conformance.md` end-to-end agent guide: scan → checklist → attest → coverage → signed statement.

### Dependencies + interactions with Track S

Track S speculative tools (`verdict-candidate`, `draft-vpat-narrative`, `resolve-component`) become concretely useful once **C-ATTEST-TOOL** lands — their output is evidence the agent writes to the ledger. Track C resolves Track S's "pick 1–2 speculative tools" decision (ADR 0005 §Follow-up): `verdict-candidate` is the natural first pick because its output IS an attestation entry. Promote Track S `[!]` items to `[ ]` once C-ATTEST-TOOL is merged.

### Considered and rejected

- **Embed axe-core as a runtime dep.** Rejected per **§3 invariant 1** (zero runtime deps). Parse axe's JSON output; don't pull axe-core into the install. Agents already run axe in their test harness; they hand us the JSON path.
- **In-tool headless browser (Playwright / Puppeteer).** Rejected per zero-deps + MCP-first-consumer framing — the agent already runs the browser in its test harness. We ingest; we don't drive.
- **Blanket "ra11y verifies conformance" marketing.** Rejected — the conformance statement will cite evidence sources; ra11y is the aggregator, the attestation is the agent's (or human's) word, and the signed bundle is what survives audit. Honesty > reach.

Source: 2026-04-19 three-agent gap analysis (rule coverage vs WCAG 2.1/2.2 A+AA, MCP shape discipline, backlog + semver + CI infra). The conformance capstone in Track C is shipped; these are the remaining gates for the "find all violations" bar.

No item here proposes new rule detection logic — Track V is about honesty surfaces, decision closure, and coverage verification. Items that WOULD add detection get logged as Track R or Track C items.

### v1.0.0 — plugin API integrity (2026-04-20)

Source: 2026-04-20 architectural audit — two independent Explore passes converged on the same finding, verified by grep. `grep -rln "BUILTIN_RULES\|BUILTIN_STANDARDS" src/` returns 33 files; 31 of those are non-barrel leaks past the existing `src/engine/registry/{criteria,rules,standards}.ts` primitives. The leak means CLAUDE.md §3.8 is aspirational, not enforced, and ADR 0019's frozen `defineRule`/`defineStandard`/`defineCandidateFinder` exports function as type-inference helpers only — user-authored rules have no wiring path to `list_rules`, `suggest_fix`, or any scan. ADR 0022 has the full design and 7-commit migration plan. Dispatch sequential within this subsection: stages 1 → 2 → {3, 5} → 4 → 6 → 7, with 3/4/5 parallelizable across agents once 2 lands. Every stage is additive and reversible until V1-REGISTRY-LINT.

- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-AGGREGATE** (68e1240) New `src/engine/registry/registry.ts` wraps the existing `CriteriaRegistry` + `RulesRegistry` + `StandardsRegistry` primitives via a `Registry` class with `rules`, `standards`, `finders`, `criteria` arrays + `findRule` / `findStandard` / `findCriterion` / `rulesForCriterion` accessors. `rulesForCriterion` closes over the reciprocal equivalence graph — returns `readonly Rule[]` in input-position order so call-site swaps stay deterministic. `createBuiltinRegistry()` is the designated single-call site into `BUILTIN_RULES` / `BUILTIN_STANDARDS` / `BUILTIN_CANDIDATE_FINDERS`; no other `src/engine/**` file references those barrels. 20 unit tests cover accessor round-trips, transitive equivalence (both directions), input-order determinism, and a shipped-rule smoke (`media/alt-text-missing` surfaces under `wcag22:1.1.1` + declared equivalents). Pure addition — zero call-site change; stages 2–7 migrate consumers.
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-SESSION** (06d6ea5) `McpSession` constructor now instantiates `createBuiltinRegistry()` and exposes it as `readonly registry: Registry`. Zero call-site migration — existing `BUILTIN_*` imports inside `src/mcp/**` untouched so stage 3+ can land incrementally. Three new tests in `tests/unit/mcp/session.test.ts` pin the construction contract + the `findRule("contrast/minimum")` / unknown-ID round-trip.
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-HELPERS** (9ebfbce) Scope mis-sized. Four of the six call sites sit inside exported module-level helpers with no `session` parameter — `findRule` / `findStandard` / `firstUnknownStandard` / `satisfyingRulesForCriterion`. Dropping the `BUILTIN_*` barrels requires adding a `session` parameter to each, which cascades to ~9 single-line call-site updates across eight stage-4 files (`src/mcp/tools.ts`, `tool-suggest-fix.ts`, `tool-coverage.ts`, `tool-checklist.ts`, `tool-vpat.ts`, `tool-review-candidates.ts`, `tool-conformance-statement.ts`, `tool-attest.ts`). The `Registry` aggregate surface is sufficient — this is a helper-signature constraint, not an accessor gap. Two resolutions: (1) merge stages 3 + 4a into one commit that threads `session` through callers (net diff ≈20 lines outside `tools-helpers.ts`); (2) split stage 3 into 3a (migrate the two session-bearing sites, keep the four helpers reaching `BUILTIN_*` locally) + 3b (drop barrels alongside helper-signature change alongside stage 4). Prefer (1) on 2026-04-21 re-plan. Blocked per the `/continue` scope-lock; re-dispatch with widened scope.
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-MCP-TOOLS** (6d6d6a8 — 6 atomic commits dd47e19..5d94912) Migrate `src/mcp/tools.ts` + `src/mcp/tool-*.ts` (16 files) to read through `session.registry`. Dispatch in parallel groups by family: {scan + scan-process + review-candidates}, {suggest-fix + apply-fix + apply-fix-internals}, {baseline + propose-baseline + attest}, {coverage + checklist + vpat + conformance-statement + explain-standard + list-suppressions + propose-config}, plus {completions + scan-project-budget + wrappers-meta}. Each group is its own commit under the 400-LOC budget. Blocks on V1-REGISTRY-HELPERS. Owner: main session + parallel agent dispatch. Medium (≈4 commits).
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-CLI** Landed across three commits (1a57d4b scan-family + 734490d explain-family + 1c46a6c list-family). All eleven `src/cli/commands/*.ts` hits and `src/cli/run.ts` now route through a `registry: Registry = createBuiltinRegistry()` parameter; `attest` dropped its local `criterionExists` / `satisfyingRulesForCriterion` helpers in favor of `registry.findCriterion` + `registry.rulesForCriterion` (equivalence-closure semantics now match MCP attest). Invariant: `rg "BUILTIN_(RULES|STANDARDS|CANDIDATE_FINDERS)" src/cli/` returns zero hits. `V1-REGISTRY-LINT` can now converge once `V1-REGISTRY-MCP-TOOLS` (blocked on `V1-REGISTRY-HELPERS`) lands.
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-LINT** (f6c133a) New `scripts/check-builtins-scope.ts` modeled on `scripts/check-network-isolation.ts`. Forbids importing `BUILTIN_RULES` / `BUILTIN_STANDARDS` / `BUILTIN_CANDIDATE_FINDERS` outside `src/engine/registry/**`, the three barrel files themselves, and `tests/**`. Added to the `scripts/verify.ts` sequence (CLAUDE.md §4). Acceptance: `bun scripts/check-builtins-scope.ts` green. Blocks on V1-REGISTRY-MCP-TOOLS + V1-REGISTRY-CLI converging (every non-barrel call site must be migrated first). This is the commit that locks the invariant. Owner: main session. Small.
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-PLUGIN-WIRE** `src/api/plugin.ts` helpers remain identity functions at the type level (ADR 0019 freeze), but `createRegistry({ rules, standards, finders })` now composes builtins with user-authored additions. `McpSession` + CLI bootstrap accept a registry override so a future `ra11y.config.ts` hook or CLI `--plugin` flag can thread one in. Working example under `examples/plugin-rule/` showing a user rule visible to `list_rules` and producing violations through `scan_project`. Integration test covering end-to-end plugin path. ADR 0022 flipped Proposed → Accepted. (b546831, turn 23)

### v1.0.0 — MCP response-assembly seam (2026-04-20)

Source: 2026-04-20 architectural audit (Explore-agent survey of `src/mcp/**` + grep verification). Same class of leak as V1-REGISTRY-AGGREGATE but one layer up: the AI-first consumer doctrine in `docs/kb/architecture/ai-first-consumer.md` (conditional-spread on zero counts, `warnings` on zero-output success, split composite headline counters, present-when-meaningful optional fields, honest `fixesByClass` tally) is currently enforced in 24 separate `src/mcp/tool-*.ts` files by convention + the path-scoped rule at `.claude/rules/mcp-response-shapes.md`. Verified counts on 2026-04-20:

| measurement                                                                 | count |
|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------|------:|
| `src/mcp/tool-*.ts` files                                                   | 24    |
| Tool files routing through `runScanAndFormat` (the existing near-assembler) | **2** (`tool-scan-project.ts`, `tool-scan-diff.ts`) |
| Tool files hand-rolling `meta: {` blocks directly                           | 10    |
| Tool files calling `buildAgentFinding` or composing `AgentFinding` manually | 8+    |
| `scan` + `scan_file` tool definitions                                       | inline in `src/mcp/tools.ts` (625 LOC mixing schemas + handlers) |
| `src/mcp/tools-helpers.ts` current size                                     | 856 LOC (hosts `runScanAndFormat` + fraying) |
| `scripts/check-response-nullability.ts` allowlist entries                   | 1 (catches a narrow slice; doesn't catch field-presence drift) |

The partial assembler `src/mcp/scan-assembly.ts` (`buildScanPlan` / `buildScanMeta` / `buildAnalysisCoverage` / `suppressionsMetaBlock` / `wrappersMetaBlock`) is already the right shape, but it lives underneath a non-authoritative wrapper (`runScanAndFormat`) and is only consumed by 2 of 24 tools. Every new scan-family field (future sampling outputs, new fix-class lanes, new `warnings` codes, new honest-counter splits) currently requires touching ≤24 sites with no CI gate forcing synchrony — silent cross-surface drift is the waiting failure mode, and the invariants called out in `ai-first-consumer.md` ("`scan` says 21, `checklist` says 4") are exactly what this seam prevents.

Why v1.0 blocker: the MCP response shape is part of the public contract v1.0 freezes (ADR 0019 + the migration table in `docs/migrations/0.1-to-0.2.md`). Freezing the shape before codifying the single-path invariant means third-party tooling pattern-matches on hand-rolled examples; changing the pattern later is a semver-breaking event. This is structurally identical to the Registry aggregate work (ADR 0022) — an internal seam that must be in place before the surface it backs becomes immutable.

Design captured in ADR 0024 (Proposed, 2026-04-20). Dispatch sequential within this subsection: stage 1 (assembler) → 2 (handler extract from `tools.ts`) → {3, 4, 5 parallel} → 6 (lint) → 7 (handler-size budget). Stages 3-5 are parallelizable across agents on separate worktrees (no shared files — each touches a disjoint set of `tool-*.ts`). Every stage is additive and reversible until V1-RESPONSE-LINT. Golden snapshots under `tests/snapshots/mcp/**` must stay byte-identical through every refactor commit — any intentional shape change splits into a separate `feat(mcp):` commit with rationale.

- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-ASSEMBLER** (e095fc9) New `src/mcp/response-assembler.ts` exporting `assembleScanFamilyResponse(input: ScanFamilyResponseInput, options?: ScanFamilyResponseOptions): ScanFamilyResponse`. `input` carries `violations, perRuleCoverage, reviewCandidates, discovery, wrappers, config, durationMs, toolName, scopeHint`; `options` carries `hoistReferenceGuide, includeReviewCandidates, tokenBudget`. Internally orchestrates the existing extracted helpers: `buildScanPlan` + `buildScanMeta` + `buildAnalysisCoverage` + `suppressionsMetaBlock` + `wrappersMetaBlock` + `buildAgentFinding` (per violation) + `buildReferenceGuide` + `dedupReviewCandidates` + `tokenBudget` clamping + `warnings` code emission (`scanned_zero_files`, `root_source_defaulted`, `no_config_found`, `tailwind_detected_css_undercounted`, `template_files_parsed_as_literal`). Pure addition — zero call-site change; stages 3-5 migrate consumers. Unit tests mirror the existing assembly-helper tests: honest-counter-split cases, conditional-spread fixesByClass, empty-scan warnings emission, reference-guide dedup, per-scope variants. Blocks on nothing. Owner: `feature-dev:code-architect`. Medium. ≤400 LOC net.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-CORE** (d32bdb8..7e7a523, turn 20, 2026-04-21) `scan` + `scan_file` migrated through `assembleScanFamilyResponse`; handlers extracted to `src/mcp/tool-scan.ts` + `src/mcp/tool-scan-file.ts`; `src/mcp/scan-collect.ts` added as `runScanAndCollect` seam helper. `scan_process` deferred — per-page `ScanResult[]` shape is structurally different and warrants separate analysis; split into V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-PROCESS below. 4 commits: 1b273e1 64c5f21 ac7f760 61be322.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-PROCESS** (turn 25, 2026-04-21) Decision: `scan_process` stays OUTSIDE `assembleScanFamilyResponse`. Three converging reasons: (1) `perPageResults: ScanResult[]` is the documented primitive (ADR 0016) future process-level finders cross-index — collapsing N pages into one `files[]` discards the per-page boundary; (2) `meta` is process-level (`processName`, `resolvedProcess`, `missingPages`) not scan-level (`activeNativeWrappers`, `rulesEvaluated`, …) — merging would violate "ambiguous field shapes are dishonest"; (3) looping the assembler per-page would force composing a dishonest composite plan/meta across pages. Rationale lives as a durable docblock at the top of `src/mcp/tool-scan-process.ts` so V1-RESPONSE-LINT's allowlist entry can cite the file by path. No code changes to the handler; no snapshot drift.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-DERIVATIVE** (7007df8..7b2f49a, turn 21, 2026-04-21) Migrated `checklist` + `coverage` + `conformance_statement` handlers through `buildDerivativeScanWarnings` assembler seam. `buildDerivativeScanWarnings` exported from `src/mcp/response-assembler.ts`; cross-tool invariant test guards `scan.totalFindings === checklist.automatedFindings + checklist.manualReviewRequired` drift class. No snapshot drift (derivative tools use bespoke shapes). 2 commits.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-FIX-FAMILY** — **DECISION 2026-04-21: pick (a), narrow scope.** Share the assembler's `groupByFile` helper for `baseline.check` only; drop `apply_fix` + `suggest_fix` from scope. Preserves the byte-identical-snapshots acceptance gate that v1.0.0 depends on. `apply_fix` and `suggest_fix` legitimately emit different shapes (before/after slices, primary/alternative fix paths) — forcing them through the assembler would sum categorically different data, which the "composite headline counts are dishonest" rule forbids. ADR 0024's lint gate (scripts/check-response-assembly.ts), LOC budget, and assembler for scan-core + scan-derivative tools all still land. If post-v1 an agent reports cross-tool drift between `baseline` and `scan_project`, revisit — structurally the fix-family responses carry different data.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-LINT** New `scripts/check-response-assembly.ts` modeled on `scripts/check-network-isolation.ts` + `scripts/check-response-nullability.ts`. Statically forbids scan-family tool files from defining object literals with a `plan` key or a `meta: {` block without routing through `assembleScanFamilyResponse`. Allowlist: `src/mcp/response-assembler.ts`, `src/mcp/scan-assembly.ts` (the underlying helpers), non-scan-family tools (`attest`, `suppress`, `list_rules`, `list_suppressions`, `explain_*`, `bootstrap`, `detect_native_wrappers`, `wrapper_introspect`, `sessionConfigure`), and `tests/**`. Added to the `scripts/verify.ts` sequence (CLAUDE.md §4). Acceptance: `bun scripts/check-response-assembly.ts` green; dry-run synthetic test (temporarily hand-roll a `plan:` block in `tool-coverage.ts`, confirm the check fails, revert) passes before landing. This is the commit that locks the invariant. Blocks on V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-CORE + V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-DERIVATIVE + V1-RESPONSE-FIX-FAMILY converging. Owner: main session. Small.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-HANDLER-BUDGET** Enforce `src/mcp/tool-*.ts` ≤ 150 LOC after migration via an extension to `scripts/check-limits.ts`. Handlers that still exceed the budget are doing work that belongs in the assembler — keep extracting until they fit (or carry an inline `// ra11y-limits-exempt: <reason>` pragma, matching the existing limits-check escape). Acceptance: `bun scripts/check-limits.ts` green; aggregate `src/mcp/tool-*.ts` LOC drops from 8,485 to ≤ 3,600 (≈150 × 24). Blocks on V1-RESPONSE-LINT. Owner: main session. Small. Final commit of the subsection — flips ADR 0024 status Proposed → Accepted.

Headroom watch: cold-start grows with CLI module graph; 1000-file throughput scales linearly with rule count.

ADR enumerates every exported name across both entry points: 8 names from `@ra11y/core` (`Criterion`, `ReportData`, `ScanResult`, `Severity`, `Standard`, `Violation`, `scan`, `ScanOptions`) and 7 from `@ra11y/core/plugin` (`defineRule`, `defineStandard`, `defineFormatter`, `defineConfig`, `defineCandidateFinder`, `FormatterFn`, `Formatter`). Escape-hatch `experimental` column documented but empty at v1.0.

New empty `## [Unreleased]` sits at top. Ready for `/release 1.0.0` when the user triggers.

### v1.0.0 — detection gaps (third-pass scan, 2026-04-19)

Source: "find all violations" bar re-raised 2026-04-19. Four-agent gap audit against WCAG 2.2 A+AA surface. Each item widens detection on an existing rule or adds a small new primitive — none reshape the engine. Gaps are where a real violation on common production patterns walks past our rules undetected. Dispatch in parallel — independent files.

- [x] **V1-DETECT-ALT-WIDEN** (db325c9, a174d62) `src/rules/media/alt-text-missing.ts` today only walks `<img>` and `<input type="image">` (line 13 header). Real violation patterns miss: (a) SVG `<image src="…">` inside `<svg>`; (b) `role="img"` on arbitrary elements (`<div role="img">`, `<span role="img">`); (c) `<canvas>` with no fallback text + no aria-label/aria-labelledby. Widen element walk + apply existing `hasAccessibleName*` logic. Satisfies wcag22:1.1.1, wcag21:1.1.1. Fix scope: rule extension; add three walkers with the same accessible-name check that runs today on `<img>`. Tests: `tests/fixtures/{good,bad}/alt-text-missing/` gains svg-image, role-img-div, canvas-no-fallback cases. Effort: small.
- [x] **V1-DETECT-BUTTON-NAME-SVG** (2af5caf) `src/rules/semantics/button-name.ts:130` uses `htmlTextContent` which walks HtmlElement + HtmlText but does NOT descend into SVG `<text>` / `<title>` children. `<button><svg><title>Close</title></svg></button>` computes as name-less today but is accessibly named in every major AT. Same blind spot for `<a>`/interactive controls. Extend `hasAccessibleNameHtml`/`hasAccessibleNameJsx` to walk SVG `<title>` + `<text>` as direct children of interactive controls. Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2. Effort: small.
- [x] **V1-DETECT-BUTTON-NAME-IMAGE-INPUT** (2af5caf) `src/rules/semantics/button-name.ts:155-173` filters `["button", "submit", "reset"]` only; `<input type="image" src=... title="Submit">` is never checked. Alt-text rule covers the `alt` path but not the `title`-as-name path — so a `title`-named image input reports no violation correctly, but a name-less one also reports no violation (false negative). Add `type="image"` to the button-name filter and recognize `alt` OR `title` OR `aria-label` as valid name sources. Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2, wcag22:1.1.1. Effort: small.
- [x] **V1-DETECT-LABELS-CONTENTEDITABLE** (e8ca208) `src/rules/forms/labels-required.ts:38` `LABELABLE_TAGS = ["input","select","textarea"]`. `<div contenteditable="true">` acts as a form control (Slack-style composers, Notion-style editors) and has the same labeling requirement under WCAG 1.3.2 + 3.3.2 but is not checked. Extend the element set to include `contenteditable="true"` (not `false` or `inherit`). Effort: small.
- [x] **V1-DETECT-CONTRAST-BG-IMAGE** (431cd1b, 6c49c2b) `src/rules/contrast/_shared.ts:114` resolves only `background-color` and `background` (color-only shorthand). Text over `background-image: url(…)` / `background: linear-gradient(…)` currently skips contrast evaluation silently — no violation, no `couldBeWrongBecause` signal. Two paths: (a) emit `couldBeWrongBecause: "background_image_unresolvable"` as additive signal for affected declarations (honest surfacing, no false positives); (b) defer luminance heuristic for gradients to a follow-up. Path (a) is the v1.0 scope. Effort: small.

### v1.0.0 — fourth-pass external-codebase scan (2026-04-20)

Source: `scan_project` run against the twbs/bootstrap repo (125 parseable files, 131 findings, 114 ms). Bootstrap is 104 `.astro` + 122 `.scss` + 47 `.css` + 14 `.html` + js/ts; it is documentation-site-shaped (Astro) with a traditional JS-test HTML fixture corpus. Two classes of gap surfaced: (a) the discovery pipeline silently drops unknown extensions — 226 of 351 source files vanished without a warning; (b) the response clocked 147 KB and overran the MCP token ceiling on the first call even with `limit: 50`, driven by one rule echoing user-authored label text twice per finding. Five shape/coverage-honesty items + one response-size item. Each has a concrete file:line anchor. Dispatch in parallel — independent files.

- [x] **V1-DETECT-SILENT-EXT** `src/utils/path.ts:20-28` `PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS` is `{.ts,.tsx,.js,.jsx,.html,.htm,.css}`. `hasParseableExtension` + `src/input/discover.ts` silently skip every other extension — no surfacing anywhere in the scan meta. On Bootstrap 226 source files (104 `.astro`, 122 `.scss`) were dropped at discovery; `filesScanned: 125` reads as "tool covered everything" when the tool actually ignored 64% of the repo. Landed: `discoverFilesWithDiagnostics` + `parseFilesWithDiagnostics` + warning code `extensions_skipped_no_parser` + `meta.analysisCoverage.skippedByExtension` wired through `runScanAndFormat`; `scan_project` threads the diagnostic. Follow-up: migrate remaining scan-surface tools (scan, scan_file, scan_diff) to `parseFilesWithDiagnostics` so they also emit the warning.
- [x] **V1-SHAPE-SNIPPET-EMPTY** `src/output/agent-response/build-finding.ts` + `src/output/agent-response/types.ts` — `buildSnippet` returned `{ before: [], highlighted: v.snippet ?? "", after: [] }`; `before` / `after` had zero writers anywhere in src/. Landed: `AgentFinding.snippet` is now `string | undefined`, omitted via conditional-spread when absent; `AgentSnippet` type removed; snapshot tests + CHANGELOG Breaking Changes entry updated.
- [x] **V1-SIZE-LABEL-ECHO-CAP** `truncateForEcho(text, max = 200)` helper added to `src/engine/ast-helpers.ts` (dda1414); single U+2026 ellipsis. Threaded through 13 rule files (227725b) — the three backlog-named offenders plus 10 additional sites discovered in the audit grep: `media/alt-text-missing` (4 sites), `semantics/button-name`, `document/iframe-title`, `tooltip/dismissable` (replaced bespoke truncator), `media/video-captions-missing`, `layout/reflow-hardcoded-width`, `parsing/duplicate-id`, `parsing/html-has-lang`, `document/lang-on-parts`, `document/meta-refresh`, `motion/pause-stop-hide`, `navigation/skip-link`. Read-only / controlled-vocabulary hits explicitly skipped (ARIA enums, table cell ≤40 cap, accesskey single chars). Helper unit tests + invariant assertions on label-in-name. `pointer/target-size` retains its own 40-char cap (intentional, tighter than default).
- [x] **V1-SIZE-RESPONSE-BUDGET** ADR 0021 (`docs/adr/0021-scan-response-size-budget.md`) accepted option (a): lower `DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT` 200 → 25 in `src/mcp/tool-scan-project.ts` (c1945fa). Calibration from a 16-fixture measurement corpus (real-world + synthetic): bytes-per-file is stable ~2.9–6.5 KB across profiles; 25 keeps typical responses under 100 KB and caps worst-case label-heavy at ~163 KB with honest `truncated` + `nextOffset` signals. 200-file responses were 1.24 MB — factor-of-8 cut, not marginal tuning. New unit test asserts default-cap triggers truncation at 25 on a 30-file fixture. Token-aware budget (option b) rejected to preserve existing `limit`/`offset` contract semantics consumers already understand.
- [x] **V1-SHAPE-FIXCLASS-HEADLINE** `src/mcp/scan-assembly.ts` plan-summary builder — Bootstrap scan returned `plan.summary: "129 violations (129 guidance fixes). 2 notes… 4 actionable manual review items + 17 untargeted criteria"`. The parenthetical sums four categorically different `fixClass` sub-buckets (`mechanical` 31, `guidance` 30, `runtime-only` 24, `verify-in-source` 46) under one "guidance fixes" label. Directly violates `@docs/kb/architecture/ai-first-consumer.md` §"Composite headline counts are dishonest" — agents budget against the summary string and get four distinct work kinds conflated. Fix: either stop summing in the prose ("129 violations (31 mechanical, 30 guidance, 24 runtime-only, 46 verify-in-source)") or drop the parenthetical and rely on the per-finding `fixClass` discriminator (already shipped in Q2-FIXKIND). Effort: small. Landed `33a1978` (Option A — per-lane breakdown, zero-count lanes omitted). Follow-ups: (1) rename top-level `mechanicalEditsAvailable`/`guidanceFixesAvailable` fields to avoid the fixClass enum-name collision; (2) `src/output/agent-response/build-plan.ts` CLI `--format agent` summary uses the same composite "mechanical edits / guidance fixes" pattern and warrants the same split.
- [x] **V1-SHAPE-RULECOV-COUNT** `findingsEmitted: number` added to `PerRuleCoverage` (`src/types/violation.ts:261-268`) and stamped on every entry in `buildPerRuleCoverage` via a new `countFindingsByRule` linear pass over `allViolations` (f035908). Always present, zero-is-meaningful per the AI-first consumer doctrine. Scanner now passes `allViolations` (pre-severity-filter) to keep telemetry honest about what the engine observed. Unit + integration tests assert `findingsEmitted === violation-stream-count` invariant; existing test fixtures updated to populate the new required field. Counter was NOT pre-computed (backlog hint inaccurate); the linear pass adds negligible cost.

### v1.0.0 — fifth-pass external-codebase scan (2026-04-20)

Source: second pass of `scan_project` against twbs/bootstrap (today's HEAD, 125 parseable files, 131 findings) after the V1-SHAPE-* / V1-SIZE-* fourth-pass items landed. Whole re-scan confirms four of the six fourth-pass fixes are wired through the MCP path. Two new items: (a) V1-SHAPE-FIXCLASS-HEADLINE follow-up (2) — the CLI `--format agent` summary — promoting from a buried follow-up line into a first-class item; (b) V1-SIZE-RESPONSE-BUDGET's shipped file-count cap does not bind on label-dense codebases where findings cluster in few files, so the MCP-ceiling overrun symptom the ADR intended to prevent still fires on bootstrap.

- [x] **V1-SHAPE-CLI-AGENT-HEADLINE** (331a805 — option (c): `buildFixClassBreakdown` extracted to neutral `src/output/agent-response/fix-class-breakdown.ts`; `src/mcp/plan-summary.ts` re-imports from the neutral location (MCP→output was already legitimate direction, inverse would have violated layering). `build-plan.ts:buildSummary` now tallies violations by `v.fixClass` and routes the parenthetical through the shared helper. `AgentPlan` field names (`mechanicalEditsAvailable`/`guidanceFixesAvailable`) unchanged — parent item's follow-up (1) stays separate. Three new snapshot tests: mixed-lane breakdown, zero-count lane omission, all-four-lanes fixture. CHANGELOG entry under `## [Unreleased]`.)

- [x] **V1-SIZE-RESPONSE-BUDGET-DENSITY** Option (b) landed (402e546 + 2a26c25 — `src/mcp/reference-guide.ts`: `hashFixDescription` / `hoistFixDescriptions` / `applyFixDescriptionHoist` / `hoistAndBuildReferenceGuide` extend the Q2-PROSE hoist; duplicates keyed by `(ruleId, 12-hex SHA-256 hash)` so two distinct descriptions under one rule both survive (verified on `semantics/label-in-name` which emits a 466-char and 632-char verdict independently). Threshold: hoist when `(ruleId, hash)` repeats ≥2 in the response; singletons stay inline. Wired through scan / scan_file / scan_project / scan_diff (matches Q2-PROSE coverage; suggest_fix / apply_fix / baseline skipped per small response bodies). `AgentFix.description` → optional + `AgentFinding.fixDescriptionRef: { hash }` replaces it on hoisted findings — never both. 11 unit tests in `tests/unit/mcp/reference-guide-hoist.test.ts` + 1 integration round-trip. Breaking CHANGELOG entry under `[Unreleased]`. Measurement on `/tmp/bootstrap` MCP response: 124,624 → 107,036 bytes (**14.1% / ≈ 17.6 KB saved**). Partial win flagged honestly: **100 KB MCP-ceiling target not met** — post-hoist response ≈ 107 KB (~26.7k tokens vs 25k ceiling). Remaining per-finding bloat: `message` ≈ 22 KB (per-finding context, not dedupable), `fix` ≈ 13 KB, `suppressWith` ≈ 7.6 KB, `criteriaTitles` ≈ 3.8 KB. Options (a) token-aware cap and (c) shift full prose to `suggest_fix` are now promoted to the follow-up item below.

- [x] **V1-SIZE-RESPONSE-BUDGET-DENSITY-2** Option (a) landed (66a9147 + 9c73266 + 538e392 — ADR 0021 amended with the token-density secondary budget; `src/mcp/token-budget.ts` exposes `applyTokenBudget` + `CHARS_PER_TOKEN_PROXY = 4` + `DEFAULT_TOKEN_BUDGET_CHARS = 88000` (~22000 tokens, ~3k headroom under the 25k ceiling); scan-tool wiring lives in `scan-project-budget.ts` + `scan-diff-budget.ts` to keep the scan-tool files under the 500-effective-line budget; wired through `scan` / `scan_project` / `scan_diff` — `scan_file` is a single-file shape with no trailing-file axis to trim, so it's not wired through, matching the V1-SIZE-RESPONSE-BUDGET-DENSITY (b) hoist's own skip); new `response_token_budget_truncated` warning code disambiguates density truncation from the existing file-count cap; `truncated: true` composes cleanly between the two caps, `totalFilesWithFindings` persists, `nextOffset` advances on `scan_project`, omitted on `scan`/`scan_diff` (no `limit`/`offset` contract there); progress guarantee keeps ≥1 file entry even when a single file exceeds the budget. Tests: `tests/unit/mcp/token-budget.test.ts` (helper invariants) + `tests/unit/mcp/token-budget-scan-project.test.ts` (50-file label-dense integration fixture). Bootstrap measurement (post-merge, in-process handler): body 107,036 B / ~26.7k tokens → 78,167 B / ~19.5k tokens; envelope 86,098 B / ~21.5k tokens; `truncated: true`, `nextOffset: 8`, `response_token_budget_truncated` fires; 25k MCP ceiling held with ~3.5k-token envelope headroom. ADR 0021 amendment updated with the post-merge measurement table. Option (c) remains on the table only if a future measurement shows the density cap can't hold typical codebases under 25k tokens — density cap holds bootstrap under budget today.

### v1.0.0 — sixth-pass external-codebase scan (2026-04-20)

Source: third `scan_project` pass against twbs/bootstrap HEAD after fourth- and fifth-pass items landed (125 parseable files, 131 findings, 115 ms, response body 78 KB truncated at nextOffset 8 — density budget held). This pass confirms three rule-accuracy false positives: two are rule-logic bugs (select/option composition; modal runtime-toggle pattern), one is reason-text thinness on a widely-used framework idiom. A fourth item captures a per-file noise ratio that suggests rollup hints. Each has a concrete file:line anchor + a verbatim finding from the scan. Dispatch in parallel — independent files.

- [x] **V1-FP-LABEL-IN-NAME-SELECT** `src/rules/semantics/label-in-name.ts:34-41` — `<select>` is in `HTML_INTERACTIVE_TAGS`, so the rule harvests its descendant text as "visible label." On `twbs/bootstrap` at `site/src/assets/examples/floating-labels/floating-label.html:77`, `<select class="form-select" id="floatingSelect" aria-label="Floating label select example">` with three `<option>` children fired 18× (13.7% of the whole scan): "visible text 'Open this select menu One Two Three' … not contained in aria-label". `<select>`'s accessible-name computation does **not** include `<option>` descendant text — only the currently-selected option's text is the widget's value, and its accessible name comes from `aria-label` / `<label for>` / none. Fix: exclude `<option>` text when computing the visible-label substring for a `<select>`; or more broadly, use only direct text nodes (not descendant `<option>`s) for `<select>`. Curate `tests/fixtures/real-world/bootstrap-floating-label-select/` with the three-option pattern asserting zero findings.
- [x] **V1-FP-TOOLTIP-DATA-BS-TOGGLE** `src/rules/tooltip/dismissable.ts` — rule has no awareness of JS-enhanced tooltip toggles. On `twbs/bootstrap` at `js/tests/integration/index.html:18`, `<button … data-bs-toggle="tooltip" title="Tooltip on top">` fired 18× across the scan. Bootstrap's tooltip JS **replaces** the native `title` with an ARIA-aware tooltip widget at runtime (`aria-describedby` + role=tooltip + keyboard dismiss); the `title` attribute is a source string, not a rendered native tooltip. Surface-don't-suppress per §1: keep the finding but enrich `reason` when sibling attrs `data-bs-toggle="tooltip"|"popover"` or `data-toggle="tooltip"|"popover"` (BS4 legacy) or `data-tippy-content` are present — "note: a JS tooltip library appears to enhance this element; the native `title` may be an input to a runtime widget. Verify the runtime behavior at the call site." Curate `tests/fixtures/real-world/bootstrap-data-bs-toggle-tooltip/`.
- [x] **V1-FP-HIDDEN-FOCUS-MODAL** `src/rules/aria/hidden-focus.ts` — rule cannot observe runtime `aria-hidden` toggles. On `twbs/bootstrap` at `js/tests/integration/index.html:41`, `<div class="modal fade" … aria-hidden="true">` containing focusable `<button class="btn-close">` fired 3× on this scan and is the canonical FP of the Bootstrap/BEM modal family. The `limitations` block already names this class of gap, but the finding still lands at `severity: error` with no framing. Surface-don't-suppress: keep severity, but when `aria-hidden="true"` sits on an element with class containing `modal`, `dialog`, `drawer`, `offcanvas`, `popover`, `toast`, `overlay`, `backdrop`, enrich `reason` with "note: class name suggests a runtime-toggled overlay (`aria-hidden` likely flips when the widget opens); static analysis cannot observe the toggle — verify the open-state runtime behavior." Curate `tests/fixtures/real-world/bootstrap-modal-aria-hidden/`.
- [x] **V1-NOISE-RULE-PER-FILE-ROLLUP** `src/output/agent-response/build-plan.ts` + `src/mcp/scan-assembly.ts` — on bootstrap, `forms/autocomplete-missing` fired 31× with 28 on `site/src/assets/examples/floating-labels/floating-label.html` alone; `semantics/label-in-name` fired 18× all on the same file (entangled with V1-FP-LABEL-IN-NAME-SELECT but the pattern generalizes). When one rule fires >N× on one file (threshold proposal: 10), keep every finding in `files[].findings` (no grouping, no dedup — surface-don't-suppress) but add an honest hint: e.g. `perRuleCoverage[ruleId].concentration?: { file, count }` pointing at the densest file when its share exceeds 50% of that rule's total. Signal for the agent that one file is likely where the idiom lives; zero information loss.

### v1.0.0 — seventh-pass external-codebase scan (2026-04-20)

Source: fourth `scan_project` pass against twbs/bootstrap HEAD after sixth-pass fixes landed (125 parseable files, 131 findings, 123 ms, 78 KB response truncated at nextOffset 8 — density budget held). This pass surfaces a previously-invisible coverage gap: bootstrap has 453 source files, but 328 were silently dropped at ingestion (110 `.mdx`, 114 `.scss`, 104 `.astro`), so `filesScanned: 125` reads as full coverage on 27% of the repo. The `extensions_skipped_no_parser` warning fires but doesn't enumerate the hits, and `parseErrorFiles: [remark.ts]` is not echoed as a structured warning at all. Three parser gaps, two honest-signal gaps, two detection gaps, three reason-text enrichment gaps. Each has a concrete evidence anchor from the scan. Dispatch in parallel — independent files.

- [x] **V1-PARSER-SCSS** Landed (7c9987d parser + 96620ec wiring). `src/input/parsers/scss.ts` + `src/input/parsers/scss-internals.ts` implement a phased preprocess/flatten/substitute pipeline into the existing CSS AST — `$var: <literal>;` inlines to subsequent usages, non-literal RHS preserved un-substituted (no synthesized colors), nested selectors flatten one level, `@mixin`/`@include`/`@import`/`@use`/`@forward` stripped, `//` line comments normalized. `.scss` added to `PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS` + `extensionMatches` alias to `.css`; routed through cli scan/conformance, mcp session + apply-fix + build-artifacts + agent-response + per-rule-coverage. 26 unit tests; recoverable parse errors emit ParseError rather than silent drop. Downstream contrast rules see SCSS files transparently — no rule code changes required.
- [x] **V1-PARSER-MDX** Landed (6a8977a parser + e6cc850 tests + e88956b wiring + 056d52f tsx-generic-classifier unblock). `src/input/parsers/mdx.ts` strips frontmatter, import/export statements, and code fences, extracts JSX + raw HTML blocks at line-start boundaries, delegates fragments to the existing TSX/HTML parsers. `.mdx` added to `PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS`; routed through cli scan/conformance, mcp session + apply-fix, agent-response suppression pragmas, per-rule-coverage label. Non-literal expression blocks preserved un-resolved; unparseable fragments emit recoverable parseError. TSX generic-call classifier refuses to cross blank lines (previously swallowed JSX blocks whose preceding line ended in an identifier).
- [x] **V1-PARSER-ASTRO** Landed (39182db parser + a5c37a2 wiring). `src/input/parsers/astro.ts` strips the `---` frontmatter fence (line-preserving whitespace replacement), delegates the remaining template to the existing HTML parser. Opaque component tags (`<Icon>`, `<Header>`) flow through as HTML elements; `{expr}` literal text is honest (unresolved). `.astro` added to `PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS` with `.html` extensionMatches alias; routed through cli scan/conformance, mcp session + apply-fix, agent-response suppression (HTML-comment form), per-rule-coverage "Astro" label. 21 unit tests cover line-preservation, unterminated fence, CRLF, garbage input, opaque-tag templates.
- [x] **V1-WARN-PARSE-ERRORS** (b6167be) `parse_errors_present` added to the `ScanWarningCode` union + emitted via `hasParseErrors(analysisCoverage)` inside the shared `computeScanWarnings` helper. Conditional-spread (never `[]`) — propagates through every consumer of `warningsField` / `warningsFromScanMeta` (scan_project, coverage, checklist, review_candidates, suggest_fix). Three new unit tests in `tests/unit/mcp/warnings.test.ts`; `docs/errors.md` unchanged (that file indexes structured-error codes + CLI exit codes, not MCP scan-warning codes).
- [x] **V1-WARN-EXT-ENUMERATE** Landed (430ee69 ADR 0023 + a549d5c impl). Option 2 chosen — bare `warnings: string[]` stays the stable triage surface; parallel `warningsDetails` sibling carries structured payloads keyed by code, conditional-spread so presence-only warnings add no noise. New `ScanWarningDetails` type + `computeScanWarningDetails` + `summarizeSkippedExtensions` helpers in `src/mcp/warnings.ts`; `warningsField` / `warningsFieldFromScanMeta` co-emit the sibling so every consumer (scan, scan_project, coverage, checklist, review_candidates, suggest_fix) picks it up. 13 new tests (6 payload builder + 5 composite shape + 2 integration on `tool-coverage`). CHANGELOG entry under `### Added` (additive, non-breaking).
- [x] **V1-RULE-ARIA-EXPANDED-DISCLOSURE** Landed (2d24801 rule + 9ca3f0d tests + b82dfd0 kb regen). `src/rules/aria/expanded-on-disclosure.ts` fires on `<button>` / `<a>` / `[role="button"]` matching any of three predicates (aria-controls target, data-toggle value in disclosure set, onclick classList.toggle/add/remove with visibility tokens) that lack `aria-expanded`. Native `<details>/<summary>` exempt. `data-bs-*` / `data-toggle` are additive evidence hints only; no framework branches. 21 unit tests, 6 fixtures, satisfies wcag22:4.1.2 + wcag21:4.1.2.
