- [x] **V1-FINDER-MODAL-FOCUS-MGMT** (c65db1d + f6dbbfd) New `src/review/finders/modal-focus-management.ts` flags modal-shaped containers (`role="dialog"`/`role="alertdialog"` or word-boundary class literal containing `modal`/`drawer`/`offcanvas`) that show zero static focus-management hints in the same file. Hint signals (any one silences all modals in the file): container `tabindex="-1"`, descendant `autofocus` / `data-*autofocus*`, or source-text hit on `.focus(`, `trapFocus`, `focusTrap`, `focus-trap`, `FocusTrap`, `useFocusTrap`, `createFocusTrap`. Confidence `medium`; reason text makes the single-file-check framing explicit. Four criteria emitted per candidate: wcag22:2.4.3, wcag21:2.4.3, wcag22:2.4.11, wcag22:2.4.12. 6 fixtures (3 bad, 3 good), 14 unit tests. Coverage matrix regenerated — WCAG 2.2 2.4.12 promoted from attestation-only → finder.
- [x] **V1-FP-NESTED-INTERACTIVE-NONFOCUSABLE-OUTER** (c3227a4) When the outer element's explicit role is in the ARIA 1.2 composite-widget-delegating set (`tab`, `menuitem`, `option`, `treeitem`, `gridcell`, `row`), the reason text is enriched with a composite-widget-delegates-focus note citing the tablist-roving-tabindex expectation. Severity and finding set unchanged; candidate stays visible per surface-don't-suppress. 7 new unit tests cover the three delegating-role cases plus the non-delegating controls (tablist container, native-outer, `<a href>` inside `<button>`).
- [x] **V1-FP-HEADING-HIERARCHY-CITE-PREV** (21742b4) `reportSkippedLevels` now tracks the full previous `HeadingEntry`; message reads "previous was `<h1>` at line 14, this is `<h5>`. Skipped 3 level(s)." and the suggestion echoes the same line. Scope-note: `Violation` has no `detail` field and `src/types/` was out of scope, so the line is inlined in `message`/`suggestion` — strictly additive, no schema change. Zero detection change (same condition, same findings); three new test assertions cover line-citation, suggestion echo, and most-recent-prior-heading selection.
- [x] **V1-FP-AUTOCOMPLETE-CITE-TRIGGER** (4ae6c7a) `expectedToken` refactored to `matchPurpose` returning `{ expected, trigger }` tagged union (`{ kind: "type" | "name" | "id", ... }`); `describeTrigger()` renders the evidence into the message (`type="email" triggers this rule` / `name attribute contains "email"` / `id attribute contains "email"`). Type beats name/id when both match (most concrete signal); `name` vs `id` distinguished in citation. Fixture-dismissal scenario from backlog covered: `name="floatingInput"` stays silent; `name="emailInput"` fires with name-path citation (no false `type="email"`). Zero detection change.

### v1.0.0 — eighth-pass external-codebase scan (2026-04-20)

Source: 10-agent parallel MCP-tool audit against twbs/bootstrap HEAD after the seventh-pass items landed. Each agent targeted a distinct tool lens (scan_project envelope, checklist, coverage, review_candidates, suggest_fix, explain_rule/standard, bootstrap composer, detect_native_wrappers, list_rules/propose_*, attest/vpat/conformance). `suggest_fix` and `detect_native_wrappers` came back clean — fix text is context-aware, wrapper detection returns honest empties. Seven categories of gap across the other eight tools: composite-headline counts, envelope/meta honesty, onboarding-tool shape, filter/correctness bugs, rule-metadata drift against spec, review-finder reason-text enrichment, new-finder coverage, and conformance-claim honesty. Each has a concrete evidence anchor from the run. Dispatch in parallel — independent files.

Composite-headline counts (doctrine: "composite headline counts are dishonest — must count one kind of thing"):

- [x] **V1-COMP-PLAN-GUIDANCE-FIXES** (82f41e5) `plan.guidanceFixesAvailable` replaced with structured `plan.fixesByClass: { mechanical, guidance, runtimeOnly, verifyInSource }` — each key counts one kind of thing per the doctrine; `mechanicalEditsAvailable` retained as an orthogonal "actionable inline edit" counter. `next-step.ts` `fixable` now reads from `fixesByClass.mechanical + .guidance` to preserve prose-only-suggestion coverage that the old composite incidentally provided. New `tests/unit/output/agent-response/build-plan.test.ts` covers per-lane emission; `fixesByClass` conditional-spread when violations > 0.
- [x] **V1-COMP-CHECKLIST-MANUAL-REVIEW** (b60abc5) `checklist.summary.manualReviewRequired` dropped — the canonical dishonest-composite in `docs/kb/architecture/ai-first-consumer.md`. Consumers should read the existing `summary.actionable` + `summary.untargetedCriteria` split (also in `summary.headline`). No `ChecklistSummary`/`ChecklistResponse` type existed (ad-hoc shape at assembly site); integration tests adjusted to derive the total from the split when needed; no non-test consumer read the field. CHANGELOG Breaking entry added under `[Unreleased]`.
- [x] **V1-COMP-BOOTSTRAP-SCAN-TOTAL** Landed (46fd94b). `ScanSubset` now carries `violationsCount` + `notesCount` (both required) and present-when-meaningful `fixesByClass: { mechanical, guidance, runtimeOnly, verifyInSource }` + `mechanicalEditsAvailable` — all forwarded verbatim from upstream `scan_project` plan. `totalFindings` removed (bootstrap was the sole caller; split is strictly more information — CHANGELOG `### Breaking Changes` under `[Unreleased]`). `buildNextStep` / `buildNextStepStructured` read `violationsCount`; headline prose reads "N violation(s) + M note(s)". New focused test asserts per-lane numeric + lanes sum to `violationsCount` invariant.

Envelope honesty (doctrine: "verbose meta is scan-confidence signal; warnings are the silent-failure channel"):

- [x] **V1-ENV-LIST-RULES-BARE** (a83fe44) `list_rules` response now carries `meta: { rulesTotal, rulesMatched, standardsLoaded, standards: string[] }` + `nextStep` prose + `nextStepStructured: { tool: "scan_project", args }` (filter pre-fills `standard`). No metaMode — list_rules is pure registry enumeration, no scan-confidence delta across calls. Next-step builder extracted into `src/mcp/list-rules-next-step.ts` to keep `tools.ts` under line budget. Four new tests cover meta + nextStep branches (filter vs no-filter). Filter echo + matchedOf already landed in c868a39.
- [x] **V1-ENV-COVERAGE-NO-ANALYSIS-COVERAGE** (128c247) `coverage` tool response now carries `analysisCoverage` + `warnings` at the top level, unconditional (not gated behind metaMode). `parseFilesWithDiagnostics` swapped in so discovery diagnostics flow; `buildAnalysisCoverage` reused from scan-assembly; local `countFilesByExtension` helper mirrors the private one so the same `extensions_skipped_no_parser` + Tailwind-undercount codes fire on parity with `scan_project`. `src/reports/coverage.ts` untouched (kept data-pure). 4 new unit tests cover warning-fires / warning-absent / scanned-zero-files / cross-tool parity.
- [x] **V1-ENV-BOOTSTRAP-STRIPS-LIMITATIONS** (fa5220c) Optional `limitations?: readonly string[]` added to `ScanSubset`; `extractScanSubset` copies it verbatim from upstream `scan_project`'s `plan.limitations` (conditional-spread — omitted when upstream emits no prose). The bootstrap composer's response now surfaces the static-analysis caveat to CI readers pasting the `ciSnippet`. `buildCiSnippet()` deliberately unmodified — the optional CI-caveat expansion overlaps V1-ENV-CISNIPPET-DRY-RUN-DISHONEST which is scoped separately.
- [x] **V1-ENV-CISNIPPET-DRY-RUN-DISHONEST** Landed (65a9adf). `buildCiSnippet` takes `{ baselineExists, writeBaseline, baselineWrittenByThisCall }`; handler probes `${root}/${BASELINE_FILENAME}` via `existsSync`. Three content branches: (1) baseline on disk → plain `--baseline check` (steady state); (2) baseline absent + this call wrote it → `--baseline check` with a `# commit .ra11y-baseline.json before pushing` comment; (3) pure dry-run (no baseline, writeBaseline:false, or writeBaseline failure) → prepend `# create ... first:` + a `- run: npx @ra11y/core --baseline create` step before `--baseline check`. Top-level response shape unchanged (`ciSnippet: string`). Three new tests in `tool-bootstrap.test.ts`.
- [x] **V1-WARN-TOKEN-TRUNCATION-NO-LIMIT-ECHO** Landed (992cab2). `ScanWarningDetails` union extended with `response_token_budget_truncated?: { requestedLimit, effectiveLimit }`; new `tokenBudgetTruncatedDetailsField()` helper returns a spreadable fragment. `scan-project-budget.ts` + `scan-diff-budget.ts` thread pre-density page size (`requestedLimit`) and post-density page size (`effectiveLimit`) into `mergeBudgetedFields`. New `scan-budget.ts` module extracted the `scan` tool's merge to stay under line budget; `tools.ts` delegates to `mergeScanTokenBudget`. Tests cover truncation-fires + omission on default-budget passthrough. Shape follows ADR 0023 (option 2 — bare `warnings[]` stable, structured payload under `warningsDetails`).
- [x] **V1-META-RULES-EVALUATED-COVERAGE-DRIFT** (92ba8cc) `buildPerRuleCoverage` now iterates every active+filter-passing rule (not just tracker entries) and synthesizes a project-scoped branch for rules whose only lifecycle is `afterProject` (`focus/outline-visible`, `wrapper/drift`). `rulesEvaluated` derives from `perRuleCoverage.length` so the two cannot drift again. Zero-file scans honestly surface "no files were scanned; project-scoped rule had nothing to evaluate" instead of silent absence. Reused existing `"low"` confidence + structured `reason` (no enum widening needed). Four new invariant tests + one existing test updated. CHANGELOG `### Changed` entry added.

Filter/correctness bugs:

- [x] **V1-LIST-RULES-MATCHED-COUNT** (c868a39) `list_rules` handler now emits `matchedOf: { total, matched }` always and `filter: { standard }` via conditional-spread — agents can distinguish no-filter / no-op filter / narrowing filter from the response alone. Four new unit tests cover all three states plus the shape contract (`"filter" in data` absent when unset, `matched === total` on wcag22, `matched < total` on wcag21). Filter semantics under the current 53 rule set: `wcag22:` → 53 (universal), `wcag21:` → 50 (genuine subset), `section508`/`en301549` → 0 via direct-prefix match (their coverage flows through `equivalentTo`, not this handler's `startsWith` filter — the `matched: 0` is now a structured signal rather than an empty list; filter logic itself unchanged). Envelope additions (`meta`, `nextStep`) left for V1-ENV-LIST-RULES-BARE.
- [x] **V1-PROPOSE-BASELINE-DUP-FINDINGID** (acd6e61) `proposed[]` deduped by `findingId` — first-seen wins, order preserved. `tallyReasons` now runs over the deduped list so `counts.unclassified` and the `proposed.length == sum(counts)` invariant stay honest. Two new unit tests: three `<button title="…">` on one line collapse to one entry (shared findingId via identical line-context hash); invariant assertion that no two `proposed[]` entries share a `findingId` under any scan shape. No engine or findingId-generation changes.
- [x] **V1-SESSION-WRAPPERS-CWD-BLEED** (3b89f8c) Honesty patch (option b) shipped: `McpSession.configure()` records the anchor cwd (explicit `cwd` param preferred, falls back to `process.cwd()`) on first wrapper-landing call — first-write-wins. `session.sessionWrappersMismatchCwd(root)` + new `session_wrappers_configured_for_different_cwd` warning code wired into `scan_project` and `list_suppressions`. `sessionConfigure` schema gains optional `cwd` param; `docs/mcp/tool-reference.md` updated. Full correctness filter (option a — drop wrappers when roots mismatch) deferred as follow-up; 13 test-file cascade was past the turn budget.

Rule-metadata drift against spec (CLAUDE.md §3 invariant 4 — every rule cites every criterion it checks):

- [x] **V1-RULE-HIDDEN-FOCUS-ADD-2.1.1** (84a57b2 + 9f52268) `satisfies` widened to `[wcag22:4.1.2, wcag21:4.1.2, wcag22:2.1.1, wcag21:2.1.1]`; equivalence rollup now credits the rule under section508:2.1.1 + en301549:9.2.1.1. Header cites both SCs (Name-Role-Value + Keyboard); rationale paragraph added for the keyboard-silence failure mode. No behavior change — pure declarative coverage widening.
- [x] **V1-SC-2.4.11-AUTOMATABLE-MISMATCH** (3697e35 + 2079bb4) SC 2.4.11 retagged `automatable: "manual"` → `"partial"`; rationale comment cites `focus/not-obscured` as the auto-checking rule. No rule/satisfies changes (rule was already correct). wcag21/Section 508/EN 301 549 unchanged — 2.4.11 is wcag22-only. KB `docs/kb/wcag/2-4-11.md` + coverage matrix regenerated.
- [x] **V1-RULE-HEADING-H1-OVERCLAIM** (e2aad8f + 60efbba) Softened `docs.description`, `docs.rationale`, missing-h1 `suggestion`, and the file-header block comment: dropped "must have an `<h1>`" overclaim; new wording frames SC 1.3.1 as non-mandating but surfaces the missing-top-of-document-landmark concern and points at the disable pragma for fragment/layout cases. Detection logic / satisfies / severity / message strings unchanged.
- [x] **V1-RULE-AUTOCOMPLETE-MODAL** (575862b + 5d27ca8) `docs.description` and `docs.rationale` rewritten to cite SC 1.3.5 AA directly and reference the 53 input-purpose tokens; per-finding `message` + `suggestion` strings also updated to must-modality. Trigger-citation from the earlier 4ae6c7a commit preserved via merge-resolution ("appears to collect information about the user but has no autocomplete attribute — {describeTrigger}. WCAG 2.2 SC 1.3.5 (AA) requires …"). Detection unchanged; `satisfies` unchanged.

Review-finder reason-text enrichment:

- [x] **V1-FP-TIMING-CITE-DURATION** (d398092) `reason` now carries the call's duration argument (`this._config.interval`, `emulatedDuration`, etc.) and enclosing function/method name via text-level lookback. Honest omission when unknown — no guessed names. 11 new tests, zero detection change. Commit oversize driven by biome cognitive-complexity decomposition helpers.
- [x] **V1-FP-IMAGES-OF-TEXT-SVG-DATA-URI** (a2b8ee0) Additive note when `<img src>` is `data:image/svg+xml,...` and decoded payload contains neither `<text` nor `<tspan` — "text-baked-in concern is provably lower, but verify the SVG isn't rendering text glyphs directly in path data." Base64 SVGs skipped (not inspected). 6 new tests covering svg-with-text/svg-with-tspan/non-svg/base64/JSX parity.
- [x] **V1-FP-MULTIPLE-WAYS-CITE-COUNTS** (5afba2d) `reason` appends nav-signal counts (`<nav>`, `<a>`, `<input type="search">`, breadcrumb, sitemap link) derived by `summarizeHtmlSignals` / `summarizeJsxSignals`. Preserves existing SPA-shell annotation after em-dash. 4 new tests; zero detection change.

New review finders (detection gaps — zero candidates on strong static evidence):

- [x] **V1-FINDER-POINTER-GESTURES** (349e489 + b57c2f8) Two new detection paths on `src/review/finders/pointer-input.ts`: (a) co-occurrence — when `touchstart`+`touchmove` OR `pointerdown`+`pointermove` both appear as `addEventListener` in one file, emits one path-based candidate anchored at the first `touchmove`/`pointermove` offset and dedupes against the standalone candidate at the same location; (b) name-pattern — left-word-boundary regex `/\b(swipe|pan|pinch|rotate)/i` matched against file basename + class/function/const/let/var identifier declarations, skipping comment/JSDoc lines. `swipe.js`, `class PinchZoom`, `function rotate(`, `const panHandler =`, `panelSlide` fire; `span`, `planet`, `expanded`, `plan` stay silent. Confidence: `"high"` on the deterministic pair pattern, `"medium"` on name-pattern hits. 29 new tests + 6 fixtures (3 bad, 3 good). Surface-don't-suppress — no thresholds, no heuristic suppression.
- [x] **V1-FINDER-FOCUS-ORDER-NEGATIVE-TABINDEX** (802013d + ed2eca8 + 8197d84) New `src/review/finders/focus-order.ts` fires on `tabindex="-1"` (string or numeric expression) applied to natively-focusable controls (`<input>`, `<textarea>`, `<select>`, `<button>`, `<a href>`) OR elements with widget roles (`button`, `link`, `textbox`, `checkbox`, `radio`, `combobox`, `slider`, `spinbutton`, `switch`). Walks both HTML and TSX/JSX ASTs. `<a>` without `href` correctly skipped (not natively focusable); `<div>` without widget role skipped. Confidence `"high"` — deterministic predicate. Reason text frames the a11y-dialog-pattern vs. accidental-suppression question. Six fixtures (3 bad, 3 good across HTML + TSX), 17 unit tests, KB coverage matrix regenerated.

Conformance-claim honesty (the surfaces agents use to decide "should I claim this?"):

- [x] **V1-CONFORMANCE-LIMITATIONS-FIELD-MISSING** (d53eab0 + 72fd5e2 + 718ef76) `ConformanceBlocker.status` widens with `"undetermined"`; `ConformanceBlockerReason` gains `"runtime-evidence-required"`; top-level `limitations: string[]` emitted on the conformance statement (present-when-non-empty, omitted when zero undetermined criteria). New `src/reports/runtime-evidence-criteria.ts` — spec-derived enumeration of 22 WCAG SCs (1.4.3, 1.4.10, 1.4.11, 1.4.12, 1.4.13, 2.1.1, 2.4.3, 2.4.6, 2.4.7, 2.5.7, 2.5.8, 3.3.7, 3.3.8, 3.3.9 + WCAG 2.1 aliases) with inline comments citing the normative basis for each. Runtime-dependent SCs that reach the builder with zero fail-evidence + no attested/sampled source now emit `status: "undetermined"` / `reason: "runtime-evidence-required"` instead of the silent-miss `status: "pass", reason: "no-evidence"`. Attestations still clear the blocker; static failures still route to `"failing"`. Markdown renderer emits a `## Limitations` section when present, omits when absent. Ledger `EvidenceStatus` unchanged — undetermined lives only at claim level. CHANGELOG Breaking entry added.
- [x] **V1-VPAT-RUNTIME-ONLY-PARTIALLY-SUPPORTS** (e807012) Runtime-evidence-required criteria (22 SCs from the spec-derived `RUNTIME_EVIDENCE_REQUIRED_CRITERIA`) now route to `conformance: "Not Evaluated"` on clean scans with no fresh attestation, instead of collapsing into `"Partially Supports"` via `automatable: "partial"` metadata. Fresh `pass`/`fail`/`n/a` attestations release the criterion to its automatable default; pending attestations ignored; proven static failures still route to `"Does Not Support"` (conservative default — honest negative evidence preserved). Attestations threaded through a new optional `attestations` field on `VpatBuildOptions`; MCP `vpat` tool forwards its already-loaded durable store. **2.5.3 / 2.4.9 left on the static path** — both are `automatable: "partial"` with real rule coverage (`label-in-name`, `link-purpose`) and NOT in `RUNTIME_EVIDENCE_REQUIRED_CRITERIA`; inventing a parallel manual-review-only constant would create two sources of truth for the runtime-vs-manual distinction. 5 new unit tests + 1 MCP integration test; `docs/certification/vpat-mapping.md` updated.
- [x] **V1-ATTEST-EVIDENCE-SOURCE-UNSTRUCTURED** (fb789f0 + 539fbb2) `evidenceSource: "runtime_tool" | "manual_review" | "human_study" | "declaration"` now required on the TS type + on write (`requireValidRecord` rejects missing); absent on read coerces to `"declaration"` for backward compat, unrecognized values skip the record. Optional `toolName` / `runUrl` / `observedAt` (ISO-8601 via new `src/utils/iso-timestamp.ts` zero-dep validator) conditional-spread at every surface. Pragma-derived records stamp `"manual_review"` unconditionally. Attestation summary splits `totalCount` + `bySource` (no composite headline). Downstream: `conformance_statement` markdown + `list_attestations` + VPAT remarks cell all surface evidenceSource + toolName (present-when-meaningful). Two commits; 29 files touched.

Generator-script footgun (tooling, surfaced repeatedly by agents running /fix-drift inside worktrees):

- [x] **V1-TOOL-KB-GEN-PRESERVE-HANDWRITTEN** (b85940b) Option (c1) landed — the unlink decision extracted into a pure `chooseFilesToUnlink` helper in `scripts/generate-rule-kb.ts`. Preserves `index.md` / `README.md`, slugs listed in exported `HAND_AUTHORED_KB_SLUGS` (`fix-suggestion-audit`, `coverage`), and any filename already in the expected-write set. Script body wrapped in `if (import.meta.main)` per repo convention so tests import the helper without triggering regeneration. New `tests/unit/scripts/generate-rule-kb.test.ts` — 9 cases covering index/README preservation, stale-slug removal, overwrite path, both hand-authored filenames from the bug report, allowlist coverage, full-wipe case. Allowlist mirrors `check-kb-drift.ts` `knownRuleExtras` / `knownExtras` sets from the opposite direction; follow-up to hoist the shared constant noted but out of scope.

### v1.0.0 — ninth-pass four-repo field test (2026-04-22)

Source: 20-agent parallel field test across four external repos (design-system MDX/Astro; static-site-generator with Liquid + kramdown; vanilla-HTML/CSS/JS pedagogical bundles; bulk-template catalog with 174 jQuery-era marketing templates). Five angles per repo: full-repo meta, per-surface drift / per-repo specialty, rule-accuracy audit, fix-suggestion quality, edge-case + structured-error hygiene. All items below are net-new after dedup against Track Q3/Q4/Q5/Q6 cross-repo shared entries and all eighth-pass Track V items. Regression audits (symptoms still reproduce on commits previously marked `[x]`) are filed as net-new items named `…-REGRESSION` — they investigate whether the fix shipped narrower than the spec, whether a stale subprocess served a pre-fix bundle, or whether a sibling rule re-introduced the symptom.

Parser / language coverage:

- [x] **V1-PARSER-LESS** No `.less` parser. website-templates skips 672 `.less` files silently (startbootstrap-agency-1.0.2, startbootstrap-grayscale-1.0.3, startbootstrap-sb-admin-2-1.0.5 bower_components); Bootstrap 3-era design tokens + theme overrides invisible to every rule. `scan_file` on a `.less` file returns `file-unsupported`. Fix: add a lightweight Less parser that shares the CSS AST (like the SCSS parser — variables inlined when literal, nested selectors flattened one level, `@import`/`@mixin`/`@include` stripped). Add `.less` to `PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS` with `.css` alias via `extensionMatches`. (repro: wt-03 §files, wt-01 §skippedByExtension)
- [ ] **V1-PARSER-SVG-TITLE** No SVG parser / no SVG-accessibility rules. website-templates corpus carries 222 `.svg` files; `scan_file` on `fontawesome-webfont.svg` returns `file-unsupported` and the remediation list does not include `.svg`. Inline SVGs inside `<button>` are handled by existing button-name SVG-title traversal (V1-DETECT-BUTTON-NAME-SVG) but standalone SVGs never get `semantics/svg-title-missing` / `aria/svg-role-img-name`. Fix: register `.svg` as a parseable extension, implement a minimal SVG parser (reuses HTML tokenizer, maps `<title>` / `<desc>` / `role="img"` / `aria-label`), and add a rule pair for missing accessible name when SVG is a standalone asset vs. a decorative `<use>` child. Pairs with V1-PARSER-LESS for parser-registry widening. (repro: wt-05 §gaps)
- [ ] **V1-HTML-PARSER-MARKDOWN-URL-AUTOLINK** HTML parser treats bare Markdown URL `<https://…>` autolinks as an element open tag and emits `partialParseFiles[].reason: "Unclosed <https:> element"` on README files parsed as HTML residue (bootstrap `README.md`). Tightens `Q-SHARED-MD-DISPATCH-UNIFY` — the routing is correct, the tokenizer's recovery is wrong. Fix: tokenizer must reject impossible element names (`https:`, `http:`, any scheme-shaped token with a `:` in a non-attribute-value position) as element openers and emit them as text. Add the pair: drop the false-positive `semantics/heading-hierarchy` "Document has no <h1>" that cascades from the truncated parse when the README's top-level `# Bootstrap` is Markdown syntax that never reached the HTML residue pass. (repro: bootstrap-05 §B1-B2)
- [ ] **V1-SCSS-CONTRAST-VARIABLES-ZERO-OUTPUT** `.scss` files containing `$variable:` declarations with no same-rule color-pairs produce `findings: []` with `perRuleCoverage.contrast/minimum.coverageConfidence: "high"` — telemetry reads "clean on this file" when the reality is "no color pairs resolvable to literals." Reproduces on every Font Awesome SCSS file, every `_variables.scss`, every theme-token partial. Doctrine: zero-output success is ambiguous failure. Fix: when a `.scss` file contains `$variable:` declarations AND the SCSS preprocessor's variable-substitution pass produced zero literal-color usages in downstream selectors, emit `warnings: ["scss_unresolved_variables"]` + downgrade `coverageConfidence` to `"medium"` with `reason: "scss variables unresolved — scan the compiled CSS output for full contrast coverage"`. Pairs with V1-PARSER-SCSS (closed) — the parser works; this is the honest telemetry on the zero-output case. (repro: wt-03 §bug-2, §bug-4)
- [ ] **V1-CSS-CONTRAST-VAR-ROOT-RESOLUTION** `contrast/minimum` does not resolve `var(--token)` references when the token is declared at `:root` and consumed in a descendant rule. `:root { --fg:#fff; --bg:#fff } .card { color: var(--fg); background: var(--bg) }` silently passes; `.card { color:#fff; background:#fff }` fires 1.00:1. Every design-system CSS file uses this pattern; silent miss on real low-contrast designs. Fix: CSS parser or contrast rule resolves `var()` via a two-pass walk — collect `:root { --name: <literal> }` in pass 1, substitute at contrast-rule evaluation in pass 2. Out-of-`:root` custom properties stay unresolved (honest: the cascade can override). Doctrine: silent miss on common pattern. (repro: 50p50d-03 §bug-1 with var-test.css repro)
- [ ] **V1-CSS-CONTRAST-CASCADE-INHERITED** `contrast/minimum` requires both `color` and `background` on the same rule. Real-world CSS sets `body { color: #fff }` then declares `.btn { background: lightblue }` — the button text is `#fff` on `#ADD8E6` (1.53:1, error) but rule never fires. Fix: rule walks the ancestor-selector chain for a missing `color` (fall back to nearest `html` / `body` / matching-container color), OR adds a review-candidate tier "color not declared on this rule but background is — verify the cascaded foreground contrast." Pairs with V1-CSS-CONTRAST-VAR-ROOT-RESOLUTION — same class of cascade-awareness gap. Doctrine: silent miss on real-world cascade pattern. (repro: 50p50d-03 §bug-2, form-input-wave 1.53:1)
- [ ] **V1-PARSER-ERB** `.erb` extension returns `file-unsupported` (strict allow-list gate) even though `.html` files mixing ERB + Liquid parse cleanly (identical content, different filename). Jekyll `test/source/_layouts/default.html` mixes `<%= @page.author %>` ERB with Liquid `{{ ... }}` and scans fine; `test/fixtures/*.erb` errors out. Rails views + Middleman templates + Jekyll `lib/theme_template/*.md.erb` scaffolds invisible to every rule. Fix: add `.erb` to `PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS`, route through the HTML parser's template-literal pass (ERB `<%= … %>` / `<% … %>` treated as literal text, same as Liquid per `template_files_parsed_as_literal`). The asymmetric allow-list is the bug, not parser capability. (repro: jekyll-02 §bug-6)
- [ ] **V1-TEMPLATE-CLASSIFIER-MARKDOWN-PROSE-FALSE-POSITIVE** `erb-or-ejs` / `jinja-or-liquid` template-directive detection fires on Markdown code-block examples — Jekyll `docs/_docs/troubleshooting.md:261` (a fenced code block quoting `<%= Time.now %>` in prose) populates the `erb-or-ejs` bucket, yet zero `.erb` files reached the parser (all 14 `.erb` files in `skippedByExtension`). Same shape fires `jinja-or-liquid` on `History.markdown` (release-notes prose). The token scanner treats code-fence text as live directives. Fix: when detecting template-family directives in a `.md`/`.markdown` file, skip content inside triple-backtick fences AND inline-code spans before running the directive matcher. Pairs with Q4-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-CLASSIFIER-DRIFT (closed) — same classifier, different false-evidence source. (repro: jekyll-01 §bug-4, jekyll-02 §bug-4)

Rule correctness / detection:

- [ ] **V1-SCAN-PROJECT-LAYOUT-FILES-DROPPED** `scan_project` omits findings for `site/src/layouts/BaseLayout.astro` (4 findings per `scan_file`) and `DocsLayout.astro` (5 findings) across every paged offset on bootstrap. Files are parseable, not in `scannedBuildArtifacts`, not in `parseErrorFiles`, not in `partialParseFiles` — they simply do not appear in the 110-file list despite `filesByExtension` counting 104 `.astro` files scanned. `scan` with explicit `paths` including the same files returns the 4 findings; `scan_file` does too. Silent drop in `scan_project`'s aggregation path; no warning emitted. Doctrine: zero-output success at the per-file level. Fix: instrument `scan_project`'s per-file eligibility walk to identify which files produced findings per `scan_file` / `scan` that did not make it into the aggregated response, and surface the drop via a structured `warnings: ["scan_project_findings_aggregation_drop", {fileCount, sample}]` until root cause lands. (repro: bootstrap-02 §bug-2)
- [ ] **V1-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-FILES-EVALUATED-OFF-BY-ONE** `perRuleCoverage["motion/pause-stop-hide"].filesEvaluated` reports 105 on 50projects50days where every other HTML+JS+MD rule reports 104 (52 html + 51 js + 1 md = 104). One extra evaluation is counted somewhere in the rule's eligibility pass. Low severity (does not affect findings); silent drift in scan-confidence telemetry. Fix: audit the rule's applies/eligibility calculation against the shared file pool; add an invariant test that every rule with the same `appliesTo` set reports the same `filesEvaluated` on a given scan. (repro: 50p50d-01 §bug-4)
- [ ] **V1-RULE-NAVIGATION-HREF-VOID-RENAME** `navigation/href-javascript-void` rule name promises detection of `href="javascript:void(0)"` only but also fires on bare `href="#"` (Jekyll `navigation.md` produces 50 findings, all `href="#"`, zero `javascript:void`). The rule ID is a visible identifier in `list_rules`, `explain_rule`, `concentration.file`, and pragma-suppress syntax; the misleading name mis-budgets agent triage. Rename to `navigation/href-placeholder` or `navigation/href-empty-or-void` (spans empty, `#`, `javascript:void(0)`, `javascript:;`). Breaking — pragmas referencing the old name must remain valid via an alias table for one release. Pair with CHANGELOG `### Breaking Changes` entry. Per CLAUDE.md §12 semver: rule rename is major unless an alias mechanism exists. (repro: jekyll-01 §bug-2)
- [ ] **V1-HEADING-HIERARCHY-MARKDOWN-SELF-CONTRADICTION** `semantics/heading-hierarchy` fires "Document has no <h1>" on bootstrap `README.md` parsed as HTML residue — even though `analysisCoverage.hints` prose says "Markdown files parsed as HTML residue: embedded HTML, image alt-text, and kramdown IAL are checked; link text, heading hierarchy, and prose are not." One of the two is wrong. The README has a Markdown `# Bootstrap` ATX heading the HTML parser never saw. Fix: skip `semantics/heading-hierarchy` (and any document-scope rule — `semantics/landmark-main`, `document/page-titled`) when the file extension is `.md`/`.markdown` AND the scan is using the HTML-residue pass. Update the hint to name exactly which rules are skipped. Pairs with V1-HTML-PARSER-MARKDOWN-URL-AUTOLINK (fixes the upstream parse-error vector); both should land together. (repro: bootstrap-05 §B1)
- [ ] **V1-LIQUID-TEMPLATE-EXPRESSION-AS-SOLE-CHILD-REASON-ENRICHMENT** `<h1>{{ page.title }}</h1>`, `<title>{{ page.title }}</title>`, `<button>{{ t.submit }}</button>`, `<a aria-label="{{ … }}">` — rules that flag empty/non-descriptive content emit `severity: error` with generic fix text when the only child is a Liquid/Handlebars/ERB template expression. Q4-DOCUMENT-PAGE-TITLED-LIQUID-STRIP (closed) handled `document/page-titled`; the same enrichment is missing on `semantics/empty-heading`, `semantics/button-name`, `forms/non-empty-label`, and several other visible-text rules. Fix: add a shared helper `hasOnlyTemplateExpressionChild(node)` that detects the "sole child is a `{{…}}` or `{%…%}` or `<%…%>` or `<%=…%>` expression" pattern. Every visible-text rule runs it before emitting; when true, reason text appends "note: the only child is a template expression — rendered text determines conformance. Verify at a rendered output or suppress at source with `<!-- ra11y-disable wcag22:<sc> -->` if the interpolation is trusted." Surface-don't-suppress — finding still emits, severity unchanged, reason enriched. (repro: jekyll-02 §bug-1, §bug-3; jekyll-04 §bug-3)
- [ ] **V1-FRONTMATTER-AS-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-TRIGGER** `template_files_parsed_as_literal` warning does not fire on files carrying a YAML frontmatter fence (`^---\n…\n---\n`) when no Liquid/Handlebars/ERB tokens are detected. Jekyll `test/source/properties.html` (frontmatter + 1-line body, no Liquid) returns `warnings: ["no_config_found"]` only. An agent told "no template risk" for this file will miss that the `---` fence itself was literal in the parser's eyes and would (in a richer file) corrupt the HTML parse. Fix: frontmatter fence presence is an independent template-substrate signal — emit `template_files_parsed_as_literal` whenever the file starts with a `---\n…\n---\n` block OR a Liquid/Handlebars/ERB directive is detected. Pairs with V1-TEMPLATE-CLASSIFIER-MARKDOWN-PROSE-FALSE-POSITIVE — both tighten what counts as template evidence. (repro: jekyll-02 §bug-3)
- [ ] **V1-LINK-DESCRIPTIVE-TEXT-TITLE-DUPLICATE** `src/rules/navigation/link-descriptive-text.ts::hasAccessibleNameOverrideHtml` treats any non-empty `title=` as an accessible-name override, even when the title value duplicates the visible text. `<a href="#" title="read more">read more</a>` escapes the generic-phrase match because `title` is present — but the title adds nothing the text didn't. Fix: when `title`/`aria-label` equals (case-insensitive, trimmed) the element's visible text, don't treat it as an override; continue to the generic-phrase check. Apply the same fix on the JSX path (`hasAccessibleNameOverrideJsx`). Mechanical scope. (repro: wt-04 §B1, `learn-educational/index.html:109`)
- [ ] **V1-PAGINATION-SENSORY-LINE-DRIFT** 1.3.3 sensory-characteristic finder cites `docs/_docs/pagination.md:134` for the phrase "See below" whose actual position is line 136 — the finder points at the containing block-open rather than the text run. Same class of bug as Q4-LIQUID-LINE-DRIFT-SASS-SELECTOR (closed) but on Markdown body-text extraction. Fix: line-mapping must point at the text-node's own line, not the enclosing block. Add fixture `tests/fixtures/real-world/jekyll-pagination-sensory-line-drift/` asserting the cited line exactly matches the text. (repro: jekyll-03 §B4)
- [ ] **V1-LIVE-REGION-RUNTIME-MUTATION-CANDIDATE** No review candidate fires for `wcag22:4.1.3 Status Messages` when JS mutates visible text via `innerHTML = …` / `.textContent = …` / `.innerText = …` on elements that are not declared as live regions. Canonical cases: `insect-catch-game` score + timer updated via `innerHTML` every second; `dad-jokes` joke swapped in via `innerHTML` after `fetch`; `event-keycodes` rewrites `<div class="key">` at every keydown. Pairs with Q3-RULE-LIVE-REGION-PRE-EXISTENCE (closed — detects declared live regions hidden on initial render); this is the inverse — elements mutated at runtime without a live-region declaration. Fix: new review finder `review/live-region-runtime-mutation-missing` — scan `.js`/`.tsx` for `elem.innerHTML = …`, `elem.textContent = …`, `elem.innerText = …`, `elem.insertAdjacentHTML(…)` where `elem` resolves to an HTML element that lacks `role="status"` / `role="alert"` / `aria-live="…"`. Confidence `medium` (cross-file identifier resolution is lossy; agent reads to verify). (repro: 50p50d-02 §gaps, insect-catch-game + dad-jokes)

Response shape / honesty:

- [ ] **V1-META-COUNTS-BY-SURFACE-REGRESSION** `meta.countsBySurface` is `null` on every 2026-04-22 scan across all four target repos despite commit `8c954816 feat(mcp): surface cross-surface count drift via meta.countsBySurface`. The invariant the commit advertises ("cross-surface count drift is visible") is unverified in practice — consumers either assume "no drift" from `null` or add defensive null-checks. Fix: determine whether the field ships only when drift is detected (in which case: rename to `meta.countsBySurfaceDriftDetected?: {…}` + populate; document the semantic) OR ships always with `{scan, coverage, checklist}` numeric triples. Pair with a contract test that calls `scan_project` + `coverage` + `checklist` on the same cwd and asserts `countsBySurface` reflects the three responses' counters. Doctrine: ambiguous field shapes are dishonest. (repro: bootstrap-01 §gaps, bootstrap-02 §files-sampled, jekyll-01 §gaps, 50p50d-01 §bug-2, wt-01 §gaps)
- [ ] **V1-FIX-SAFETY-CONSTANT-FIELD** Every finding in every scan surfaces `"fix": { "safety": "safe" }` including findings with `fixClass: "runtime-only"` (e.g. `tooltip/dismissable`, `motion/pause-stop-hide`). A field that never varies conveys no signal; worse, "safe" on a runtime-only fix is arguably wrong (static analysis cannot prove safety without runtime context). Fix: either drop `fix.safety` entirely, or make it vary with `fixClass` (`"safe"` for mechanical-with-unique-oldText, `"needs-review"` for verify-in-source, omit for runtime-only/guidance). Pairs with the present-when-meaningful doctrine rule. (repro: bootstrap-04 §B3 / §S7)
- [ ] **V1-FIX-DESCRIPTION-PRESENCE-INCONSISTENCY** Same rule, same scan: some findings carry `fix.description` inline while sibling findings of the same ruleId carry `fixDescriptionRef.hash` only. Observed: `motion/pause-stop-hide` on bootstrap appears twice — once with a full description, once with safety-only. An agent cannot predict whether to join with `referenceGuide.fixDescriptions[ruleId][hash]` or read inline. Fix: make emission deterministic — ALL findings for a rule in a single response use the same shape (either all inline, or all hoisted + ref). The hoist threshold (V1-SIZE-RESPONSE-BUDGET-DENSITY, 402e546) operates per-rule; extend it to always hoist when the rule has ≥1 finding in the response, OR always inline when only one finding exists. Add `nextStep` on findings-with-ref pointing at the referenceGuide path so agents don't need to learn the convention. (repro: bootstrap-04 §B4, jekyll-04 §shape-issues)
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-PERCRITERION-CURSOR** `checklist` emits `perCriterionClipped: true` and `totalCandidates: 78` but the response carries 44 candidates with no `truncated`, no `nextOffset`, no per-criterion `clippedAt` cursor. An agent that counts the returned candidates and sees 44 < 78 has no way to fetch the other 34. Q2-CHECKLIST-LIMIT (closed) established pagination for the flat stream; the per-criterion clip is the orthogonal gap. Fix: when `perCriterionClipped: true`, emit either (a) `nextOffset` cursor that advances to the next criterion-batch OR (b) per-criterion `nextOffset` on each clipped criterion's entry OR (c) a single response-level `nextOffset` + `raiseMaxPerCriterionHint: N` so the caller can re-page with a higher `maxCandidatesPerCriterion`. Pick one; document the semantic; ship the cursor. Doctrine: ambiguous field shapes are dishonest. (repro: bootstrap-03 §bug-1, jekyll-03 §cross-surface, wt-02 §B1)
- [ ] **V1-SUPPRESS-DRY-RUN** `suppress` tool has no dry-run mode — the one call writes the pragma comment immediately. `apply_fix` defaults `dryRun: true`; `baseline` distinguishes `mode:"create"` vs `mode:"check"`. `suppress` is the only write tool without a preview path. Fix: add `dryRun?: boolean` input (default `false` to preserve current semantics, or default `true` to match the apply_fix pattern — pick one in the same spec). `dryRun:true` returns `{pragma, insertedLine, commentKind, meta}` without writing. Add `revertHint: "git checkout -- <file>"` (or content-hash pointer) to every write response. Doctrine: safe-by-default on write tools. (repro: 50p50d-05 §B3, §S2)
- [ ] **V1-SCAN-FILE-CWD-CONTAINMENT** `scan_file({cwd: "/x/a", path: "../b/file.html"})` escapes the cwd sandbox and scans the sibling file. `suppress` correctly rejects the same pattern with `{code: "path-escapes-cwd"}`. Asymmetric guard — the escape boundary must be uniform across every tool taking `(cwd, file|path)`. Read-only so low-severity, but breaks the user's mental model. Fix: reuse the `suppress` escape guard in `scan_file`, `suggest_fix`, `apply_fix` (and any other `(cwd, path)` tool). Mechanical. (repro: 50p50d-05 §B2)
- [ ] **V1-PROPOSE-BASELINE-RATIONALE-DEDUP** `propose_baseline` on a 156-file / 242-finding scan returns 76 KB (all on one line) — every entry inlines the same 128-char `rationale` string. Pattern already exists elsewhere (`review_candidates.prompts[criterionId].text` dedupes prose by criterion; `referenceGuide.fixDescriptions[ruleId][hash]` dedupes fix prose). Fix: hoist `rationale` to a top-level `rationales: { [reasonCode]: string }` map; per-entry carries `reason: "unclassified"` only. Add `limit` + `offset` pagination mirroring `scan_project`. Pairs with V1-PROPOSE-BASELINE-DUP-FINDINGID (closed) — same tool, different bloat vector. Doctrine: verbose meta is signal, but repeated identical prose is bloat. (repro: 50p50d-05 §B1)
- [ ] **V1-CONFORMANCE-SCOPE-FILES-CAP** `conformance_statement.scope.files[]` inlines every scanned file path with no cap — on a 156-file repo the array alone is ~16 KB before any content. V1-CERT-STATEMENT-SHAPE (closed) added `scope.files` per WCAG §5.3.1; the size is unbounded. Fix: always emit `scope.filesCount: number`; gate the path list behind `verboseScope?: boolean` input (parallel to existing `verboseMeta` gating). Pairs with V1-CERT-STATEMENT-SHAPE. (repro: 50p50d-05 §S3)
- [ ] **V1-SESSION-CONFIGURE-ALLOWWRITE-SILENT-DROP** `sessionConfigure({allowWrite: true})` with no `cwd` returns `{active: {allowWrite: false}}` — opt-in silently dropped with no warning. Follow-up call with `{allowWrite: true, cwd}` succeeds. The coupling between `cwd` and `allowWrite` is undocumented in `tools/list`. Canonical "zero-output success is ambiguous failure" at the session level. Fix: either accept `allowWrite:true` without `cwd` (session-scoped write gate), OR reject with `{error, code: "cwd-required-for-write-gate", remediation: …}`. Never return `allowWrite:false` as a silent success when the caller asked for `true`. Pairs with Q-SHARED-SCAN-FILE-ERROR-DISCRIMINATION doctrine. (repro: bootstrap-04 §B2)
- [ ] **V1-FINDING-ID-STABILITY** `findingId` hash varies across `apply_fix` before/after snapshots even when the finding is unchanged (same ruleId, line, column, message). 50p50d `password-generator` row 11 dry-run shows `373b28c71fbf` before → `c8bad95295ec` after at line 34 with identical evidence. `findingId` is load-bearing for suppress/dedup workflows; instability breaks agent dedup and cross-call correlation. Fix: audit the hash input — exclude ephemeral surrounding state (line-context that shifts when a preceding line changed), include only (ruleId, normalized-line-text, column, reasonKind). Add invariant test: `apply_fix` dry-run on finding A must not change `findingId` of unrelated finding B. Pairs with Q6-FINDINGID-COLLISION-SAMEFILE-SAMELINE (closed) — same primary-key discipline, different failure mode. (repro: 50p50d-04 §bug-3)
- [ ] **V1-ZERO-SCAN-PASS-RATE-SENTINEL** `checklist`/`coverage` on a zero-file scan emit `summary.automatedCoverage.automatedCriteriaPassRate: 100` because division `passed/total` on 0 files yields "100%". Response also carries `warnings: ["scanned_zero_files"]` (honest at the warnings layer), but an agent summing pass-rate dashboards counts the call as clean conformance. Fix: when `warnings` contains `scanned_zero_files` OR `filesScanned === 0`, omit `automatedCriteriaPassRate` entirely (present-when-meaningful) OR emit it as `null` with a `reason: "no-files-evaluated"` sibling. Never emit 100 on zero-file scans. Pairs with Q-SHARED-PASS-RATE-COMPOSITE (closed). (repro: jekyll-03 §G1)
- [ ] **V1-HINTS-STRUCTURED-CODE** `meta.analysisCoverage.hints` ships as prose-only strings carrying actionable information ("15 PascalCase components are opaque...", "Markdown files parsed as HTML residue: …") but requires string-parsing to act on. Compare with structured `detect_native_wrappers.emptyReason` discriminator — same signal, structured. Fix: each hint becomes `{ code, text, detail? }` so agents branch deterministically. Hint-code taxonomy: `opaque_components_present`, `markdown_html_residue`, `ssg_build_output_hint`, `scss_variables_unresolved`, etc. Additive — keep `text` for backwards compat. (repro: bootstrap-01 §shape-honesty)
- [ ] **V1-NEXTSTEP-DEDUP-META-VS-TOP-LEVEL** `scan_project` responses carry both `meta.nextStep` (nested) and `nextStep` at top level, plus `nextStepStructured` at top level and `meta.nextStepStructured` nested. Identical content in two places violates Q6 de-duplication doctrine. Fix: one pointer, one place — pick top-level for discoverability; drop the `meta` nesting. Minor. (repro: jekyll-05 §S5)
- [ ] **V1-BOOTSTRAP-BASELINE-NULL-SENTINEL** `bootstrap` emits `baseline: null` in dry-run mode (when `writeBaseline: false`). `null` here means "dry-run, not created" but is semantically distinguishable from "baseline-creation-failed" only by reading the tool docstring. Fix: conditional-spread — `...(writeBaseline ? { baseline } : {})` — plus a `warnings: ["baseline_dry_run"]` code when `writeBaseline:false`. Doctrine: ambiguous field shapes are dishonest. Pairs with V1-ENV-CISNIPPET-DRY-RUN-DISHONEST (closed). (repro: bootstrap-05 §S1)
- [ ] **V1-SCAN-DIFF-SHALLOW-CLONE-HINT** `scan_diff` with `HEAD~N` on a shallow clone errors with `{code: "unknown-ref", remediation: "Fetch the remote first if you're comparing against an origin branch."}`. The remediation addresses branch-shaped refs but not shallow-clone depth — PR-review agents using `actions/checkout@v4` default `fetch-depth: 1` hit this every time. Fix: extend remediation with "If this is a shallow clone (`git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` → true), run `git fetch --unshallow` or re-clone with `fetch-depth: 0`". Pair with a shallow-clone probe at error-construction time so the correct branch of advice is picked. (repro: bootstrap-05 §G2, 50p50d-05 §G3)
- [ ] **V1-TRUNCATED-FIELD-PRESENCE-CONTRACT** Small scans omit `truncated` / `totalFilesWithFindings` from the response; large scans populate both. An agent reading `truncated` can't tell "not truncated" from "field never emitted on this scan shape." Fix: always emit `truncated: false` + `totalFilesWithFindings: N` on `scan_project` responses regardless of truncation state. Conditional-spread is WRONG here (the emptiness is meaningful — "not truncated" is a load-bearing negative answer). Doctrine: present-when-meaningful requires the value to be meaningful; here, the negative is meaningful. (repro: wt-02 §B4)
- [ ] **V1-LIST-SUPPRESSIONS-RULES-EVALUATED-DRIFT** `list_suppressions` emits `meta.rulesEvaluated: {loaded: 75}` but the tool is documented as "does NOT run rules; fast enumeration only." The field name is misleading — no rules were evaluated. Fix: rename to `rulesLoaded: {count: 75}` OR drop from this tool's response (rule count is irrelevant to suppression enumeration). Doctrine: cross-tool field semantics must be honest; `rulesEvaluated` on a tool that evaluates no rules is dishonest. (repro: jekyll-05 §G3)
- [ ] **V1-DETECT-NATIVE-WRAPPERS-CWD-NOT-FOUND-REGRESSION** Q-SHARED-DETECT-NATIVE-WRAPPERS-CWD-NOT-FOUND closed turn 3 (`3b89f8c`-era) but both jekyll-05 and 50p50d-05 edge-case passes report the structured-error envelope working correctly. Regression-audit item: verify the guard is still present on `detect_native_wrappers` and add a regression-guard test asserting `{code: "cwd-not-found"}` on a non-existent path. If still holding, mark this item closed on first verify turn. (repro: jekyll-05 §matrix #6, 50p50d-05 §matrix #5)
- [ ] **V1-CONFIG-CONTEXT-TRIPLE-READOUT-REGRESSION** Q6-CONFIG-CONTEXT-TRIPLE-READOUT closed but all four 2026-04-22 repos reproduce the same triple-emission (`configSource: null` + `configSearchedFrom: <cwd>` + `configNote: "No ra11y.config found …"` all together, with `configSearchedFrom` echoing `cwd` exactly). Regression-audit item: verify the closure's conditional-spread on `configSearchedFrom` landed in the scan-assembly path and not only on a narrower surface. If the fix is narrower than the spec, widen to every MCP response that names the config-context triple. (repro: bootstrap-01 §confirmed-bugs-7, jekyll-01 §shape-honesty, 50p50d-02 §shape-honesty, wt-01 §S1)
- [ ] **V1-PROPOSED-CONFIG-ALIAS-DEPRECATION-WARN** Q3-PROPOSE-CONFIG-FIELD-DRIFT closed as "transition alias for one release" — `bootstrap` still ships both `suggestedConfig` and `proposedConfig` with identical contents across all 4 repos on 2026-04-22. Regression-audit item: confirm the alias window is still live (CHANGELOG `### Deprecated`) and emit a `warnings: ["proposed_config_deprecated_use_suggested_config"]` code so agents drop the alias immediately instead of paying the double-payload cost on every bootstrap call. If the deprecation window is overdue, this is the removal item. (repro: bootstrap-05 §S2, jekyll-05 §S2)
- [ ] **V1-WARNINGS-DETAILS-CROSS-SURFACE-REGRESSION** Q4-WARNING-DETAILS-CROSS-SURFACE-UNIFY closed, but field tests reproduce the asymmetry: only `response_token_budget_truncated` carries a `warningsDetails` payload; `no_config_found`, `scanned_build_artifacts_present`, `extensions_skipped_no_parser`, `parse_errors_present`, `source_language_unsupported`, `template_files_parsed_as_literal` fire as bare codes. The payload-per-code contract is 1/7. Regression-audit item: verify the unify commit did add payloads for every code listed, OR narrow the doctrine to "only codes carrying quantitative payload get warningsDetails" and document that in the schema. Doctrine must match behavior. (repro: jekyll-05 §B1, wt-01 §S4)

Fix-suggestion regressions:

- [ ] **V1-FIX-OLDTEXT-AMBIGUITY-LABEL-ADJACENT** `scan_project` per-finding `fix.oldText` for `forms/label-adjacent-unassociated` emits bare `"<label>"` for each of 5 sibling findings in `password-generator/index.html` — the same 4-char literal appears 5 times in the file, so an agent pasting through to `apply_fix` would clobber the first match every time regardless of intended line. Separately, `suggest_fix` correctly emits a multi-line unique-context `primary.edit.oldText` for the same rule. Two tools disagree on edit shape. Fix: unify — always ship the multi-line unique context. Update `buildFix` in `src/output/agent-response/build-finding.ts` to emit the same shape `suggest_fix` does when the rule supports an inline edit. Pairs with Q-SHARED-NEXTSTEP-SCHEMA-DISPATCH-TEST (closed) — same cross-tool contract discipline. (repro: 50p50d-04 §bug-2)
- [ ] **V1-FIX-LANG-AUTOCOMPLETE-ALT-MECHANICAL-DOWNGRADE** `suggest_fix` returns `kind: "guidance"` for every finding tagged `fixClass: "mechanical"` on `document/lang-attribute`, `forms/autocomplete-missing` (×3), and `media/alt-text-missing` on canvas — 5 of 10 mechanical findings degrade to guidance. `document/lang-attribute`'s one-hop fix is literally `<html>` → `<html lang="en">`; `forms/autocomplete-missing` has deterministic token derivation from `type="password"` / `id="email"`. Pairs with Q6-SUGGEST-FIX-MECHANICAL-VS-GUIDANCE-DRIFT (open). Fix: every rule scan tags as `fixClass: "mechanical"` must return `kind: "edit"` from `suggest_fix`; when the edit depends on surrounding context the rule can't resolve (e.g. canvas needs `aria-label="<derived-from-parent-text>"`), emit `kind: "edit"` with the canonical default + a `needsReview: true` signal rather than degrading to guidance. OR re-tag as `verify-in-source` at scan time so `fixClass` and `suggest_fix.kind` stay consistent. Specific rules named: `document/lang-attribute`, `forms/autocomplete-missing`, `media/alt-text-missing`. (repro: 50p50d-04 §bug-1, bootstrap-04 §B1)
- [ ] **V1-SUGGEST-FIX-VERIFYCOMMAND-ON-NONE** `suggest_fix` with `kind: "none"` (when no finding exists at the cited line) still ships a populated `verifyCommand` + `verifyCommandStructured` pair that tells the caller to "confirm the rule no longer fires at this location." A caller cannot distinguish "finding never existed" from "you already fixed it and verified." Fix: when `kind: "none"`, drop the verify fields (present-when-meaningful). Doctrine: ambiguous field shapes are dishonest. (repro: 50p50d-04 §shape-honesty)
- [ ] **V1-SUGGEST-FIX-TAILWIND-HINT-SCOPED** `focus/outline-visible` guidance text appends a Tailwind escape-hatch suggestion ("If this element uses Tailwind's focus-visible:ring-*…") regardless of whether Tailwind was detected in the scan. On vanilla CSS repos (50projects50days) this reads as context-blind advice. Fix: gate the Tailwind hint on `meta.tailwindDetected` (already computed for the `tailwind_detected_css_undercounted` warning). When false, omit the hint; when true, keep. Mechanical scope in `suggest_fix`'s guidance-composition path. (repro: 50p50d-04 §shape-honesty, 50p50d-03 §gaps)
- [ ] **V1-SOURCECONTEXT-LINE-ENDING-NORMALIZE** `suggest_fix.sourceContext` preserves the source file's `\r\n` CRLF line endings (observed on `simple-timer/index.html` on 50p50d). If `apply_fix` generates `newText` normalized to `\n`, a write would silently mix line endings. Fix: detect the file's native line-ending (probe first `\r\n` vs `\n` occurrence) and normalize `newText` to match before writing. Add fixture under `tests/fixtures/real-world/crlf-source-file/` + integration test: apply a fix to a CRLF file, assert file byte-content has only CRLF afterwards. (repro: 50p50d-04 §shape-honesty, simple-timer/index.html)

Minification / build-artifact classification:

- [ ] **V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-REGRESSION-AUDIT-MINIFIED** Q3-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SINGLE-LONG-LINE-SECOND-PROBE closed (second-probe shipped) but the "minified" mis-label reproduces across all four 2026-04-22 repos: bootstrap 54 authored files mis-labeled; jekyll 22 authored markdown + SCSS partials; 50p50d 2 pretty-printed HTML with SRI-hash CDN links; website-templates 1168 entries (hand-authored contact.html, courses.html, index.html, custom-fonts.css). Either the closure shipped narrower than spec (did not add all secondary predicates), the classifier's predicate ordering is wrong (short-circuits on single-long-line before second-probe runs), OR the MCP subprocess is serving a pre-closure bundle. Regression-audit item: read `src/mcp/build-artifacts.ts`, confirm the second-tier predicate is a required conjunct (not a disjunct bypass), and curate fixtures for the three prevalent false-positive shapes: (a) HTML with inline CDN `<link integrity="sha512-...">` (one long line from the SRI hash), (b) HTML with inline Google Maps embed or inline SVG (one long line from the embed payload), (c) SCSS function body with long type signatures. Until it lands, the `scannedBuildArtifacts` warning mistakenly trains agents to skip real authored source. (repro: bootstrap-01 §bug-1, jekyll-01 §bug-1, 50p50d-01 §bug-1, wt-01 §confirmed-bugs §3)
- [ ] **V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-REASON-EXPLAIN** When a file is labeled `{reason: "minified"}` in `scannedBuildArtifacts`, the label carries no explanation of the heuristic signal ("longest line 519 chars > threshold 500", "minified-infix pattern match", "sibling sourcemap detected"). An agent wanting to verify a false positive has to re-open the file and guess the heuristic. Fix: enrich `reason` to `{kind: "minified", signal: "max-line-length-exceeds-threshold", value: 519, threshold: 500}` OR add a `reasonDetail: string` sibling field. Pairs with V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-REGRESSION-AUDIT-MINIFIED — deterministic explanation helps both the classifier fix and consumer dismissal. Q4-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-REASON (closed) added the reason taxonomy; this item adds the per-entry signal. (repro: 50p50d-01 §gaps)
- [ ] **V1-MINIFIED-FILE-SCAN-KIND-SPLIT** Minified files are scanned with the full rule set and findings flow into `plan.violations` alongside authored-source findings. On one website-templates sample: 187 total violations on a template where 41 live in `css/bootstrap.min.css` (vendor). An agent reading "187 violations" has no idea 41 are on files they cannot edit. Fix: add sibling counter `plan.violationsByScanKind: { source: 146, buildArtifact: 41 }` (or similar naming). Findings still emit in `files[]` per surface-don't-suppress; the headline counter splits by kind. Per-rule version is Q6-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-PER-FILE-AGGREGATION (open). This is the cross-rule sibling. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest. (repro: wt-05 §confirmed-bugs §1, §6)
- [ ] **V1-SCANNED-MINIFIED-FILE-WARNING-CODE** No structured warning code for "this scan touched a file ra11y classifies as minified; findings on it may be unreliable." `scanned_build_artifacts_present` signals the scan touched some; it does not signal per-file confidence. Fix: add `scanned_minified_file` as a new structured warning code with `warningsDetails.scanned_minified_file: { files: [paths] }` payload. Pairs with V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-REASON-EXPLAIN — together they let agents triage vendor noise without per-file reading. (repro: wt-05 §gaps)
- [ ] **V1-JS-PARSE-ERROR-REASON-MISLEADING** `partialParseFiles` entries for minified `.js` routed to the TSX parser emit reasons like `"Unclosed JSX element <script>"` / `"<r.length>"` / `"<parseInt>"`. The reason string is parser-internal terminology about JSX — the honest diagnosis is "minified JavaScript parsed as TSX; re-parse as JS or skip." Current reason actively misleads: an agent reads "fix the JSX" when there is no JSX. Fix: when the TSX parser's error fires inside a file without `.tsx`/`.jsx`/`.mdx`/`.astro` extension AND the error site looks like a JS comparison operator (`identifier<other`), rewrite the reason to `"Minified or plain-JS <...> comparison operator parsed as JSX element; file likely needs a non-JSX parser."` Pair with Q-SHARED-TSX-PARSER-FALSE-JSX-CONTEXTS (reopened `[~]`) — this item is the reason-text enrichment so the reason stays honest until the underlying parser-choice fix lands. (repro: wt-01 §confirmed-bugs §2, wt-05 §confirmed-bugs §4, jekyll-03 §B2)
- [ ] **V1-PARSE-ERROR-LIVERELOAD-MIXED-SIGNAL** `livereload.js` routed to tsx parser errors with "Unclosed JSX element `<r.length>`" — yet `wcag22:2.2.1` review candidates still surface correctly from the same file at lines 1161 and 1178 with `setTimeout` context. `parseErrorFileCount` claims the file produced zero findings; reality is that some path in the candidate-finder bypasses the parser-error short-circuit. Either the bucket is mislabeled (should be `partialParseFiles`) or the finder bypass is silent. Fix: audit every review finder whose substrate is source text (not parsed AST) — if the source-text path produces candidates even when the AST parse failed, either (a) move the finding entry from `parseErrorFiles` to `partialParseFiles` when ANY candidate emerged, or (b) annotate the candidate with `fromSourceTextOnly: true` so the agent knows the AST didn't support the find. Per-file-coverage telemetry is load-bearing; `parseErrorFiles` claiming "invisible to rules" while candidates emerge is dishonest. (repro: jekyll-03 §B2)
- [ ] **V1-VENDOR-LIBRARY-BANNER-DETECTION** `bootstrap.min.css`, `animate.css`, `font-awesome.min.css` are identifiable from first-line banner comments (`/*! Bootstrap v3.3.1 */`, `/*! Font Awesome ... */`). No signal surfaces today — the agent sees contrast findings on `bootstrap.min.css` and cannot tell the file is a well-known vendor library. Fix: add `meta.vendorLibraries: [{path, library: "bootstrap", version: "3.3.1"}]` derived from first-line banner-comment matching against a curated list (bootstrap, font-awesome, animate.css, jquery, jquery-ui, fancybox, modernizr, normalize.css, reset.css). When populated, agents can triage "these 43 findings live inside bootstrap 3" instantly. Companion to V1-MINIFIED-FILE-SCAN-KIND-SPLIT. No suppression, no severity downgrade — only a label. Doctrine: labeled buckets are only honest when provable from the code; first-line banner matching is deterministic. (repro: wt-03, wt-05 §gaps)
- [ ] **V1-VENDOR-ANIMATION-LIB-GUARD-HINT** `animate.css` in a website-template (3263-line hand-authored library clone) emits 27 `motion/pause-stop-hide` findings — one per keyframe helper class. All 27 are spec-correct but the remediation is "wrap the whole `@import animate.css` in a `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`" — one wrap, not 27 pragmas. Fix: when a rule fires >N× (threshold: 20) on a single CSS file with the same selector-family pattern (`.animate__*` or similar keyframe-helper namespace) AND that file is tagged as a vendor library (depends on V1-VENDOR-LIBRARY-BANNER-DETECTION), emit `warnings: ["animation_library_without_reduced_motion_guard"]` + a response-level hint "wrap the vendor animation library in `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` instead of adding per-selector pragmas." Additive — findings still emit individually. (repro: wt-03 §gaps, animate.css 27 hits)

Checklist / review-candidate shape:

- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-PRAGMA-IN-CANDIDATE** `checklist` response has no per-candidate `pragma` / `suppressWith` field — the MCP workflow instructions tell agents to "Suppress at source only when genuinely N/A via `<!-- ra11y-disable wcag22:… -->` / `{/* ra11y-disable wcag22:… */}`" but the prose pragma is absent from the checklist response. `scan_project` findings carry `suppressWith` per-finding; `checklist` candidates do not. An agent walking the checklist has no in-band pointer. Fix: every candidate emits `suppressWith: { html, jsx }` or `pragma: "<!-- ra11y-disable wcag22:<id> --> or {/* ra11y-disable wcag22:<id> */}"` — same shape the `scan_project` finding uses. Closes the loop the doctrine promises ("Dismiss by reading. Suppress at source only when genuinely N/A"). (repro: bootstrap-03 §gaps §1)
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-CRITERION-GROUP-DEDUP** Same candidate surfaces three times under `wcag22:1.2.1 / 1.2.3 / 1.2.5` (bootstrap `ratio.mdx:14` — an `<iframe>` mention inside a `<Callout>` prose block). Three identical reason strings; three independent `attest` entries needed. Fix: when one candidate location (file:line + groupKey) satisfies multiple criteria, emit ONCE with `criteriaIds: string[]` array — mirror the `scan` finding shape where `criteria: [...]` is the existing pattern. OR group under a shared `criterionGroup` / cross-criterion reason block. Per-criterion `attest` can still happen, but the candidate record is one. Surface-don't-suppress: no suppression — same three criteria still covered. (repro: bootstrap-03 §bug-3, jekyll §1.2.5 iframe candidates)
- [ ] **V1-UNTARGETED-CRITERIA-DEFAULT-EMIT** Checklist `summary.untargetedCriteriaCount: N` ships by default but the list is gated behind `showUntargeted: true`. Per the AI-first consumer doctrine (agents read in ms, not seconds; surface-don't-suppress), the default should invert: emit `untargetedCriteriaList` with bare criterion IDs by default; keep `showUntargeted: false` as a size-pressure escape. Low-cost change; high-value for agent consumers who need to see A-level blockers vs AAA prompts. Doctrine: surface-don't-suppress, verbose meta is signal. (repro: bootstrap-03 §gap-3, jekyll §summary)
- [ ] **V1-REVIEW-CANDIDATE-REASON-MDX-CALLOUT-ENRICHMENT** MDX `<Callout>` prose that mentions an HTML tag name in code-spans (backticks) is parsed into a real candidate by iframe/media review finders — `bootstrap ratio.mdx:14` quotes `<iframe>` in a `<Callout>` and surfaces three 1.2.x candidates with reason "iframe element -- verify embedded media". No real iframe exists at line 14. Surface-don't-suppress per doctrine, but reason should name the evidence honestly: "iframe token is inside an MDX `<Callout>` prose block (may be inside backticks/code); verify the element actually renders." Apply the same enrichment whenever the candidate's surrounding AST context is a documented prose container (`<Callout>`, `<Note>`, `<Warning>`, `<Example>` when its body is JSX/prose rather than raw HTML). Reason-enrichment only; candidates stay in the primary list. Pairs with Q3-MDX-EXAMPLE-TEMPLATE-LITERAL-SUBSTRATE (closed) — this item is the post-parse reason layer; that item was the substrate-extraction parser layer. (repro: bootstrap-03 §bug-2, ratio.mdx:14)
- [ ] **V1-LIKELY-IRRELEVANT-INCONSISTENT** Same scan emits `likelyIrrelevant: 0` on a real repo (`bootstrap`) yet `likelyIrrelevant: 4` (1.2.1/1.2.3/1.2.4/1.2.5) on an empty scan. The bootstrap scan carries iframes that keep the media criteria out of the irrelevant bucket — arguably correct — but the inconsistency "0 in a real scan, 4 in an empty scan" reveals the bucket's eligibility predicate is not stable across scan shapes. Regression-audit: verify the predicate in `src/review/finders/media-*.ts` — `likelyIrrelevant` is triggered on emptiness (provable from code per doctrine) but the iframe-as-media heuristic leaks one finder that contradicts. Pairs with Q-SHARED-LIKELY-IRRELEVANT-IFRAME-NOT-MEDIA (open). Doctrine: labeled buckets must be provable from evidence. (repro: bootstrap-03 §gap-4)
- [ ] **V1-PRAGMA-LIQUID-COMMENT-SYNTAX** Jekyll source files can use `<!-- ra11y-disable wcag22:… -->` but the HTML comment renders to the generated page. Jekyll authors would prefer `{% comment %}ra11y-disable wcag22:… {% endcomment %}` which disappears at render. Same concern for every SSG: Hugo `{{/* ... */}}`, Eleventy `{% comment %}...{% endcomment %}`, Astro `{/* ... */}`. Fix: extend `src/config/inline-disables.ts` to recognize Liquid comment (`{% comment %}ra11y-disable …{% endcomment %}`), Hugo comment (`{{/* ra11y-disable … */}}`), and Astro JSX comment (`{/* ra11y-disable … */}`) as pragma carriers. Pick the syntax based on file extension / detected template family. Additive; no removal. Doctrine: the deterministic dismissal escape hatch must be usable without polluting rendered output. (repro: jekyll-03 §G2)

`additionalPaths` / scope:

- [ ] **V1-ADDITIONAL-PATHS-PRESENT-BUT-UNPARSEABLE** `scan_project({additionalPaths: ["rake/"]})` on a Ruby-only directory returns `filesAdded: 0` with NO `skipped[]` entry and no per-path reason. `nonexistent-subdir/` correctly reports `skipped: [{path, reason: "not-found"}]`. Caller cannot distinguish "dir absent" from "dir present but all files unparseable-by-extension" without re-checking with their own tools. Pairs with Q3-ADDITIONAL-PATHS-SKIP-REASON (closed, added `not-found` / `unsupported-extension` / `excluded-by-glob`). Fix: add a fourth `reason: "no-parseable-files"` with `extensions: {[ext]: count}` payload echoing the observed extensions. Add invariant test: every passed `additionalPaths` entry resolves to either `filesAdded > 0` OR a `skipped[]` entry — never silently nothing. Doctrine: present-when-meaningful requires the negative answer to be structurally distinguishable. (repro: jekyll-05 §bug-2, §matrix #3-5)
- [ ] **V1-ADDITIONAL-PATHS-SCOPE-RESTRICT** `scan_project({additionalPaths: ["subdir/"]})` on website-templates returned identical counts (4043 scanned, 40132 violations) to the root-scoped scan — `additionalPaths` only ADDS; it has no complement. A caller wanting to scope the project scan to a single subdirectory (without using `scan` which has different shape + no paging) has no affordance. Fix: add `restrictToPaths?: string[]` (or `scope?: string[]`) to `scan_project` that intersects the discovered file set with the passed paths. Alternatively document that `scan` (not `scan_project`) is the scoped variant and update `tools/list` descriptions accordingly. Pick one. Doctrine: one tool call should answer "what next?" — on a 1793-file-with-findings repo the agent has no scoping primitive other than giving up on `scan_project`. (repro: wt-01 §bug-5)

Catalog-scale perf / scoping:

- [ ] **V1-BULK-CATALOG-SCAN-PERF-12S** `scan_project` on website-templates (4043 files / 172 templates / 16 MB) took `durationMs: 12188` vs the 3s budget for 1000 files/100k LOC. The corpus is ~16 MB of near-duplicate vendor libraries (bootstrap.css × 172 copies) — the scan is honest but the real-world performance class is "bulk vendor catalog." No hint or warning surfaces the perf class. Fix: when `durationMs > budget*K` (K=3) AND `scannedBuildArtifacts.length > N` (N=50), emit `warnings: ["bulk_catalog_detected"]` + `analysisCoverage.hints` suggesting `exclude: ['**/bootstrap*.css', '**/animate.css', ...]` OR a per-template scoped scan. Pair with V1-ADDITIONAL-PATHS-SCOPE-RESTRICT. Doctrine: agents need scan-confidence telemetry that includes perf class, not only correctness. (repro: wt-02 §S3)
- [ ] **V1-RESPONSE-SIZE-PRE-ESTIMATOR** `scan_project` runs the full scan THEN truncates to fit the response-token budget. On website-templates, `limit: 100` returns `effectiveLimit: 1` — the agent pays 12 seconds of scan time and gets back one file. Fix: add `estimateOnly?: boolean` (or `dryRun?: boolean`) input that returns `{filesWithFindings, estimatedResponseBytes, suggestedLimit}` without per-file bodies. Lets agents plan pagination before committing to the full scan. Pairs with V1-BULK-CATALOG-SCAN-PERF-12S — both give the agent pre-scan signal about scale. (repro: wt-02 §G6)
- [ ] **V1-CROSS-FILE-ROLLUP-PRIMITIVE** No cross-file rule-frequency rollup exists at response top-level; `meta.perRuleCoverage` has per-rule `findingsEmitted` but not cross-file rank. On a 1793-file-with-findings repo the agent has to page 1793 times (each `scan_project` call at `effectiveLimit: 1` costs ~12 s of server time or up to 300 KB of response tokens) to see the rule distribution. Fix: add `plan.topRules: [{ruleId, count, topFile}]` rank-ordered (top 10). Additive, zero information loss. The pieces already exist in `perRuleCoverage.findingsEmitted` + `concentration.file`; this item exposes them at the headline. (repro: wt-02 §G1)
- [ ] **V1-GROUPKEY-COLLAPSED-RESPONSE-MODE** `groupKey` is emitted on every finding and is cross-file for location-agnostic groupings. No response-shape option compresses `(ruleId, groupKey)` into one entry with the file list. On website-templates 40k findings likely collapse to <500 unique groups. Fix: add `collapseByGroupKey?: boolean` to `scan_project` (default false, preserves current shape). When true: response emits one entry per unique `(ruleId, groupKey)` with `occurrences: [{path, line, column}]` — same paging/truncation semantics. Additive — callers opt in. Doctrine: one tool call should answer "what next?" on scale. Pairs with Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE (open, cross-template pattern ID). (repro: wt-02 §G2)
- [ ] **V1-GROUPKEY-POLICY-DOC** `groupKey` semantics are undocumented. It's cross-file for `navigation/link-descriptive-text` + `navigation/href-javascript-void` but includes file-specific tokens for `media/alt-text-missing` (filename participates). Agents cannot tell without experimentation whether `groupKey` equality implies "same underlying snippet" or "same rule firing." Fix: add `referenceGuide.groupKeyPolicy: { fields: ["ruleId", "reasonKind", "hrefLiteral?", "srcBasename?", …] }` to the response root documenting which fields participate in the hash, per rule family. Alternative: one-sentence docstring in every rule's metadata surfaced via `explain_rule.groupKeyFields`. Doctrine: ambiguous field shapes are dishonest; a load-bearing key whose semantics vary by rule is the ambiguity. (repro: wt-02 §S1)

### Ninth-pass net-new (2026-04-23)

Source: four-way parallel field test (2026-04-23) against Bootstrap (docs-starlight + MDX + Astro + SCSS), Jekyll (Liquid + kramdown + ERB fixtures), 50projects50days (vanilla HTML/CSS/JS pedagogical bundle × 52), and a bulk website-templates catalog (40,132 findings across 4,043 files). All items below are net-new after dedup against Track Q3/Q4/Q5/Q6 and all prior Track V entries above. Regression-audit notes (symptoms reproduce on previously-`[x]`-marked items) are filed in a trailing sub-section — the originating item keeps its ID; the v3 observations are the audit payload.

Parser / language coverage:

- [ ] **V1-PERRULE-COVERAGE-HONESTY-ON-PARSE-ERRORS** `scan_file` on a parse-errored file (e.g. bootstrap `site/src/libs/remark.ts`) reports `meta.analysisCoverage.parseErrorFileCount: 1` while `perRuleCoverage` marks every rule as `filesEvaluated: 1, filesEligible: 1, findingsEmitted: 0, coverageConfidence: "high"` — a consumer reads "file fully evaluated, no violations" when the parser never ran the rules at all. Same shape for `partialParseFiles`. Fix: when a file is in `parseErrorFiles` or `partialParseFiles`, drop its per-rule `coverageConfidence` to `"low"` with `reason: "file-parse-error"` / `"partial-parse"` and do not count it in `filesEvaluated`. Doctrine: zero-output success is ambiguous failure (applied at per-rule granularity). (repro: bootstrap-v3 §bug-1)

Rule correctness / detection:

- [ ] **V1-FP-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-DATA-BS-CONTAINER** `keyboard/handler-missing` false-positives on Bootstrap's container-level `data-bs-toggle="buttons"` (e.g. `<div class="btn-group" data-bs-toggle="buttons">`) and on the auto-init attribute `data-bs-ride="carousel"` — both are group-container / auto-init attributes, not interactive triggers. Worse, `suggest_fix` recommends wrapping the container in `<button type="button">`, which would nest interactive descendants (labels + inputs) inside a button and break Bootstrap 5 layout. Fix: build an explicit disclosure-value allowlist for `data-bs-toggle` (`{modal, tooltip, popover, collapse, offcanvas, dropdown, tab}`) and exempt container values (`buttons`) plus every `data-bs-ride` auto-init attribute. Add fixtures `tests/fixtures/real-world/bootstrap-btn-group/` and `tests/fixtures/real-world/bootstrap-carousel-ride/`. Doctrine: surface-don't-suppress, but false-positive fix suggestions are worse than silent misses because they teach anti-patterns. (repro: bootstrap-v3 §bug-4, `js/tests/visual/button.html:20` and `js/tests/visual/carousel.html:20`)
- [ ] **V1-ROLE-FROM-CLASS-ADMONITION-HEADING-CHILD** `aria/role-from-class-only` fires on Jekyll admonition patterns `<div class="note info"><h5>Topic</h5><p>…</p></div>` — the `<h5>` IS the accessible-name / severity carrier (screen readers announce "heading level 5: Topic…"), but the rule flags the wrapper as role-bereft anyway. 53 of 323 findings (16 %) on the Jekyll corpus are this shape. Same pattern recurs across every SSG admonition style (Hugo, Docusaurus, Astro, MDX). Fix: before flagging, check whether the admonition's first element child is an `h1..h6`; when true, downgrade to review-candidate or suppress (the heading carries structural severity). Doctrine: rule premise contradicts the structural severity signal AT already honors. Distinct from Q4-RULE-NOTE-ROLE-FROM-CLASS (which added the rule) — this is the tightening pass. (repro: jekyll-v3 §bug-7, `/tmp/jekyll/docs/_docs/assets.md:27`)
- [ ] **V1-LINK-DESCRIPTIVE-TEXT-SAME-NAME-SAME-HREF** `navigation/link-descriptive-text` flags repeated `{same visible text, same href}` link pairs as "same accessible name / same href" violations. WCAG 2.4.4's rationale permits this — two links to the same destination are allowed to share a name; AT announces "visited" on re-encounter. Only same-name + DIFFERENT-href is the true violation. Current rule drops ≥8 FPs on Jekyll docs (`docs/_docs/step-by-step/05-includes.md:52,79` twice to `http://localhost:4000`; `docs/_docs/structure.md:104,206` twice to `/docs/front-matter/`). Fix: dedupe the "duplicate-name" check by `(normalizedText, normalizedHref)` tuple — only flag when text matches but href differs. Distinct from V1-LINK-DESCRIPTIVE-TEXT-TITLE-DUPLICATE (title-duplicate axis). (repro: jekyll-v3 §bug-8)
- [ ] **V1-NAVIGATION-HREF-VOID-RULE-SCOPE-DOC** `navigation/href-javascript-void`'s rule ID implies the JavaScript-void pattern only, but the rule also fires on bare `href="#"` with no fragment target. An agent registering a suppression via the rule ID may silence more than expected. This is the documentation half of V1-RULE-NAVIGATION-HREF-VOID-RENAME (which is the rename half): until the rename lands, `explain_rule` should enumerate every shape the rule catches (`javascript:void(0)`, `javascript:;`, bare `#`, empty `href=""`). Fix: `explain_rule[navigation/href-javascript-void].scope` enumerates all matched href shapes. Pairs with but distinct from the rename item. (repro: bootstrap-v3 §bug-9, `js/tests/visual/alert.html:21`)
- [ ] **V1-ICON-FONT-HIDDEN-NONINTERACTIVE-ANCESTOR** `aria/icon-font-hidden` fires zero findings across 50+ FA-using pages — including `<h3>Double click on the image to <i class="fas fa-heart"></i> it</h3>` where the icon sits inside non-interactive visible text and still falls through silently. The rule appears to require an accessibly-named interactive ancestor; when the surrounding context is prose (h1..h6, p, li) the check never triggers. Fix: extend `aria/icon-font-hidden` to fire on `<i class="fa-*">` without `aria-hidden` sitting inside `<h1>..<h6>`, `<p>`, `<li>`, or any non-interactive visible-text context. Doctrine: surface-don't-suppress — the icon communicates meaning that AT cannot read without a hidden or label. (repro: 50p50d-v3 §bug-1, `double-click-heart/index.html:11`)

Fix-suggestion regressions:

- [ ] **V1-SUGGEST-FIX-MECHANICAL-LANE-EMIT-EDIT** Every rule tagged `fixClass: mechanical` in the scan plan (`aria/expanded-on-disclosure`, `semantics/landmark-main`, `navigation/href-javascript-void`, `motion/pause-stop-hide`, `keyboard/handler-missing`, `semantics/heading-hierarchy`, etc.) still returns `kind: "guidance"` from `suggest_fix` — zero `kind: "edit"` observed across the bootstrap sample. If `plan.fixesByClass.mechanical: 266` is load-bearing telemetry for "how many apply-now edits," it is currently inflated: most mechanical-tagged rules don't ship structured edits. Either the mechanical tag is wrong (re-tag at scan time so `fixClass` matches `suggest_fix.kind`) or `suggest_fix` is failing to construct the edit. Extends V1-FIX-LANG-AUTOCOMPLETE-ALT-MECHANICAL-DOWNGRADE to every `fixClass: mechanical` rule, not just the three named there. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest — the lane counter must match what `suggest_fix` can actually produce. (repro: bootstrap-v3 §bug-5)
- [ ] **V1-BUTTON-NAME-ICON-FONT-MECHANICAL-EDIT** `semantics/button-name` on icon-font-only buttons emits `kind: "guidance"` even when the rule's own `referenceGuide.fixDescriptions` prose already names the deterministic aria-label (`Primary fix: aria-label="Close"`, `Primary fix: aria-label="Next"`). The derivation is mechanical — FA glyph → action — and ships for some files (faq-collapse, background-slider) but falls back to generic "add visible text" prose for others (simple-timer `fa-play`, password-generator `fa-clipboard`). Fix: unify the code path that picks the FA-aware fixDescription so it applies for every `<button>` whose sole child is `<i class="(far|fas|fab|fa) fa-<glyph>">`, and emit the structured `{oldText, newText}` edit matching the prose. Extends V1-FIX-LANG-AUTOCOMPLETE-ALT-MECHANICAL-DOWNGRADE. (repro: 50p50d-v3 §bug-2 + §bug-3, `simple-timer/index.html:49`, `password-generator/index.html:15`)
- [ ] **V1-FA-GLYPH-ARIA-LABEL-DERIVATION-UNIFY** FA-glyph → action-label derivation is inconsistent across the scan: the code path that maps `fa-times → Close` / `fa-arrow-left → Previous` fires for some files but not others (simple-timer, password-generator, event-keycodes fall through to generic fallback). Single shared derivation helper should be called by both the fixDescription composer and `suggest_fix` so the same mapping drives both surfaces. Pairs with V1-BUTTON-NAME-ICON-FONT-MECHANICAL-EDIT (the emit-as-edit fix) — this item is the derivation consistency axis. Doctrine: the tool doing it must be right every time or it trains agents to distrust the derivation. (repro: 50p50d-v3 §bug-2)
