- [ ] **V1-SUGGEST-FIX-ALT-DIMENSIONS-ANTIPATTERN** `suggest_fix` for `media/alt-text-missing` literally echoes `alt="700x400"` as the suggested alt text when the image URL ends in `…/700x400` — teaching the WCAG 1.1.1 dimensions-as-alt anti-pattern that ra11y's own `media/alt-text-placeholder` rule would flag. Fix: strip URL basenames matching `/\d+x\d+$/` (and other placeholder shapes `/placehold(?:er)?\.\w+/`, `/\d{2,4}[.-]?\d{2,4}/`) from the suggested alt placeholder before emitting; fall back to generic "describe what this image communicates" prose. Doctrine: the tool must not teach patterns its own rules catch. (repro: wt-v3 §bug-5, `3-col-portfolio/index.html:56`)
- [ ] **V1-APPLY-FIX-LIQUID-FP-DIAGNOSIS** `apply_fix` on a finding whose root cause is a Liquid/template false-positive (e.g. `document/page-titled` firing on `<title>{{ page.title }}</title>`) returns `code: "edit-no-match"` with remediation "Re-run suggest_fix" — but `suggest_fix` on the same finding emits `kind: "guidance"` with no edit, so a naive agent loops. The error doesn't diagnose the upstream "finding is a template-directive FP" root cause. Fix: when `apply_fix` fails to locate `oldText` AND the target line contains a detected template-directive token (`{{…}}`, `{%…%}`, `<%=…%>`), emit a dedicated `code: "target-contains-template-directive"` + remediation "verify the rendered output — the cited finding may be a false positive on a template expression; suppress at source if the expression is trusted." Pairs with V1-LIQUID-TEMPLATE-EXPRESSION-AS-SOLE-CHILD-REASON-ENRICHMENT — this item is the apply-fix-side surface. (repro: jekyll-v3 §bug-9)

Response shape / honesty:

- [ ] **V1-RULES-BY-EXTENSION-LABELING** `meta.analysisCoverage.rulesByExtension` and `perRuleCoverage` carry incompatible "what ran?" signals under look-alike labels: the first lists rules that FIRED at least once on the extension; the second lists rules that were EVALUATED. On `scan_file` of `accessibility.mdx` the first returns 2 rules, the second returns 53 — both plausibly "rules that ran." Fix: rename `rulesByExtension` to `rulesFiredByExtension`, OR merge into `perRuleCoverage` with an explicit `evaluated` / `fired` split. Doctrine: ambiguous field shapes are dishonest (at field-name level). (repro: bootstrap-v3 §bug-12)
- [ ] **V1-SESSION-RULECOUNT-VS-SCAN-RULESLOADED** `sessionConfigure.active.ruleCount` is off-by-one against `scan_project.meta.rulesEvaluated.loaded` (74 vs 75) on the same registry in the same session. Either align both counters, or label the difference (e.g. `ruleCount` excludes internal synthetic rules like `internal/rule-crash`; `rulesEvaluated.loaded` counts them). Doctrine: cross-surface counts that name the same concept must agree or disclose the difference. (repro: bootstrap-v3 §bug-13)
- [ ] **V1-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVES-FOUND-STRUCTURED** `analysisCoverage.templateDirectivesFound` ships as bare string tags (`["erb-or-ejs", "jinja-or-liquid"]`). An agent can't tell whether `erb-or-ejs` means "`<% %>` literals inside an HTML file the parser saw" (rules ran) or "an `.erb` file got fully skipped" (rules invisible). On Jekyll both sources contribute to the same bucket. Fix: emit `templateDirectivesFound: [{ kind, extension, fileCount, source: "literal-in-parseable-file" | "skipped-extension" }]` so agents branch deterministically. Doctrine: ambiguous field shapes are dishonest. (repro: jekyll-v3 §bug-11)
- [ ] **V1-DETECT-NATIVE-WRAPPERS-PROJECTKIND-HINT** `detect_native_wrappers` on non-JSX projects (Ruby, Python, Go, static-site) returns `emptyReason: "no-pascalcase-onclick-components"` — technically accurate but the agent can't distinguish "no components detected in a JSX-using repo (coverage miss)" from "this isn't a JSX repo at all (tool doesn't apply)." Fix: add sibling `projectKind: "jsx" | "static-site" | "ruby" | "python" | "go" | "unknown"` derived from file-extension ratios (`.tsx`/`.jsx` > 0 → jsx; `.rb`/`.py`/`.go` dominant → named; otherwise `static-site` or `unknown`). Short-circuits speculative calls. Additive. Doctrine: one tool call should answer "what next?" — the current response forces a second call to the same tool on a different cwd to discriminate. (repro: jekyll-v3 §bug-12)
- [ ] **V1-SCAN-PROJECT-FINDINGS-BY-FILE-HISTOGRAM** `scan_project` exposes `totalFilesWithFindings: N` but no per-file or per-rule distribution at the response root — agents paginating to understand distribution walk every page blind. Fix: add `plan.findingsByFile: [{path, count}]` (top-N rank-ordered) OR `plan.findingsByRule: {ruleId: count}` (whole-scan histogram). Pairs with V1-CROSS-FILE-ROLLUP-PRIMITIVE (top rules) — this item is the per-file orthogonal. Additive; no information loss. Doctrine: one tool call should answer "what next?" on scale. (repro: jekyll-v3 §bug-10, §actionable-12)
- [ ] **V1-COVERAGE-SCANNED-POINTER-MISSING** `coverage({cwd})` returns `scanned: null` while `scan_project({cwd})` returns `scanned: {mode, root}` on the same cwd. An agent reading `coverage`'s response to verify what got scanned has to cross-reference a separate `scan_project` call. Fix: populate `coverage.scanned` with the same shape `scan_project` uses (`{mode, root, filesScanned, filesByExtension}`). Present-when-meaningful would drop `scanned: null`; when populated it must match `scan_project`'s pointer. Doctrine: cross-surface shape drift is dishonest. (repro: wt-v3 §gaps-3)
- [ ] **V1-COVERAGE-MANUAL-CANDIDATES-TOTAL-CROSS-SURFACE** `checklist.summary.totalCandidates` (pre-paging per-candidate count) has no sibling on `coverage` — an agent asking "how many manual-review items" reads `coverage.manualWithCandidates: N` (criteria count) and `checklist.summary.actionable: M` (same criteria count) and `checklist.totalCandidates: K` (candidate count) — three numbers, one concept, disambiguated only by reading field names carefully. Fix: expose `coverage.manualCandidatesTotal: K` alongside the existing criteria-level counters, with the "candidates vs criteria" split explicit in both `coverage` and `checklist`. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest; labels must make kind explicit. (repro: wt-v3 §actionable-13)
- [ ] **V1-CONFIG-NOTE-COLLAPSE-REDUNDANT-PROSE** `meta.configNote` ships a ~260-char prose string ("No ra11y.config found walking up from … — using built-in defaults …") on every scan when `configSource: null` — duplicating the `no_config_found` structured warning code. Fix: collapse to `configNote: true` when `warnings` contains `no_config_found`, rely on the structured warning for details; keep the prose field only when the detail differs from the code's documented meaning. Pairs with V1-CONFIG-CONTEXT-TRIPLE-READOUT-REGRESSION — same triple-readout concern, simpler axis. Low priority. Doctrine: verbose meta is signal, but redundant prose alongside a structured code is bloat. (repro: wt-v3 §actionable-14)
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-HEADLINE-COMPOSITE-SPLIT** `checklist.summary.headline` renders `"12 actionable · 12 untargeted · 0 likely irrelevant"` on one line — while `meta.plan.actionableManualItems` + `untargetedCriteria` live separately as structured counters. The adjacent rendering invites agents to sum "12+12 = 24 things to review." Fix: drop the one-line composite headline; keep the structured per-kind counters; rename the headline string to explicitly name the split (`"12 actionable items; 12 untargeted criteria; 0 likely irrelevant criteria"`) OR emit as `headline: { actionable: 12, untargeted: 12, likelyIrrelevant: 0 }` object. Minor but canonical composite-headline-lie shape. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest. (repro: bootstrap-v3 §bug-14)

Tool-orchestration / bootstrap / suppress / write-gate:

- [ ] **V1-BOOTSTRAP-SUGGESTED-CONFIG-EXCLUDE-LOUD** `bootstrap` tool emits a `suggestedConfig.exclude` mechanically derived from `scannedBuildArtifacts` entries — on Bootstrap the suggestion was `exclude: ["js/**", "scss/_functions.scss", "site/**"]`, which covers every source tree in the project (54 mis-classified artifact entries × a `.*/**` generalization). An agent pasting this into `ra11y.config.ts` would drop 2273 findings to near-zero. Fix: cap `suggestedConfig.exclude` at a conservative set (≤ 3 entries, require ≥N independent mis-classification signals per entry) OR route build-artifact paths through a commented-out `likely-build-paths` block the agent must opt into. Never emit blanket `js/**` / `site/**` excludes from artifact heuristics. Pairs with V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-REGRESSION-AUDIT-MINIFIED downstream — but even with the classifier fixed, the generator should not amplify single heuristic signals into tree-sized excludes. Doctrine: surface-don't-suppress, applied at the generator step (an agent can't un-exclude a tree that was never parsed). (repro: bootstrap-v3 §bug-2)
- [ ] **V1-SUPPRESS-MARKDOWN-FILE-UNSUPPORTED** `suppress({file: "…/docs.md", ruleId: "aria/role-from-class-only", reason: …})` rejects with `{code: "file-unsupported", error: "suppress handles .tsx/.jsx, .ts/.js, .html/.htm, and .css only."}` — yet the scanner flags findings on `.md` / `.markdown` files (145+ findings across `aria/role-from-class-only`, `semantics/heading-hierarchy`, `semantics/table-*` on Jekyll's corpus), and the scan response echoes a `suppressWith: "// ra11y-disable-next-line aria/role-from-class-only"` shape that the suppress tool itself refuses to apply. The MCP workflow doctrine ("dismiss by reading, suppress at source only when genuinely N/A") is unimplementable for the majority of Jekyll's findings. Fix: extend `suppress` to accept `.md` / `.markdown` and emit the correct pragma shape — `<!-- ra11y-disable-next-line ruleId: reason -->` for embedded-HTML findings, OR a dedicated kramdown IAL / `{::comment}` form. Update the echoed `suppressWith` field to match the comment-kind of the target file extension. Sub-item of the `ra11y-disable` pragma family (pairs with V1-PRAGMA-LIQUID-COMMENT-SYNTAX, which is the reader half; this is the writer half). Doctrine: the deterministic dismissal escape hatch must be usable on every file the scanner flags. (repro: jekyll-v3 §bug-2)
- [ ] **V1-PLAN-SAFE-EDITS-STALE-MCP-SERVER** Commit `fce189c8 refactor(mcp): drop plan.safeEditsAvailable composite counter` removed `plan.safeEditsAvailable` per the composite-headline-lies doctrine — but the live MCP server still emits it on both `scan_project` and `bootstrap` responses. Either (a) the MCP subprocess is serving a pre-drop bundle, (b) the MCP server's build artifact isn't being picked up by `npm link` / the smoke harness, or (c) the drop landed in one code path but not the other. Fix: audit the MCP server build / release pipeline — add a CI smoke test that scans a fixture and asserts `plan.safeEditsAvailable` is absent from the response; confirm the dropped-field refactor reaches every invocation path. Doctrine: doctrine changes must propagate to the shipped artifact, not just the source. (repro: bootstrap-v3 §bug-10)
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-LIMIT-NEGATIVE-VALIDATION** `checklist({limit: -1})` silently clamps to 1 per the tool's documented clamp range [1, 2000]; the caller passed a clearly-invalid value and received paginated-to-one-entry results rather than a validation error. Fix: reject any `limit < 1` with `{code: "invalid-argument", field: "limit", remediation: "limit must be in [1, 2000]"}`. Same treatment for the `maxCandidatesPerCriterion` bound. Mechanical. Doctrine: silent coercion on invalid input masks caller bugs. (repro: bootstrap-v3 §bug-15)

Checklist / review-candidate shape:

- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-IFRAME-FINDER-MDX-BACKTICKS** The iframe-review finder's source-text substrate does not strip MDX inline-code spans (backticks) or fenced code blocks before scanning — on bootstrap `site/src/content/docs/helpers/ratio.mdx:14,19` it flags prose `` "**Pro-Tip!** You don't need `frameborder="0"` on your `<iframe>`s" `` as three 1.2.x candidates with `confidence: "high"`, while the real `<iframe>` inside `<Example code={…}>` on line 22 goes un-flagged. Mirrors Q6-CHECKLIST-IFRAME-FINDER-JS-STRING-LITERALS (JS string literals) for MDX. Fix: the iframe finder's source-text path must strip inline-code spans (`` `…` ``) and fenced code blocks (```` ``` ```` ... ```` ``` ````) in `.md` / `.mdx` before the iframe-token match. Downgrade `confidence` to at most `"medium"` when the matching token sits inside what the source looks like a prose container. Pair fixtures: `tests/fixtures/real-world/mdx-iframe-in-backticks/`. Doctrine: confidence `"high"` on a backtick-escaped prose match is dishonest. (repro: bootstrap-v3 §bug-6)
- [ ] **V1-REVIEW-CANDIDATES-SIBLING-REASON-DEDUP** `reviewCandidates[*].reason` duplicates prose across sibling findings for the same criterion — on 50p50d `sound-board/index.html` six `<audio>` elements emit 6 identical candidates per criterion for `wcag22:1.2.1`, `1.2.3`, `1.2.5` (18 rows with 3 unique reason strings). The `reviewCandidates.prompts[criterionId].text` dedup already exists for top-level prompts but per-finding `reason` strings are not deduped by `(ruleId, criterionId, reason)` tuple. Fix: hoist the shared reason to `prompts[criterionId].genericReason` alongside existing `.text`, keep per-location `snippet`; OR cap sibling candidates of the same shape at `N=3` + emit `additionalSiblingCount: <rest>`. Doctrine-consistent precedent: the prompt-text dedup is the same move one level up. (repro: 50p50d-v3 §bug-9)
- [ ] **V1-HEADING-HIERARCHY-MARKDOWN-FIRES-DESPITE-HINT** `semantics/heading-hierarchy` fires "Document has no `<h1>`, first heading is `<h5>`" on every Jekyll Markdown file (29 findings, all admonition `<h5>`s being the only embedded-HTML headings) — even though `meta.analysisCoverage.hints` explicitly says "heading hierarchy [is] not checked" on Markdown-as-HTML-residue scans. One of the two surfaces is lying. Fix: actually skip the rule on Markdown-residue files (or any file where the parser saw embedded-HTML headings but the file's ATX `#`-headings were invisible), OR reason-enrich each finding with "partial-document-view: Markdown `#`-headings were not parsed; heading outline may be well-formed in the rendered output." Distinct from V1-HEADING-HIERARCHY-MARKDOWN-SELF-CONTRADICTION (which is the bootstrap README manifestation: Markdown `# Bootstrap` at top-level); this item's manifestation is admonition-only headings in a file that has well-structured ATX headings upstream. Doctrine: self-reported coverage that contradicts what actually runs is dishonest. (repro: jekyll-v3 §bug-3)
- [ ] **V1-CONFORMANCE-SCOPE-FILES-MINIFIED-LEAK** `conformance_statement.scope.files[]` lists files that `scan_project` labeled `reason: "minified"` in `scannedBuildArtifacts` — claiming coverage the scan did not produce. On 50p50d the `scope.files` enumeration includes `rotating-nav-animation/index.html` and `sticky-navigation/index.html`, both in `meta.scannedBuildArtifacts` and both evaluated by zero rules. A procurement reviewer reads "156 files in scope" but two of them contributed nothing. Fix: either (a) omit build-artifact-labeled files from `scope.files[]` and surface them in `scope.skippedFiles: [{path, reason}]`, or (b) annotate each entry in `scope.files[]` with a per-file `evaluated: boolean` flag. Pairs with V1-CONFORMANCE-SCOPE-FILES-CAP. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest — a file count that sums "actually evaluated" with "skipped as build artifact" under one "scope.filesCount" is the same bug at the procurement-surface layer. (repro: 50p50d-v3 §bug-11)

Minification / build-artifact classification:

- [ ] **V1-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-ENVELOPE-DRIFT** `meta.scannedBuildArtifacts` is documented (`src/mcp/build-artifacts.ts:593-666`) and typed as `BuildArtifactsGrouped { grouped, ungrouped, ungroupedTruncated? }` with `capMetaArray` applied to the ungrouped tail, but the on-wire response for `scan_project` on the website-templates corpus ships **1168 flat `{path, reason}` entries** (~148 KB) — zero occurrences of `grouped`, `ungrouped`, `basename`, `suggestedGlob`, or `ungroupedTruncated` reach the wire. The grouper is called at `src/mcp/tool-scan-project.ts:1108-1113` but the serialization path between `groupBuildArtifactsByBasename` and the wire drops the envelope. This is the single largest source of response-bloat (responsible for `effectiveLimit: 1` truncations on every `scan_project` call at catalog scale — body bodies are dropped so the flat SBA tail fits) and directly contradicts the Q-SHARED-META-ARRAY-BUDGET-CAP work. `metaMode: "delta"` cannot help either, because the delta baseline contains the same 148 KB payload. Fix: audit the serialization path between `groupBuildArtifactsByBasename` and the response root; post-fix grep the wire payload for `grouped` / `ungrouped` / `ungroupedTruncated` (must be present). Distinct from V1-MINIFIED-FILE-SCAN-KIND-SPLIT (per-kind counter) — this is the pre-existing grouped-envelope that isn't reaching the wire. Doctrine: the shape on the wire must match the type; advertising an envelope that flattens on emission is dishonest. (repro: wt-v3 §bug-1)
- [ ] **V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-CLASSIFIER-BYPASS-HTML-FP** `classifyBuildArtifact` called directly on website-templates' mis-classified authored HTML files (e.g. `above-educational-bootstrap-responsive-template/contact.html` — 213 lines, one line > 500 chars, median 27, ratio 0.5 %) returns `null` — so the classifier itself did NOT classify the file as `"minified"`. Yet the file reaches the wire labeled `reason: "minified"` (459 non-`.min.*` files on this corpus; top filenames `jquery.fancybox.pack.js ×64`, `jquery.js ×49`, `index.html ×40`, `html5shiv.js ×38`, `bootstrap.css ×33`, `contact.html ×18`, `custom-fonts.css ×18`). The label enters via a path that bypasses the per-file classifier. Fix: trace the path from `collectBuildArtifacts` / the `buildArtifactsFields` assembly down to every site that can stamp `reason: "minified"` on an entry, and confirm every stamp site routes through `classifyBuildArtifact`. Pairs with V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-REGRESSION-AUDIT-MINIFIED, but distinct — that item investigates classifier predicate ordering; this item traces the non-classifier bypass. Doctrine: labels must be provable from the code; a label that says `minified` on a 213-line authored HTML file is dishonest. (repro: wt-v3 §bug-2)
- [ ] **V1-OPAQUE-COMPONENT-NAMES-MINIFIED-TOKEN-LEAK** `opaqueCustomComponentNames` on the website-templates scan contains `["AG.y", "B", "H.length", "J", "J.length", "Math.abs"]` — not React components but minified JS symbol fragments the tsx parser captured from minified bundles. An agent reading the list might add `Math.abs` to their `nativeWrappers` config. Fix: before emitting, filter the candidate list by `(a) contains "." (member-access path)` → drop; `(b) matches a well-known global prototype (Math, Object, Array, JSON, Reflect, Symbol, Proxy)` → drop; `(c) single-uppercase-letter with numeric suffix (A1, B2, etc.)` → drop. Same file set that triggers this is the `.js`-parsed-as-TSX shape covered by V1-JS-PARSE-ERROR-REASON-MISLEADING. Doctrine: labeled buckets are only honest when provable from the code; "PascalCase-ish token seen in a parsed file" is not provably a React component. (repro: wt-v3 §gaps-4)

Catalog-scale perf / scoping:

- [ ] **V1-PATTERN-ID-DEAD-INFRASTRUCTURE** Commit `feba3df8` added `patternId` (cross-template fingerprint) to `Violation` with the engine stamping at `src/engine/rule-runner.ts:182` and `src/engine/stamp-project-emission.ts:47` — both gate on `emitted.snippet` being a non-empty string. Grep of `src/rules/` for `snippet:` returns **zero** hits — no in-tree rule populates the `snippet` field that the stamp predicate requires. Verified on a 40,132-finding website-templates scan: `patternId` occurrences in the response = 0. Particularly fatal for this corpus: the three biggest-volume rules (`motion/pause-stop-hide`, `contrast/minimum`, `contrast/non-text` — ~43 % of findings combined) are CSS-declaration rules with no natural snippet source, so `patternId` can never reach them under the current design. Fix: decide — either (a) populate `emitted.snippet` on at least one snippet-emitting rule (HTML/JSX shape rules are the realistic candidates: `navigation/href-javascript-void`, `semantics/button-name`, `aria/expanded-on-disclosure`, `media/alt-text-missing`) so `patternId` is non-empty on at least one finding family; OR (b) remove the type field and delete the stamp sites — current state advertises an affordance that no finding carries. Pairs with Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE (open — proposes the cross-template fingerprint for CSS findings via a different hash path that does not depend on `snippet`). Doctrine: advertising an affordance that never reaches the wire is dishonest. (repro: wt-v3 §bug-3)
- [ ] **V1-CSS-CROSS-TEMPLATE-FINGERPRINT** CSS-declaration rules (`motion/pause-stop-hide` 8956, `contrast/minimum` 5458, `contrast/non-text` 2805) fire tens of thousands of times on website-templates where 117 near-identical copies of `bootstrap.css` ship across 172 template dirs. `patternId` cannot fingerprint these (see V1-PATTERN-ID-DEAD-INFRASTRUCTURE — it gates on `snippet`) and `groupKey` operates per-file, so 117 identical `.img-thumbnail` transitions collapse to 117 distinct finding groups. Fix: add a CSS-rule-specific fingerprint — `cssPatternId = hash(ruleId + selectorFamily + propertyFamily + valueShape)` — that collapses "same selector + same declaration shape" across files. Likely reduces the 17k CSS-rule findings on this corpus to ~40 canonical patterns. Pairs with Q6-CONTRAST-VENDOR-CSS-CROSS-FILE-DEDUPE (open, same-basename) and Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE (open, HTML/JSX element-level). This item is the CSS-declaration sibling that neither covers. Doctrine: the agent has no honest way to say "this is one canonical Bootstrap 3 transition, 117 copies" without such an affordance. (repro: wt-v3 §actionable-11)
- [ ] **V1-PROJECT-DIRECTORY-ROLLUP** 50projects50days is a mono-repo of 50 independent mini-projects under one root; `scan_project` treats it as one repo and merges findings. `meta.perRuleCoverage.findingsEmitted` tells per-rule but not per-project / per-directory. An agent triaging a mono-repo-of-demos or a website-template catalog benefits from `plan.topDirectories: [{path, violationCount, topRule}]` — rank-ordered to expose uneven distribution and let the agent pick the sub-tree that matters first. Additive extension of V1-CROSS-FILE-ROLLUP-PRIMITIVE (which proposes `plan.topRules`). Doctrine: one tool call should answer "what next?" — on a 50-project mono-repo, per-project scoping is the first "what next" question. (repro: 50p50d-v3 §actionable-7)
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-MAX-CANDIDATES-DEFAULT-LOWER** `checklist({limit: 5})` on website-templates returns 84,879 chars — one criterion (`wcag22:1.3.2 Meaningful Sequence`) alone carries enough candidates to blow the MCP host token ceiling even with `perCriterionClipped: true`. The per-criterion clip fires but the default `maxCandidatesPerCriterion` is too high for bulk catalogs. Fix: lower the default `maxCandidatesPerCriterion` to ≤ 20 (from current default) AND emit a `maxCandidatesPerCriterionHint: N` that the caller can raise via the input param if they want a deeper cut. Pairs with V1-CHECKLIST-PERCRITERION-CURSOR — that item adds the cursor; this item tightens the default to match realistic host budgets. Doctrine: response-size-first defaults are load-bearing for usability on catalogs. (repro: wt-v3 §bug-9)

### Regression-audit notes

These items already exist in Track V and reproduce on the 2026-04-23 pass. No new IDs — the observations are telemetry for the existing items.

- **V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-REGRESSION-AUDIT-MINIFIED**: reproduces across every 2026-04-23 repo (bootstrap `_functions.scss`, `accessibility.mdx` with long-prose paragraphs is a newly-documented long-prose shape; jekyll 22 authored markdown + SCSS partials + UTF-16-BOM; 50p50d 2 files; website-templates 1168 entries with the classifier-bypass diagnosis above moved into the new V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-CLASSIFIER-BYPASS-HTML-FP item).
- **V1-PROPOSE-BASELINE-RATIONALE-DEDUP**: reproduces at 10.3 MB on website-templates (33,386 entries), 564 KB on bootstrap (1,818 entries), 72 KB on 50p50d (242 entries). Size class confirms the pagination / rationale-hoist fix is necessary, not nice-to-have — the tool is unusable over MCP on catalog-scale inputs today.
- **V1-PROPOSED-CONFIG-ALIAS-DEPRECATION-WARN**: still live on bootstrap-v3; `bootstrap` tool ships both `suggestedConfig` and `proposedConfig` with identical contents on every call.
- **V1-WARNINGS-DETAILS-CROSS-SURFACE-REGRESSION**: bootstrap-v3 reproduces the payload-asymmetry axis AND surfaces a new sibling axis — the `warnings` code SET differs across surfaces (`scan_project` emits `scanned_build_artifacts_present` on Bootstrap while `coverage` on the same cwd does not). The audit should cover both axes: per-code payload (original) and per-surface code set (new).
- **V1-CONFORMANCE-LIMITATIONS-FIELD-MISSING** (closed `[x]`): jekyll-v3 reproduces `conformance_statement` emitting `status: "pass"` with `reason: "no-evidence"` for 17 of 55 criteria on a scan where 22 runtime-dependent SCs should route to `"undetermined"`. The runtime-evidence-criteria enumeration shipped but the pass-vs-no-evidence split has not propagated to every criterion. Needs re-open or a follow-up item tracing which criteria still emit `pass` with `reason: "no-evidence"` despite the closure.
- **V1-ZERO-SCAN-PASS-RATE-SENTINEL** / **V1-META-COUNTS-BY-SURFACE-REGRESSION**: jekyll-v3 reports `coverage.automatedCriteriaPassRate: 58 %` vs `conformance_statement.summary.pass: 17/55 = 31 %` on the same cwd / standard / level. Cross-surface pass-count drift — pairs with the V1-CONFORMANCE-LIMITATIONS-FIELD-MISSING regression-audit row above (the `no-evidence` side of the gap) and with V1-META-COUNTS-BY-SURFACE-REGRESSION (the drift-detection side).
- **V1-SOURCECONTEXT-LINE-ENDING-NORMALIZE**: 50p50d-v3 reproduces on `simple-timer/index.html` (`file(1)` reports CRLF terminators).
- **V1-LIVE-REGION-RUNTIME-MUTATION-CANDIDATE**: 50p50d-v3 reproduces on `event-keycodes/script.js:4`, `dad-jokes/script.js:20`, `insect-catch-game/script.js:35,77`, `simple-timer/`, `theme-clock/`, `incrementing-counter/` — the pattern is pervasive across the corpus; finder still absent.
- **V1-BOOTSTRAP-BASELINE-NULL-SENTINEL**: 50p50d-v3 reconfirms `baseline: null` in dry-run.
- **V1-CSS-CONTRAST-CASCADE-INHERITED**: 50p50d-v3 reconfirms on `form-input-wave/style.css` (body `color:#fff` + `.container { background: rgba(0,0,0,0.4) }` cascade; 1.53:1 equivalent on nearby button).
- **V1-PARSER-LESS** / **V1-PARSER-SVG-TITLE**: website-templates-v3 reconfirms (672 `.less` + 222 `.svg` files skipped with `file-unsupported`).
- **V1-VENDOR-LIBRARY-BANNER-DETECTION**: website-templates-v3 reconfirms (`animate.min.css`, `bootstrap.min.css` × 117 copies, `fontawesome-webfont.svg` all show deterministic first-line signatures).
- **V1-MINIFIED-FILE-SCAN-KIND-SPLIT**: website-templates-v3 reconfirms — `scan_file` on `bootstrap.min.css` returns 53 violations mixed into `plan.violations` with no per-kind split.
- **V1-ADDITIONAL-PATHS-SCOPE-RESTRICT**: website-templates-v3 reconfirms (identical counts with `additionalPaths: ["3-col-portfolio/"]` as without).
- **V1-RESPONSE-SIZE-PRE-ESTIMATOR**: website-templates-v3 reconfirms AND diagnoses root cause — the `limit=N → effectiveLimit=1` consistent clamp is downstream of V1-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-ENVELOPE-DRIFT (the flat SBA tail eats the budget before file bodies can fit). Fixing the envelope likely unblocks real pagination without needing the pre-estimator, but both items remain valid axes.
- **V1-BULK-CATALOG-SCAN-PERF-12S**: website-templates-v3 reconfirms at `durationMs: 12188` on 4,043 files; no `bulk_catalog_detected` warning surfaces.
- **V1-JS-PARSE-ERROR-REASON-MISLEADING**: website-templates-v3 reconfirms with `parseErrorFileCount: 501` (453 `.js` files routed to tsx parser, errors of the form `Unclosed JSX element <d>` / `<script>` on `prettify.js`, `jquery.js`).
- **V1-SCAN-FILE-CWD-CONTAINMENT**: bootstrap-v3 reconfirms — `scan_file({path: "../escape-test.html", cwd: "/tmp/bootstrap"})` succeeds and scans outside cwd; `apply_fix` / `suggest_fix` / `suppress` correctly reject the same pattern.
- **V1-RULE-NAVIGATION-HREF-VOID-RENAME**: bootstrap-v3 reconfirms (pairs with the new V1-NAVIGATION-HREF-VOID-RULE-SCOPE-DOC until the rename lands).
- **V1-HEADING-HIERARCHY-MARKDOWN-SELF-CONTRADICTION**: bootstrap-v3 reconfirms on `README.md`; jekyll-v3's admonition-only manifestation is moved into the new V1-HEADING-HIERARCHY-MARKDOWN-FIRES-DESPITE-HINT.
- **Q6-CHECKLIST-IFRAME-FINDER-JS-STRING-LITERALS**: bootstrap-v3 surfaces the same finder-substrate gap on MDX (moved into the new V1-CHECKLIST-IFRAME-FINDER-MDX-BACKTICKS item since MDX is a distinct substrate from JS string literals; original item remains for the JS path).
- **Q3-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SINGLE-LONG-LINE-SECOND-PROBE** (closed `[x]`): jekyll-v3 reproduces on `docs/_docs/maintaining/avoiding-burnout.md`, `docs/_tutorials/convert-site-to-jekyll.md`, `docs/_posts/2014-05-06-jekyll-turns-2-0-0.markdown`, `docs/_sass/_style.scss`, `docs/_sass/_docsearch.scss`, `test/source/_encodings/Unicode16LECRLFandBOM.md` (UTF-16 encoding inflates byte ratio), `docs/pages/resources.md`. Closure landed narrower than spec OR the Markdown-prose case isn't covered by the second predicate. See V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-REGRESSION-AUDIT-MINIFIED for the umbrella regression-audit.

---



- **Implement the remaining manual-only criteria as finders.** The coverage matrix (V1-COVERAGE-MATRIX) will enumerate actual gaps — per finder review the finder list confirms most are already covered. If V1-COVERAGE-MATRIX surfaces genuine gaps, each becomes a Track R `R-<criterion>-<topic>` item, NOT a Track V item. Track V verifies; Track R implements detection.
- **Widen real-world fixtures beyond the five above.** SPA routing, internationalization, dark-mode contrast, reduced-motion — all worth fixtures, none are "find all violations" gates. Log as Track F `F-V1.x` items if the v1.0 bar rises.
- **OSSF Scorecard / npm audit workflows.** Nice-to-have for supply-chain posture; zero-dep invariant already covers most of the surface. Skip unless a real signal arrives.

## History — Phases 0–17 complete

Historical record; do not modify. The `/continue` skill does not walk this section.

- **Phase 0** — Autonomous Claude Code infrastructure (hooks, agents, skills, settings).
- **Phase 1** — Guard + generator scripts; `scripts/verify.ts` single entrypoint.
- **Phase 2** — Core types, registries, scanner skeleton, AST helpers, public API surface.
- **Phase 3** — WCAG 2.2 standard module; 86 active criteria + historical 4.1.1.
- **Phase 4** — In-house utilities (logger, assert, fs, path, glob, git, ansi, string-width, wrap, color, contrast, args, index).
- **Phase 5** — Parsers: tsx, html, css, tailwind + theme resolver + discover; unit + fuzz tests.
- **Phase 6** — Rule engine + first 5 rules (alt-text-missing, contrast/minimum, link-descriptive-text, focus/outline-visible, parsing/duplicate-id).
- **Phase 7** — Scanner + terminal/plain/json formatters + theme + snapshot tests.
- **Phase 8** — CLI: scan, list-rules, list-standards, explain, coverage, checklist, vpat, certification, init, doctor.
- **Phase 9** — WCAG 2.1 standard module (reuses rules via equivalentTo).
- **Phase 10** — Section 508 + EN 301 549 standard modules (thin equivalentTo wrappers).
- **Phase 11** — Remaining rules across contrast, focus, keyboard, aria, semantics, forms, pointer, navigation, layout, tooltip, document domains. 54 rules total.
- **Phase 12** — Alternative formatters: sarif, junit, markdown, html, agent.
- **Phase 13** — Reports: coverage, checklist, vpat, certification.
- **Phase 14** — Config, inline disables, baseline mode (create/check/update), monorepo `projects: []` support.
- **Phase 15** — Plugin API (`defineRule`, `defineStandard`, `defineFormatter`, `defineConfig`) + three example plugins + CI smoke job.
- **Phase 16** — Long-form docs: getting-started, CLI, configuration, architecture, KB (architecture + patterns + concepts + gotchas + glossary), certification guides, plugin authoring, MCP docs, ADRs 0001–0005.
- **Phase 17** — CI workflows (ci.yml, release.yml), issue + PR templates, dependabot.
- **Phase 18 partial** — README with real output snapshots + multi-standard-scan integration test + mcp-session integration test coverage audit. Remaining demo/tag/publish items live in Track D above.
- **Phase 19 partial** — In-house JSON-RPC 2.0 MCP server (~200 LoC), session state + AST cache by mtime, 10 live tools, session config for `nativeWrappers`, `.mcp.json` auto-attach, integration tests. Remaining hardening items live in Track M above.
- **Phase 22 partial** — Review finders shipped: use-of-color, error-identification, headings-and-labels, on-input-body (landed as reason-text tiers on on-input-change per CLAUDE.md §1), `tool-audit` meta-tool. Remaining finder + finder tests live in Track R.
